Take Me, Baby, Or Leave Me

by WhothefelsewantedtobeLynNyx

Summary

“Shut up,” Agatha all but snarls. Rio crooks an eyebrow at her, smirking.

“What, you have dibs on her? Can’t blame you, I guess- she is pretty. Seems awfully nice, too. Sweet little thing offered to get up at the crack of dawn tomorrow to welcome me to town, can you believe it?”

She doesn’t step closer, but she does lean forward slightly. “Tell me, Agatha. Is she that sweet out of the uniform too?”

“Shut up,” Agatha hisses, and yanks her in for a searing kiss.

~

Over a decade ago, Agatha Harkness ran to Westview to escape the mess of pain and emotions that was her life. Years after she'd left THAT behind, Social Services showed up on her doorstep with the surviving son of her ex-best friend. Now finally, she's gotten her shit together and-

And now her ex-wife is in town. What's a lesbian to do?
~
Or: a modern AU (for lack of a better word) surrounding Agatha Harkness, her adopted source of stress (I mean, son), her mess of a friend group, and the ex-wife she definitely doesn't still harbor feelings for.

Notes

Really hoping this will be a longfic! I'm working at least a couple chapters ahead in hopes that it'll take the pressure off me a bit. Nevertheless, please be patient with me- I have ADHD and some other mental health issues that can make keeping up with a longfic a difficult process. (PS: I enjoy adding some fun little details in the chapter notes, so I plan to do that as well.)

Chapter 1

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Typically, when a person tells someone her mother is dead, the response is something stupidly sappy or sentimental. She’s always with you, she’ll always be in your heart, she’s watching over you. She’s sending you blessings from above. Cheesy crap straight out of a sympathy card, like they’re fumbling for a script on what to say. 

 

Some people would find the sentiment sweet. Agatha Harkness, on the other hand, thinks it’s bullshit. No, she knows it’s bullshit. Her mother isn’t watching her from above, and she certainly isn’t sending her any blessings. If anything, Evanora Harkness is probably laughing up at her daughter from Hell, delighting in the predicament Agatha has found herself in. 

 

Her mother can’t reach her anymore, so she sent her a traumatized kid with more space in his mind for pretty boys than common sense. She supposes she can appreciate the irony ( she’d certainly snuck her share of girls into the house when she was his age ) but come on. She was absolutely more clever about it than her young charge and his boyfriend are being right about now. 

 

Agatha gnaws on her lip as she contemplates the boys through her window, curled up together across the roof. She almost reaches for the light switch- she can barely see them in the dim glow of her office lamp- but the overhead light is likely too bright, and there’s a decent chance they’ll see her. 

 

Christ, when did she suddenly become the one sneaking around? 

 

She nibbles the tip of her pen, trying to decide a course of action. A responsible parental figure would probably open the window and demand to know what they think they’re doing out there at this time of night. In all honesty, however, Agatha is almost more inclined to ask why they chose the roof in particular.  Considering Teen’s room is in the basement (as opposed to hers and Jen’s, on the second floor, and Lilia’s odd little loft in the attic that she for some reason loves ) the only person he’d have to slip past is Alice. Who, last she checked, probably hasn’t slept in at least thirty-six hours. That’s usually about when Jen starts threatening to drug her, anyways.  

 

All goes to say, much easier to pull off than sneaking out Jen’s office window and helping his boyfriend down from the tree out front- and more privacy to boot. Honestly, what was this, amateur hour? 

 

Now, where was she? Oh, right. Responsible parenting. 

 

She’s…more than a bit rusty.

 

Honestly, she has no idea why her old friend had put her of all people in her will. Wonders about it every damn day, and has for the past three years. She knows the woman was sort of lonely, sure, but did she really have no one else to leave her kid to? For crying out loud, she hadn’t even thought about that family in years when Social Services had shown up on her doorstep with a letter from Wanda and a lifetime of responsibility.

 

It certainly hadn’t been what she’d intended for her life. Hell, in a way, it was exactly what she’d been fleeing when she’d run to Westview. And damn it all, she actually thought she’d run far enough when she’d left again, left to a world she thought she’d pieced herself back together enough to return to.

 

And then they’d brought Billy to her, and she’d nearly fallen apart all over again. 

 

But she hadn’t. Somehow, by the grace of whatever divine powers that are out there, Agatha had actually managed to keep her shit together . At least, no one has taken the kid away from her yet.

 

Maybe she needs to be less hard on herself about this whole parenting thing. Not as though the little critters came with a manual. 

 

Although her rabbit certainly is easier to look after. 

 

Agatha sighs, glancing at the clock. She’d honestly prefer to let the sweethearts be- you can only be sixteen and in love for so long, after all- but it’s nearing midnight on a Tuesday, and she gets the sense that Eddie’s parents already don’t like her. Besides, Alice has an early briefing tomorrow ( some out-of-towner that she’s not happy about- Agatha hadn’t been in the mood to pry her for specifics) that she’s no doubt going to oversleep for, and Agatha isn’t sure the house can withstand them both running late at the same time. That had happened once with Jen and had ended in a very embarrassing trip to the emergency room- not an experience she’s eager to repeat. 

 

She considers knocking on the window- or even opening it and sticking her head out, really giving them a startling little lesson there- but dismisses the idea. As funny as their faces would be, the risk of one of the boys falling off the roof is a bit too high for her liking. Instead, she fishes her phone out from under the pile of papers she’d been grading and sends a quick text. 



11:47

Agatha rolls her eyes as she watches the boys share an overly sweet and decidedly too lengthy kiss goodbye, before scrambling back down the tree and in the window respectively.   She makes a mental note to have a conversation with the kid about date safety tomorrow- both in the protection sense and in the whole get the hell off my roof and just invite your boyfriend over for a movie like normal kids, for fuck’s sake kind of sense. Hmm. Does anyone even keep condoms in the house? Should she pick some up? Should she even be encouraging that?

 

“Damn,” she mutters to herself. “So much for feeling like a good parent for a minute.” She sighs. It was nice while it lasted.

 

Oh well. These , Agatha decides as she stands from her chair and stretches, are questions for someone who had a more stable childhood than she did. She’ll have to ask Jen. Grabbing a pretty purple post-it note, she quickly scribbles down a reminder to herself. Gods, she hopes she doesn’t forget about that before work tomorrow.

 

Her phone chimes, effectively distracting her from her thoughts.

Freeloader: hey Agatha?

 

Freeloader: srry I’m on my way to bed I swear but Eddie just texted me

 

Freeloader: he says he saw someone in the yard

Freeloader: ok. she’s down by the garden. scream if she’s a demon

 

Freeloader: or a cannibal

 

Freeloader: or an axe murderer 

 

Freeloader: ok im going downstairs good night 

 

~

 

Agatha grumbles under her breath in annoyance as she stalks downstairs into the kitchen. She snatches a jacket off of the coatrack- not caring whose it is- and tugs it on as she slips out into the cool October night. Her fingers hover briefly over the switch to the porch light, but she ultimately chooses to leave it off. May as well keep the element of surprise on her side while she still has it. 

 

She crosses the yard, keeping her eyes peeled for any sign of movement. Agatha creaks open the gate and slips into the garden, glancing around. As far as she can tell, she’s alone.  

 

Maybe Teen was wrong. It’s late and it’s dark. Maybe he’d just seen a fox run by, or a tree swaying in the wind, or-

 

An arm wraps around her waist. Agatha yelps into the soft hand that snakes around to cover her mouth. She nearly tries to bite the person grabbing her before a familiar perfume drifts across her face. 

 

She stops in her tracks, inhaling the smell. Misty pine and wet pebbles. Soil damp with rain, fresh after a storm. The smell of cool ground under her toes, of rolling down a hill in a mess of tangled limbs, of stray leaves falling from her hair as she pins her to the grass and her sweet, high laugh as she sneezes it away. 

 

She’s only smelled it on one person in her life, and she knows exactly who it belongs to.

 

She swears, realizing who’s holding her. There’s that laugh, the one that stirs a painful whirl of emotions in her gut, and the hand over her mouth pulls away. One finger traces her lips (and Agatha has to actively resist the instinct to part them for her) before the hand perches on her shoulder instead. Its owner leans in close, lips brushing the curve of her ear. 

 

“Sorry, sweetheart,” her ex-wife purrs. “I couldn’t resist.” 

 

“You are so not sorry, ” Agatha snaps, finally gathering herself enough to wrestle away. Rio smirks at her, dark hair framed against the brilliant white of the moon. It’s a cloudless night and the stars are shining brightly, illuminating every little contour of her face. Her lips crook upwards just the slightest bit more as she gives Agatha a long look up and down, making no effort to hide the way her eyes linger on her legs.

 

Agatha crosses her arms and coughs loudly, suddenly feeling slightly self-conscious. She’s wearing an odd combination of the colorful striped pants she’d worn to work, a black graphic t-shirt gifted to her by Teen, and a fur lined jacket that she now recognizes as belonging to Jennifer. Compared to Rio -dressed in a creamy white button down and black suspenders that she somehow manages to make look incredibly elegant- she feels like a complete disaster. Rio, however, seems to disagree, if the way she chuckles appreciatively is any indicator. 

 

“Peace was never an option, huh?”

 

Agatha glares at her. “Excuse me?”

 

Rio points. “Your shirt.” She tilts her head curiously. “Is that a swan?”

 

Oh. Right. Damn. 

 

“It’s- erm- it’s a goose, I think,” Agatha mutters, trying to ignore the way her cheeks flush. “It’s from some game. I don’t know, my-”

 

She freezes, the words my kid gave it to me sticking in her throat like it’s suddenly been lined with thorns. Such a normal thing to say to her neighbors, or her students, or even her snotty coworkers- but to Rio? Suddenly she can’t get the sentence out of her mouth. The words hang heavy in the air between them, veiled by a fog of unspoken questions.

 

  Do you know? 

 

Are you angry?

 

Can you forgive me?

 

Agatha clears her throat, trying to regain some of her bravado. No, scratch that- to regain any of her composure.

 

“What the hell are you doing here? In my…my…”

 

She trails off, wondering how exactly she meant to end that sentence. My town makes her sound like some cowboy out of a bad Western. My yard, on the other hand, makes her feel like some prissy brunch-having Karen like Mrs. Hart from next door (Alice says she’s not that bad. Agatha always retorts that she likes Alice because she’s a cop and that she’d be much happier if the rest of them had never moved in because Agatha yells at her when she knocks on their door instead of calling 911. Then she leaves before Alice can remind her of the time she had to carry her drunk out of Mrs. Hart’s garden party). She crosses her arms- fully aware of how petulant she looks- and takes a step back, away from her ex. “Just answer the question.”

 

Do you hate me? For any of it?

 

Rio takes a step forward. “I was in the neighborhood and thought I’d drop by.” She produces a flower out of her pocket- a velvety black rose- and presses it into Agatha’s fingers before she can pull away. “For you. My lady. ”

 

Agatha grits her teeth as she steps back again. The thorns of the flower dig into her palm. “Did you take this from my garden?”

 

Do you miss me?

 

Rio steps closer. “Not yours.”

 

Fuck, I miss you. 

 

“So, what,” Agatha grumbles, nearly tripping over a rock as she backs up again. Her back hits the hedge, sticks and leaves tangling in her hair. “You came all this way to steal flowers from my neighbors?”

 

What are you really here for? Who are you really here for?  

 

“I caught a case in the area,” Rio murmurs. She doesn’t advance again now that Agatha’s backed up against a wall, and Agatha almost wants to pull her forward again. “The powers that be wanted one of us on it and I found it interesting.” 

 

She chuckles. “The lead detective is some dead rockstar’s kid. You certainly don’t see that every day. Although I guess you guys do, huh?”

 

A cold, harsh stab of something unbidden that absolutely isn’t jealousy pulses through Agatha. “Alice Wu.”

 

You’re here for her. Not me.

 

Rio snaps her fingers. “That’s the one! With the cute sort of ‘grunge on the weekends’ look going on. So you know her then?” She taps her chin theatrically. Agatha half-wonders if she knows what she’s doing to her before she speaks again, and then she knows. “Maybe you can answer a question for me. Are those pretty red streaks in her hair real, or do they come out if you pull on them?”

 

Shut up. 

 

“Shut up,” Agatha all but snarls. Rio crooks an eyebrow at her, smirking. 

 

“What, you have dibs on her? Can’t blame you, I guess- she is pretty. Seems awfully nice, too. Sweet little thing offered to get up at the crack of dawn tomorrow to welcome me to town, can you believe it?” 

 

She doesn’t step closer, but she does lean forward slightly. “Tell me, Agatha. Is she that sweet out of the uniform too?”

 

“Shut up, ” Agatha hisses, and yanks her in for a searing kiss.

 

It doesn’t matter how many years pass. How many decades pass. Rio still kisses like they’re twenty-one and broke and living out of the minivan they’d stole from her mother. Slow but fierce, muscles tensed with the effort of holding back while she waits for Agatha to make the first choice. Her touch stings and soothes at the same time, like cool water over a fresh burn. 

 

It’s still not enough. Agatha buries her hands in thick dark hair, pulling her as close as she possibly can- as if by holding her too close to breathe, she can meld their souls together the way they were an age ago. A vivid memory flashes across her mind- Agatha on her thirtieth birthday, weeping over the marks life had left on her body. Rio, snaking her way along her skin, pressing sweet kisses to every freckle and scar she could find. “You’re so beautiful, baby.” she’d crooned . “If I could worship you forever, I would. If I could crawl into your skin and live there, I would.” 

 

She’d accomplished it, in a way. A part of her is buried somewhere in Agatha’s chest, embedded too deep in her core to claw out. It draws her in time and time again, drags Agatha into her arms like there’s a magnet attached to her heart. 

 

Rio starts to speak, but Agatha bites harshly at her bottom lip to quiet her. “No. If we’re going to do this, you don’t talk. You don’t get to sweet-talk your way back into my head.”

 

You’re already in my heart, her inner voice whispers .  

 

Rio, to her credit, doesn’t try to talk again. Agatha rewards her by parting her lips, letting Rio slip her tongue past. It’s as much a reward to her as it is to Rio, and she knows it. She lets Rio slide their tongues together, trying not to think about how much she’d missed her.

 

I love you, her heart pleads. 

 

“I hate you,” she hisses instead. Rio nips at her jaw in response. Her hands slide under Agatha’s t-shirt, tracing an achingly familiar pattern along her ribcage. Agatha’s body arches in response, remembering an old sting from years ago. The marks have faded somewhat, but she still sees them every time she looks in the mirror.

 

Rio tugs at the clips of her bra, and it’s enough to stir Agatha from her lipgloss-induced stupor. God, kissing Rio is like a drug. She pushes on Rio’s shoulders. “Not in the- mmm…”

 

Rio pushes back stubbornly, pressing their breasts together. She’s warm and soft, and it takes everything she has not to let her hands wander. Still, what little sense Agatha has left kicks like a mule and she shoves her again insistently. 

 

“I’m serious,” she snaps. “We can’t do it out here, my neighbors are insanely nosy.”

 

Rio pouts, but obediently removes her hands. Resisting the urge to groan at the loss, Agatha wraps an arm around her shoulders and drags her back to the house alongside her. She has the brief thought that she’ll have to buy Jen an apology coffee tomorrow for everything she’s about to hear tonight, but is promptly distracted by Rio kissing at her neck.

 

“ Fuck!”

 

She stumbles slightly when Rio nibbles at the junction of her neck and collarbone, growling at the way she giggles. 

 

“Stop that,” she hisses. “Or we’re going to-”

 

As if to prove the point she doesn’t get to make, a rock promptly pops out of nowhere (at least, that’s what she tells herself). Agatha yelps, yanking Rio in close to her chest as they topple over together.

 

“ Shitfuck ass -!”

 

She hits the porch with a grunt, ex-wife clutched safely in her arms. Agatha groans, wincing at the way her bones crackle in protest. Damn it, she’s definitely going to feel that tomorrow. 

 

Rio props herself up on her elbows, hovering over her. “Aw, aren’t you sweet? Breaking my fall like a true gentlewoman.” She hums, brushing some of Agatha’s hair away from her face. “And I do like the position it’s gotten us into.”

 

Agatha stares up at her, briefly lost in the view. Forget lust and desire for a moment. Rio is one of the most beautiful women she’s ever seen. She reaches out to tuck a lock of hair behind her ear, admiring the way the warm glow of the porch light frames her pretty pale face like a halo.

 

Wait. 

 

“ Fuck! ” Agatha hisses as the realization hits her. She sits bolt upright, ignoring Rio’s questioning look. “Fuck, fuck, fuck.” 

 

 She makes a futile attempt to dust herself off as she hauls herself to her feet and swings the porch door open, trying her best to act casual. Rio peeks in over her shoulder, her own eyes widening slightly. 

 

“Oh. Whoops!” 

 

Agatha grins awkwardly, trying in vain to block her from the sight of the woman sitting at the kitchen table. Rio simply leans around her, waving as though she’s simply making a new friend on the street. 

 

“Hi! I’m Rio. Agatha’s- well, Agatha’s something, anyways.”

 

“Ex-wife,” Agatha snaps through gritted teeth. “My ex-wife. Rio, this is-”

 

“I think you two kind of already established who I am,” Alice interrupts dryly. Agatha winces.

 

“Well that answers that question, I guess.”

 

“Mmhm. Yep.”  Alice stirs her mug of tea, determinedly looking somewhere very interesting between Agatha and Rio. “By the way, you need to tell Teen to just invite Eddie over. He’s freaking Sharon out.”

 

Agatha groans, momentarily annoyed out of her mortification. “Did she seriously call 911 on him again? She’s got to get blocked or something one of these days, right?” 

 

“She called because she saw a suspicious woman lurking in our backyard,” Alice replies pointedly. “So you two should consider yourselves lucky that Lucy happened to be on patrol in the area tonight, because I don’t think anyone else on my squad would have thought to call me before they responded.” 

 

“Well, I can’t wait to thank her in person!” Rio chirps. “On that note…”

 

Before Agatha can stop her, she spins her around and kisses her on each cheek. “ Te veo.” 

 

She pulls away and actually skips out the door, waving on her way out. Agatha glares, wiping the lipgloss off her cheek. Alice gives a halfhearted wave in return, glancing tiredly at Agatha.

 

“ Ex- wife, huh?”

 

Agatha gives a noncommittal flick of the wrist, still grouchily staring out the door. “Something like that. So how much do I have to pay you to make sure the kid never finds out about that?”

 

“You make sure my name never comes up in… whatever that was again, and I won’t breathe a word to him.” 

 

“Are you lying?” 

 

“Probably.” Alice shrugs, getting up from the counter to rinse her mug off in the sink. “But seriously, never again. I mean, don’t get me wrong, your ex-wife is good looking, but I don’t like that look in her eyes. Something about that spells crazy .”

 

“You have no idea,” Agatha mutters under her breath, watching Rio disappear down the road.

 

“No goddamn idea.”



Chapter End Notes

Rio, playing off of Agatha’s jealous streak to get what she wants: 😏

Alice, sitting in the kitchen listening: What the actual fu-

 

Some world building notes! :)

- The characters’ career paths are fairly similar to how they are in canon, minus their various witchy afflictions. Jen is an influencer/beauty guru (I can’t fully decide how much I want her canon backstory to come into play quite yet) Alice stayed a cop and eventually made detective, and Lilia runs a crystal shop. It took some deciding, but I landed on Agatha being a professor of sociology at the university she graduated from (also where she met Jen).

- Agatha is wearing an Untitled Goose Game shirt (it came up under the recommendations on the Pinterest board I made for character outfit ideas and I thought it was hilarious)

- Agatha reconciled with Jen (who was already living with Alice and Lilia) after she left Westview and moved in with her. Surprisingly, they didn’t kick her out after she came home one day with a kid.

 

- Alice lets Teen dye the streaks in her hair as long as Jen helps him place it so she can hide it when she puts it up.

- In this AU, Lilia’s the only one who really practices any kind of witchcraft (and she does not have any powers like in the show). However, the rest of the household DOES lean towards a sort of pagan lifestyle (if there’s a more appropriate term, feel free to tell me!). That’s not going to be super important, but it may show up in little things like Agatha saying ‘gods’ instead of god, or Jen burning sage after Teen brought home a sketchy-ass doll from a yard sale.

- Lilia usually cooks, since she mostly makes her own schedule (so does Jen, but she’s frankly a TERRIBLE cook. Decent baker though.). Alice, contrary to what one might think, is actually a pretty good cook, but doesn’t have time to actually make food for everyone very often. Agatha is just straight up banned from the kitchen after she set the coffee maker on fire.

Chapter 2

Chapter Summary

The reappearance of old lovers puts strain on both old and new friendships alike.

Chapter Notes

Thanks SO MUCH for all the love on chapter 1! So far I have 5 chapters done :) I plan on releasing them slowly as I finish more so I can keep the pressure off myself. More world building notes at the end :) Side note- TW: for some suicidal implications. If this bothers you but you still want to enjoy the story, please let me know and I can tell you where to skip.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“You were married to an FBI agent?” Teen demands, the second she arrives home from work the next day. Agatha glares at Alice, hunched over her laptop at the table with a cup of coffee.

 

“Traitor.”

 

“I didn’t tell him ,” Alice replies, still glaring at her screen. “I told Lilia. Who has no volume control.”

 

Agatha snorts. “You’re kind of mean when you have to wake up early, aren’t you?” 

 

Alice ignores her. Teen (sitting at the other end of the table) waves his hands to get her attention. “Hello?! Can we go back to you being married to an FBI agent?! I have so many questions!” 

 

Agatha rolls her eyes as she pulls her laptop out of her bag and finds a seat between them. “Are they going to be stupid ones?”

 

“Did she have a cover identity? Did you ? Did she investigate terrorist attacks? Or cartels? Or the mafia, or corrupt politicians, or serial killers, or- ”

 

“Okay, yep. Stupid questions.” 

 

At the other end of the table, Alice fishes headphones out of her bag and puts them on. Agatha shoots her a look. Double traitor.

 

“Kid, FBI agents aren’t like in the movies. Hell, I don’t even know what movies you’ve been watching that make them sound like that.”

 

Teen frowns. “Silence of the Lambs? Or Bones, I guess, but that’s not a movie.”

 

“Bones is closer,” Alice adds helpfully. Agatha flicks an eraser at her, making sure to aim for the headphones.

 

“Are those things just for decoration?”

 

“When I want them to be.”

 

She makes a point of pressing the volume button on her laptop with her middle finger. Agatha throws her own middle finger up, opening her email with a sigh. 

 

“Okay, here’s what we’re going to do. I’ll tell you the basics, then you get one question of your choice for each assignment you finish. Got it?”

 

“But-” 

 

“No buts, Teen. That or nothing. We’ve both got homework to do.”

 

“Fine,” the boy grumbles, slumping over in his seat. Agatha considers scolding him for his attitude (something she really ought to do more often, judging by the way his teachers look at her on conference night), but ultimately decides against it. Growing up in this household, it’s a wonder he doesn’t have a worse attitude. Thank god for Alice. Agatha sneaks a glance at her, slouched over her laptop in a sullen slump that sort of mirrors Teen if she looks at her the right way. Well, most of the time, anyways. 

 

Billy looks at her expectantly. She sighs, crossing her arms. “Okay, so Rio specializes in homicides. That usually means she comes in whenever the government decides that someone’s murder counts as a federal crime, like when there’s felony-level extortion involved. Or like you said earlier, organized crime. But that’s not really her area of expertise.”

 

“Like the mafia? Is that why she’s working on this case?” Teen glances curiously at Alice, who doesn’t reply. Judging by the way the light from her screen flashes across her face, however, it’s more because she’s actually working this time and less because she’s ignoring him. Agatha answers for her. 

 

“There’s no mafia in New Jersey, kid. And even if that was why, Alice couldn’t tell you so.”

 

“Because that would put me in danger?”

 

“Because that would put her job in danger. Get your mind out of the television.” 

 

“Oh.” Teen frowns. She honestly can’t tell if he’s disappointed or not. “So did you ever have alternate identities?”

 

“No. Like I said, Rio’s never really focused on organized crime. And undercover’s not her thing. What she does really isn’t all that different than what Alice does, just…sometimes a little higher stakes. In the eyes of the government, anyways.” 

 

She adds the last bit upon glancing up at his ever-curious, overly-excited face. The actual realities of Rio’s job aren’t something he needs to hear about, not at his age. She remembers all too well wishing she’d never heard some of it herself. 



She can let the kid live in his little true-crime fantasy for now. Easier than telling him the truth.

 

“So how did you-”

 

“Ah!” Agatha points her pen at him. “We had a deal. Homework now.”

 

He pouts, but ultimately obeys her. They sit in near-silence for the next few minutes- Agatha checking her emails, Teen clicking through his notes, and Alice doing…whatever she’s doing. 

 

Teen’s computer makes a happy chiming sound and Agatha frowns. 

 

“Aren’t you supposed to be studying?”

 

“I am!” He spins his computer around to show her. “Digital flashcards. I’m almost done with this set.” 

 

Agatha rolls her eyes. “I’m sure you’re learning lots .”

 

The computer chirps again. Teen pumps his fists and spins the computer around to face her again. “Done! See? And now I get a question.”

 

“Fine. Go ahead.” 

 

“Okay.” Teen crosses his arms at her, grinning in a way that reminds her far too much of herself. “Why’d you guys break up?” 

 

Agatha coughs, startled. “ Whoa . Start slower on the questions there, dude.”

 

“But-”

 

She gives him a look . “Being serious this time. My breakup talks are for when you’re older.”

 

If she’s being honest, she hopes he’ll forget. She doesn’t want to talk about that. Not with him.

 

Billy (perhaps sensing the emotions bubbling under the surface) doesn’t push her. “Okay, well…how did you find out she was back in town? Have you gotten to see her yet?”

 

Agatha glances at Alice, raising an eyebrow. So Teen hadn’t overheard the full story? That’s probably good, at least.

 

“Alice told me. And no, I haven’t talked to her yet.” 

 

Not much of a lie. They hadn’t done much talking.

 

Teen puts his chin in his hands. “Are you going to talk to her?”

 

“You’re out of questions. Do more flashcards. Or whatever those are.”

 

Teen sighs, reluctantly going back to his laptop. “Lame.” 

 

“Too bad. You want good gossip, ya ask Jen.” 

 

“Ask me what?”

 

Alice whips her headphones off, dropping her head onto her keyboard as Jen strolls into the room, summoned by the sound of her name like a damned poltergeist. “I don’t know why I try working in this kitchen.” 

 

Jen casually leans over and runs her fingers through Alice’s messy black hair, wrestling it the rest of the way free from the formerly tight regulation bun she’d wrestled it into that morning. “Why, are they distracting you?” 

 

“Very much.”

 

Teen sits up from his spot, eyes lighting up. “Jen!”

 

“Billy!” Jen parrots, rolling her eyes affectionately at him. Though she never lowers her standards of sass for him, she (and the others) at least seems to have a soft spot for the kid. Some nights, Agatha can find it in herself to realize that they’re both lucky for that.

 

“You’ve known Agatha a long time, right?”

 

“Unfortunately,” Jen quips. Agatha throws her a rude gesture. 

 

“So do you know her ex-wife?”

 

Jen’s smile drops. “ Unfortunately, ” she repeats. “Why are we talking about her?” 

 

She eyes Agatha as she lets go of Alice and walks to the cabinet. “Seems like an appropriate and engaging topic of conversation for the study group. Really just encourages the young mind, doesn’t it?” 

 

“I didn’t bring her up!” Agatha protests. “Blame Alice. She started it.” 

 

“ Alice is trying to work,” Alice grumbles. “Something that she’s realizing is going to be impossible as long as Rio is in town.”

 

Crash! 

 

Alice yelps and whips around. “Wha- Jen?! Oh my god, are you alright?!”

 

Jen stares right past her, glaring daggers at Agatha. Droplets of crimson stain the remains of the mug she’d shattered, bright red against bone white.

 

 “ Repeat that again.”

 

Alice jumps to her feet, rushing to her side. “Oh my god, Jen, you’re bleeding . Let me- here-”

 

She swears, cradling Jen’s hand in hers. “Shit, okay. Hang on, I’ll go get the first aid kit and-”

 

“No,” Jen hisses. “Teen, you go get it. The adults need to talk for a minute.”

 

Wisely, Teen chooses not to argue with her. As soon as he’s gone, Jen explodes. 

 

“What the hell do you mean, Rio is back in town?! Agatha, I swear to god-” 

 

“It wasn’t my idea!” Agatha snaps. “She’s here for work. Alice is seeing more of her than I am.”

 

“ Really,” Jen snarls. “And you’re telling me you won’t jump to humping her like a rabbit the first chance you get?”

 

“Don’t insult rabbits like that,” Agatha grumbles, ignoring the fact that she’s technically right. Thankfully Alice (the only other witness) is too busy wrapping Jen’s hand in a clean kitchen towel to chime in. She has to forcibly uncurl the other woman’s fingers from the fist she’d clenched them into.

 

“For god’s sake, Jen, relax .”

 

“ No, ” Jen snaps at her. “Agatha, you need to promise me right now . Swear to me that you won’t go back to her.”

 

“Jen, I-”

 

“ No! ” Jen bursts out. Alice hastily steps between them, grabbing her by the shoulder to keep her from lunging to get in Agatha’s face.  “No protests, no half-baked excuses, no lies. You need to promise me right now , because I am not bailing you out of that pit you dig yourself into when she breaks your heart. Never again. ”

 

“Oh, so it’s all about you, is it?” Agatha snarks, ignoring the twinge of immediate regret that comes with the words. Hurt flashes across Jen’s face before she swallows it down, going back to spitting fire. 

 

“Of course it’s not about my feelings. We both know you have to be wasted before you give half a shit about those. But you can’t let Teen see you like that, Agatha!”

 

Agatha freezes, any retort dying on her lips. The words hit her like a sharp spike of ice straight through the heart. 

 

You can’t let Teen see you like that, Agatha!

 

Oh gods.

 

Agatha!

 

She knows exactly what she means.

 

“Agatha!”

 

~

 

“Agatha!”

 

The wind wails past her face, sleet soaking her sweater. She’s aware she’s shivering, but can’t feel the cold. 

 

“ Agatha! ” 

 

From somewhere behind her, Jen cries her name again. “Agatha, get down from there! ”

 

She rocks on the balls of her feet, gazing down at the river raging below them. Jen’s voice jumps an octave, fear leaching into her tone. “Agatha, please . Just- just come down, okay? You can come stay with me while you guys figure this out, just-”

 

A gust whips past. Before she can stop herself (if she even intended to try) she lurches forward. 

 

Her feet leave the bridge.

 

Jen screams. 

 

~

 

“Agatha!”

 

She starts in place, jolted back to the present. Alice and Jen both stare at her, the former with concern and the latter with contempt. 

 

“I- for fuck’s sake, I promise, okay?”

 

Jen tilts her head at her, a glimmer of pain peeking out from under the anger in her face. “Yeah?”

 

“Yeah. I won’t- I won’t do that to the kid.” She stares at her hands. “I promise, Jen. I know sometimes that doesn’t mean shit, but…I promise.”

 

Jen sighs. “Fine.”

 

Agatha nods wordlessly as Alice cautiously releases Jen. “You two done?” She tugs on Jen’s arm. “Let’s go get you cleaned up, okay?”

 

“Alright.” 

 

Alice starts to lead her away by the arm before Agatha calls after her, stopping her in her tracks.

 

“Jen? I-”

 

Fuck, she hates saying those words.

 

“I’m sorry.”

 

~

 

She’s sitting on Jen’s couch, wrapped in a blanket and watching detachedly as she talks to a gaggle of first responders. On the table, her phone rings again. 

 

She ignores it.

 

~

 

“I’m sorry.”

Chapter 3

Chapter Summary

Nothing says trapped in the middle like...well, like whatever the hell situation Alice Wu-Gulliver has found herself in right about now

Chapter Notes

Bit of a shorter chapter tonight, and more for exposition than anything else. Since chapter four is similar, I'm going to treat you guys and post them simultaneously! It'll get more juicy going on from here, but these were nonetheless fun to write :)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

12:11pm

 

The Actual Adults (And Alice)

 

Alice ❤️‍🔥: Okay I know we said we’d stop talking about this but

 

Jen 💖: UGH ALICE

 

Alice ❤️‍🔥: I’M SORRY BUT I HAVE TO WORK WITH HER AND IT’S WEIRD

 

Jen 💖: can I just have one minute without having to think about the trainwreck lesbians? I’m trying to work 😒

 

Lilia 💛: No you’re not 

 

Jen 💖: Excuse me???

 

Lilia 💛: You’re giving yourself a facial 

 

Lilia 💛: I can hear the spa music 

 

Alice ❤️‍🔥: LMAOOOOO

 

Jen 💖: THAT IS WORK YOU ASSHOLE

 

Jen 💖: ugh fine whatever. So what’s weird now? Like, weirder than usual 

 

Alice ❤️‍🔥: okay we were having lunch and she said the weirdest shit…

 

~

 

Sometimes the courtyard at the precinct really does remind her of her high school lunchroom. Watching her colleagues glance at the two of them out of the corners of their eyes and whisper amongst themselves as they settle amongst their teams, ever-curious but unwilling to approach? 

 

Yeah, that feels pretty juvenile to her. 

 

The stupid, immature cliquiness ( was that a word? )  was already frustrating enough to deal with on her own (she won’t pretend they didn’t stare at her when she’d first joined up, that she hadn’t sat alone for the first few months and listened to the murmurs about her mother behind clasped hands). Having the intimidating, turf-invading oddball as her temporary partner certainly wasn’t helping matters.  I didn’t choose the new girl, Alice sometimes felt like shouting at them all. She chose me. It’s not MY fault she’s weird!

 

Probably didn’t help that Rio had tried to bite one of them her first day. Not that she hadn’t thought about smacking the guy before, but still. Not helping either of us there, Agent.

 

Thankfully today, one of her more bold coworkers (an ever-chipper UC named Lucy) had marched up and joined them for lunch that day. She’d sat down across from them with a wink and a smile, and Alice had breathed a sigh of relief. Lucy is uniquely popular in a way that’s incredibly hard to achieve- nice enough to be well-liked, while still badass enough to respected. The star quarterback, if she’s sticking with the high school metaphor. Once others had seen her join them, they’d followed her example, and now Alice is finally starting to relax a little. 

 

Probably, if she’s being honest with herself, for the first time since she’d met Rio Vidal.

 

“And then,” an older female detective grins between bites of her burrito, “I asked her if the suspect had been seen in the last twenty-four hours. And she tells me ‘only on TikTok’.”

 

Alice snorts. Next to her, Rio actually chuckles. “Don’t tell me you had to go digging through the perp’s entire social media page?”

 

“Took me hours. That girl posted every single thing that ever happened to her.”

 

Lucy laughs. “Did you at least learn anything?”

“Yeah, that I was totally using the wrong foundation brush,” the other woman quips, and the group dissolves into very unprofessional chortling.

 

“So, FBI girl,” another officer chimes in after a moment ( the same person Rio had tried to bite, Alice notes . If nothing else, the guy has guts.). “You have any fun battle scars? We’ve all got ours, after all.”

 

The detective with the mismatched makeup brushes raises an eyebrow. “You’ve got battle scars, huh?”

 

“Hell yeah I do!” He rolls up his sleeve, brandishing a nasty-looking blemish across the skin of his left bicep. “Got this baby grappling with a beast of mythic proportions on Halloween of ‘05. Badass, right?”

 

His partner rolls his eyes. “By which he means he tripped over a werewolf animatronic while chasing a couple of kids egging their principal’s house, panicked, and tried to wrestle it into submission for at least five minutes before he realized what had happened.”

 

“It was dark!” Officer Werewolf Hunter protests. Alice laughs, grabbing another forkful of her pasta (courtesy of Lilia, of course. Honestly, sometimes she genuinely doesn’t know if she’d survive without her ). 

 

“If we’re talking stupid scars, I’ve got a fun one.” She lifts the side of her shirt, exposing a long, jagged mark going up the side of her stomach. “My roommate and I were both running late for work and crashed into each other in the kitchen. She happened to be holding a butter knife and well…”

 

Lucy chokes on her drink. “Wait, that’s how that happened? You told me you got jumped by a massive drunk guy!” 

 

“I lied,” Alice chuckles sheepishly. “That was one hell of a hospital visit. I thought A- er, our other roommate was going to kill us both.”

 

Rio watches them all laugh, an unreadable expression on her face. Alice is about to ask her if she’s alright when she speaks up abruptly. 

 

“I have a scar, too.”

 

All eyes land on her. She sets her drink down, staring into the distance. “A long time ago, I loved someone. And I…I had a job to do, and I failed. And that hurt her.”

 

The rest of the group glances amonst themselves, but Alice stares at Rio. The federal agent makes eye contact with her as she continues, as if their coworkers are have faded away and they’re having a one-on-one conversation. 

 

Do you understand what I’m telling you?

 

“I loved her, and I hurt her. She is my scar.”

 

There’s an awkward silence before Rio stands. “I’m going to grab my things before we have to leave again. See you in the car, detective.”

 

The others watch her leave, only breaking the silence once the doors swing closed behind her. Officer Werewolf-Hunter clears his throat. “Wow. She’s…something, alright.” He chuckles halfheartedly. “I’d hate to be her ex, huh?”

 

Everyone else at the table murmurs quietly amongst themselves. Alice nods, but her mind wandered elsewhere. Out the doors of the precinct, back to her car, back home to a friend who she’s suddenly realizing she knows much less about than she ever thought before.

Chapter End Notes

No notes here, but Alice is STRESSED. Since I don’t have world building though, I’ll leave y’all with some fun facts that I learned while writing this.

- the fresh-rain-on-grass smell that I always attribute to Rio is called petrichor, and it’s formed due a number of factors, including both bacterial and chemical reactions. My beta reader actually found a perfume online that’s supposed to smell like it. I may have to buy it for myself just to see.

- there actually IS a semi-current/active (at least as of 2021) mafia in New Jersey called the DeCavalcante crime family. For obvious reasons, I will not be featuring them in my fic.

Chapter 4

Chapter Summary

Following Alice and Rio's awkward lunch together with their fellow officers (though unbeknownst to her) Agatha runs an errand to the precinct and runs into trouble. Meanwhile, something...odd is going on with Jen.

Chapter Notes

how many references to the Rookie can I make before someone catches me

~



Of course, when Agatha gets to the station later that morning, whatever-his-name-is happens to be away from the desk. She rolls her eyes and flops down onto one of the chairs, not bothering to sit delicately. Thankfully, she’s got time before her first class of the day, or she’d be in an even worse mood. 

 

Jen had better be damn grateful for this.

 

After nearly fifteen minutes, she starts to consider marching back and handing the papers to someone herself. Thankfully, the desk jockey- a middle-aged man with the awkward air of someone twenty years younger- comes back and takes them from her before she can make any poor decisions. 

 

She’s about to leave- somehow without incident- when she catches sight of a reflection in the sky window. Agatha swears under her breath, glancing at the door ( the promises she’d made Jen still ringing through her mind ). There’s no way she’ll make it out before being spotted.  

 

Well, desperate times call for desperate measures.

 

“Uh- wait, ma’am, you can’t-!” 

 

The male cop yelps out a protest as she dives back behind his desk. She fixes him with a death glare as she crouches against the wooden fixture. 

 

“You ever been divorced, pal?”

 

“Uh, yeah, actually. But-” 

 

“Then do a fellow divorceé a solid, huh?”

 

He hesitates, but ultimately leaves her be. She scoots backwards into the space underneath the desk, awkwardly tucking her knees to her chest as she hears two sets of footsteps approaching. They pause on the opposite side of the desk and there’s a sound of papers shuffling. 

 

“Oh, did- someone drop these off, John?” Alice’s voice . Judging by the hesitation in her tone, it’s obvious who’s with her. 

 

“You can say Agatha , you know,” Rio quips. “I won’t bite.”

 

Alice mumbles a halfhearted rebuttal. There’s another shuffling sound, and Rio hums, sounding mildly intrigued.

 

“Kale, hm? As in Jenny Kale? She’s got a stalker?”

 

“Never heard anyone call her Jenny and live to tell the tale,” Alice replies wryly. “But yeah, Jen’s got some creepy-ass followers. Curse of internet fame, I guess.”

 

Agatha hears an odd clicking sound- Rio tapping her foot, she realizes. She’s always been a fidgeter. “Huh. That picture looks…familiar.” 

 

Agatha (and presumably Alice, since she doesn’t respond) both wait for her to elaborate. She doesn’t.  

 

Odd. Agatha wonders if she should have taken a closer look at the report.

 

There’s a rustle as Rio sets the papers back down. “How is Jennifer, anyways? Outside of social media, I haven’t seen her in years.”

 

There’s another lengthy pause before Rio chuckles mirthlessly. “Not supposed to talk to me about her, huh? Guess I shouldn’t be surprised. That girl can hold a grudge like nobody’s business.” She taps her fingers along the top of the desk, the sound echoing above Agatha’s head. “I’m sure she’s had plenty to say about me though, right? Her and Agatha both, I bet. You can tell me! My feelings won’t be hurt.”

 

Alice coughs awkwardly. “Um- we’re going to the victim’s workplace, right? We should…get going, they’re pretty far away.”

 

Agatha can practically hear Rio pouting, annoyed that her attempt at coaxing information out of Alice hadn’t been fruitful. “Fine. We can talk more on the drive.” 

 

“Uh, yeah, okay.” 

 

After a moment- thank everything divine- she hears the sound of the women’s footsteps strolling away. The door swings open and she dares to relax a little. 

 

“Hey, John?” Rio’s voice calls. “Tell Agatha I say hi, will you?”

 

The older officer clears his throat. “Uh, sure.”

 

“Thanks! You’re a doll.”

 

The bell on the door chimes merrily as it closes her behind her. Agatha sighs in relief, stretching out from under the desk. Officer What’s-His-Face glances down at her bemusedly. 

 

“I’m guessing you’re Agatha?”

 

Agatha grabs his arm and uses it to haul herself up, patting him roughly on the back as she walks away. “Excellent deduction, Sherlock. Keep it up and you’ll be a detective in no time.”

Chapter 5

During a surprisingly lazy weekend, Teen finds himself alone and with time to reflect. Needless to say, he's not a big fan.

Chapter Notes

November 11th, 2021

7:01 PM

Billy: Mom?

 

~







It’s 11:00 AM on a Sunday, and the house is…quiet. That in itself is strange. In a household of five (six if you count the bunny, which Billy always does), quiet is something even rarer than privacy- especially on Sundays, when everyone should be home.

 

He doesn’t like quiet much anymore. 

 

As if on cue, his door creaks open slightly. He glances down and grins as Señor Scratchy hops into the room. “Hey, buddy. Did you sense I needed some company?”

 

The rabbit makes a happy whuffling sound as Teen scoops him up, tucking him under his chin the way Agatha always does. “Where is everyone today, huh? Is that confusing you too?”

 

Señor Scratchy blinks and nibbles his fingers. Billy sighs. “And now I’m talking to the rabbit again. Come on, Scratches. Let’s go find the actual humans before I start going nuts.”

 

He gives the bunny a quick kiss on the snout. “No offense.” 

 

Thankfully, when he wanders upstairs to the living room, he finds both Jen and Alice curled up on the couch. Alice has her eyes closed, lying flat on her back with her head in Jen’s lap. The taller woman is gently massaging her neck, fingers glistening in the mid-morning sun. Billy sniffs the air as he approaches. 

 

“Essential oils?”

 

“Mmhm,” Jen hums softly in response, brushing a stray lock of Alice’s hair out of her way. “She’s got a migraine. They should help.”

 

Teen plops down on the floor next to her, tilting his head. “Maybe a little too much. I think-” Carefully shifting to hold the bunny in one hand, he reaches out and pokes Alice’s arm. The woman in question lets out a soft snore, head rolling in Jen’s lap. “Yeah, she’s totally asleep.”

 

Jen does a double take, glancing down at her. “Huh. Well, that’ll help too, I guess.” She snorts. “God knows she needs it. I swear she’s worse than you sometimes.”

 

Teen laughs. “I’d be very indignant if that wasn’t true. Hey, I’ve been meaning to ask you-” He grabs his phone out of his hoodie and pulls up a photo. “Since homecoming’s coming up, could you teach me to do my makeup like this?”

 

Jen takes the phone and glances over it. “Um, yeah, but what are you wearing to go with that, exactly?”

 

Billy hesitates. “Uh…”

 

Jen crooks a disbelieving eyebrow. “Wait, you don’t know yet? But you’re picking your makeup look?”

 

 “Maybe?” He gives her a sheepish look. “Uh, follow-up question- any chance you can take me shopping next weekend?”

 

Jen snorts again. “Sure. Do you know what Eddie’s going to wear yet?”

 

“Uh…”

 

Jen opens her mouth (no doubt gearing up to tell him how foolish he is), but pauses when her phone chimes merrily. She frowns. “Hold that thought.” 

 

She scoops it up off the coffee table and swipes to answer it. “Hello? Who’s this?”

 

 In her lap, Alice stirs sleepily. Teen waves at her and she gives him a small smile.

 

“Morning, Teen. It is still morning, right?” 

 

“Yep. Monday morning, though.”

 

Alice rolls her eyes at him, though she’s smiling. “Ha-ha. You’re developing Agatha’s sense of humor, you know that?” 

 

He gasps dramatically, getting ready to retort. Before he can, however, Jen swears into the phone. “Oh, you’ve got to be shitting me. Okay, thanks. I’m on my way.” 

 

Alice glances up at her, concerned. “Jen? What’s wrong?” 

 

“That was my security company. Someone threw a goddamn brick through one of the store’s front windows,” Jen hisses. “Thank god we’re closed today, or someone could have been seriously hurt. I’ve gotta go deal with that.” 

 

Alice sits up from her lap. “Holy shit. Okay, I’m going with you.” 

 

“It’s fine,” Jen mutters, getting to her feet. “I can handle it.”

 

“Jen, someone tried to break into your store,” Alice argues. “You promised you’d let me help you if- oh no, you are not walking away from me!”

 

She jumps to her feet as Jen stalks out of the room, pointedly ignoring her. “Don’t you dare - Jennifer Kale !”

 

“What are you, my mother? And quit using your cop voice on me,” Jen snarks, now rifling around in her purse for her keys. Alice groans, grabbing an abandoned sweatshirt off the nearby armchair. 

 

“No, just someone who cares about you and wants to look out for you, despite how freaking difficult you insist on being about it sometimes! And this isn’t up for debate. I’m coming with you, whether you like it or not- your only choice is whether I ride in your passenger seat or follow you with sirens on.”

 

“You wouldn’t .” 

 

“Wanna find out?”

 

There’s a long moment of silence before Jen throws her hands up. “Fine. Whatever. Be my guest.” She tosses the keys at Alice, who barely catches them. “I’ll be in the car. You’re driving.”

 

Alice exhales, turning to Teen once the garage door slams behind her. “Good thing too, because she was totally right. I have no idea what my plan was for if she called my bluff.” He snorts as she turns the hoodie over in her hands, running her thumbs over the university logo on the front. “Dang it, this is Agatha’s, isn’t it? Hey, ah- if you see her, tell her where we went, okay?” 

 

She hangs the pullover back up and rifles through the coat rack to find a jacket of her own, tugging it on over her t-shirt. “I swear sometimes the two of them are competing to give me an aneurysm. Just- don’t tell them I said that, okay?”

 

Billy stretches over to her and reaches out his right hand, pinky raised. “Swear.” 

 

Alice grins and taps her own against it. It’s become a little ritual between the two of them, a childish game for two people whose childhoods had been disrupted. 

 

“ I lost my mom too, when I wasn’t much older than you,” she’d told him, the first night he’d moved in. He hadn’t known at the time, but she’d given up her own bedroom in the basement so he could have the space. “ I know it doesn’t help to hear this, but…if you need someone to talk to, I’m here, okay?”

 

“Okay, I’ll see you later. Wish me luck, I guess.”

 

He waves her goodbye as she tugs on her boots and hops out the door after Jen. “Good luck! Oooh, hey- if you’re going to Jen’s store, will you bring me lunch from that pierogi place on your way back? Pretty please?”

 

“Depends on what time we come back!” Alice calls over her shoulder. “Text me in an hour, okay?” 

 

Teen throws her a thumbs up, hoisting himself and Señor Scratchy up onto the couch she’d abandoned once the garage door slams behind her. He scoops up the television remote and (after switching the set on) puts it in front of the rabbit, fishing a treat out of his pocket. 

 

“Okay, bud. Let’s try this again, huh?”

 

He taps the remote with his finger. After a long moment of contemplation, Señor Scratchy hops over and presses the channel button with both his little front paws. Teen pumps his fists in victory.

 

“Ohmigod, yes! Yes, good boy! Come on, do it again…”

 

To his delight, the bunny actually manages the trick twice more. Of course, as soon as Billy pulls out his phone to film a video, Señor Scratchy shuffles around and instead presses his tiny feet to the volume button instead. 

 

“Oh no, oh no no no… shoot, come on! ”

 

Some true crime show blares in the background as Billy hastily snatches the remote from the rabbit and fumbles to turn the volume down.

 

“ ..and today, in honor of the day his body was recovered, we investigate the still-unsolved murder of little Nic-”

 

Billy manages to turn the television off. Señor Scratchy makes an indignant huffing sound as the boy scoops him off the remote, flopping onto the other side of the couch with him cradled against his chest. 

 

“Sorry, mister. You were doing really good until then.”

 

Lying where Alice had been a few minutes beforehand, he can still smell the contrasting sharp sweetnesses of peppermint and lavender that linger in the cushions. The bunny twitches his nose, clearly smelling it too. Teen ruffles his fur. 

 

“I know, bud. That’s totally going to set off a bickering match, isn’t it?”

 

Agatha hates the scent of essential oils. It’s a constant battle between herself and Jen, who practically always smells of the stuff. Billy’s never minded it (her perfume sort of reminds him of the nail salon they go to together. He had told her once that hugging her felt like hugging a spa, which she seemed to find pleasing), but he can practically hear his godmother complaining in the back of your mind. “Ugh, Jen, you’re dragging those stupid chemical fumes of yours all over the damn furniture again! Don’t lie to me, the sofa cushions practically reek of artificial plants.”  

 

He snorts just thinking about it, scratching the rabbit’s ears. “Heh. Hey bud, where is Agatha, anyways?”

 

The rabbit, of course, doesn’t answer him. 

 

Gathering him up in his arms, Teen wanders up the stairs in the direction of Agatha’s room. “Agatha?”

 

He raps on the door. To his surprise, it swings open. He pokes his head in, glancing around the dark room. “Uh, hey, Agatha? Are you…in here?” 

 

The windows across the room are covered by thick purple velvet curtains, drawn tightly closed to block out the mid-morning light. In the dim light that shines in from the hallway, he can see Agatha still curled up in her bed. She’s lying still, too still for it to be possible to tell if she’s asleep or just ignoring him. 

 

“Agatha? Are you alright?”

 

She stirs slightly, but doesn’t respond. Teen considers walking in to try and talk to her, but decides against it. Lying in bed this late, he reasons, she’s probably not feeling well- and therefore, in a very grouchy mood. Instead, he sets Señor Scratchy down and nudges him towards Agatha. “Go on, bud. Go check on your mama, okay?”

 

He shuts the door behind him as the rabbit hops obediently towards the bed, jogging back towards the stairs and into the kitchen with the intent of making Agatha a cup of tea. He sets the kettle on the stove and switches it on, rifling through Lilia’s extensive stash of tea leaves. The rustling of the bags and the whistle of the kettle help to soothe the anxiety bubbling underneath his skin, if only just a little.

 

It’s still so quiet.

 

It had been quiet the day of the crash. He’d stayed home alone that day, let his family leave without him- and the reason had been so stupid, so, so stupid. His parents had wanted to take him and Tommy to tour a couple potential venues for their bar mitzvah, and Billy simply hadn’t felt like going. So, in all his thirteen-year-old wisdom, he’d feigned a stomach bug and begged to stay home. 

 

Some days more than others, he regrets that choice. 

 

His mother had seen through it, of course. She always did. Still, she’d indulged him, kissing him on the forehead and tucking him in on the couch before leaving. He remembers watching her leave from his spot on the sofa, turning and wriggling her fingers at him over her shoulder as his father drove away. Her red hair blowing in the wind from the open window, like the flickering of a dying flame.

 

He’d fallen asleep on that same sofa and woken up there hours later, the orange glow of the setting sun having shifted into his eyes. It had been hours after that that a kind first responder  had finally realized someone was missing from the family and had sent officers to find him, still waiting at the window.

 

Alone.

 

Ever since then, he’s hated quiet.

 

Beep!

 

Teen jumps, startled out of his thoughts by a high pitched, extremely loud beeping sound. It echoes around the kitchen again as he glances for the source. “Okay, universe, this was not what I meant by not liking quiet! ”

 

Then, the smell of something burning hits his nose. He whirls around to see a smoldering pile of tea bags, sitting on the bright red burner opposite the kettle.

 

Oh crap. 

 

“Oh crap, oh crap, oh crap!” He hastily switches the burner off and starts fumbling around for a towel, pausing to throw the kitchen window open. “ Crapcrapcrap!”

 

Finding a dishrag, he quickly runs it under the tap and then throws it over the burning tea leaves, gingerly patting at it to snuff out the tiny flames. Perhaps not the most rational choice he’s ever made, but thankfully effective. He leans back against the counter in relief. 

 

“Okay, okay, okay. Fire’s out. Just gotta get this cleaned up before-”

“Before someone sees?” Agatha’s voice drawls sarcastically from behind him. Teen whips around, guilt no doubt written all over his face. 

 

“Uh- uh, I- I can…explain?”

 

“Oh, can you?” His guardian walks over to the stove and lifts the towel like a magician revealing a trick, wrinkling her nose at the pile of very well toasted tea. “I would love to hear it. What exactly were you trying to do here, huh?”

 

He rubs his neck awkwardly. “I…was trying to make you some tea, and I guess I kinda turned on the wrong burner. Sorry.”

 

Agatha raises an eyebrow. “Uh, you do know that kettle’s electric, right?”

 

Whoops. Clearly, he did not.

 

She rolls her eyes, waving the towel around under the smoke detector to shoo any lingering smog out the window. “Well, text Jen back and tell her that. Apparently she’s got our home security system hooked up to her fancy-ass app too and got an alert that the smoke alarm went off. Now she’s freaking out in the group chat, and I’m not in the mood to deal with that.”

 

Teen hastens to obey. Apparently satisfied with her smoke removal, Agatha tosses the towel into a nearby basket. “So why exactly were you trying to make me tea, anyways?” 

 

Her young ward fires off a text message to Jen and closes his phone, tucking it back into his hoodie pocket. “Uh, well- I came up to tell you that Alice and Jen had left, and you seemed like you weren’t feeling well- which I’m seeing now is wrong, but that was where I landed- and…I dunno, I guess I thought it would help? Although come to think of it, I guess that’s more of a Lilia thing. It was just the only thing I could think of.”

 

Agatha’s expression is still fairly unreadable, but her face softens just the tiniest bit. “Yeah?”

“Yeah.” He glances down awkwardly, starting to wonder if he’d done something wrong. Immediately afterwards, however, she reaches out and ruffles his hair. 

 

“You’re a good kid, you know that? Guess between all the adults you’ve had around you, we’re doing something right.” She grabs the hoodie Alice had carefully hung up earlier and tugs it on. “Come on. I’ll take you to get that weird bubble tea stuff you like.”

 

Billy perks up. “Really?”

 

“No, I’m lying to get your hopes up. Just get in the damn car, would ya?”

 

Well, don’t have to tell him twice. Except she kind of did. Oh well. He scrambles to get his shoes on before she can change her mind, chasing after her as she strolls out to the car in her university hoodie and fuzzy slippers ( a gag gift from Lilia that she’d loved ) . 

 

“So what odd concoction are you going to make me try today in the hopes of making me like that stuff?” 

 

Teen grins, clipping in his seatbelt. “I haven’t decided yet. I’m thinking something with lychee in it, though.”

 

Agatha makes a face as she pulls out of their driveway. “Isn’t that that slimy fruit that kinda looks like eyeballs?”

 

“Yeah, but it tastes amazing. ”

 

She snorts, but he notices there’s less enthusiasm about it than usual. Scanning her face, he realizes for the first time that she really does look unwell- her eyes maybe a little puffy and cheeks maybe a little too red, more so than someone who’d just slept in a little too long. 

 

Maybe he wasn’t too far off with his earlier assumption after all.

 

“Agatha, are you…okay?”

 

She sighs and glances over at him. There’s a strange air of sadness to her face that he hasn’t really seen before- a certain melancholy longing, like she’s seeing something beyond him. Something that should be there, but isn’t. 

 

“Honestly? Not really.” She leans over and pats his knee before turning her attention back to the road. “But you don’t have to worry about me. I’m the grown-up here, not you.”

 

“But-”

 

“No buts.” She gives him a little flash of her trademark grin. “Relax, kiddo. I’m a big girl, I can handle myself. Now tell me more about this lichee stuff you’re going to inflict on me.”

 

“It’s lychee . And I know you’re just saying it wrong to distract me.”

 

“Just indulge your old lady, kid. I’m buying you that crap.”

 

“Fine. Okay, so a lychee is a Chinese fruit, but when bubble tea started to get popular in America, lychee flavored stuff actually also started getting more popular here, in stuff like…”



Chapter End Notes

Me, finishing it: Oh

Me, staring at my draft: Oh no

Me, staring at my hands: Oh no what have I done

Also, I want a bubble tea now. Why must businesses do things like close in the middle of the night.

- This fic is taking place over a series of weeks, probably ending at about two months, I would guess? At this time in the fic, it is currently *checks notes* October 13th, 2024. This may or may not be important. Or is it? Nah, it is. (But could you imagine if it WASN'T and I just totally put that there to mess with you guys? Maybe I did. Guess you'll have to find out!). I stuck with normal present day timing rather than Marvel present day timing, so when Billy's family died three years ago, it would have been 2021.

- Yes, Agatha has velvet curtains on her windows. They have little silver drawstrings because she's a drama queen.

- Señor Scratchy, being the only animal in the house (aside from when the weird little parrot Lilia keeps at her store has to come home sometimes), has free roam of the place. Sometimes instead of texting each other, the humans in the house put little sticky notes on his collar and send him like a little bunny messenger. Note: This only works about a quarter of the time, because he usually just finds the nearest person and waits patiently for a treat- whether or not they're actually the person he's looking for. Once Alice tried to give him a note for Jen to come help her in the kitchen, only for him to hop in a circle around the island and deliver the note right back to her.

- Lilia once asked Teen if he was embarrassed to be seen with a weird witchy old lady in front of his friends. He reminded her that Agatha regularly leaves the house in stompeez slippers and sweatpants that have 'naughty' written across the ass in sequins- any sense of teenage embarrassment he'd had left the building a LONG time ago.
