Chapter 6

Chapter Summary

Agatha just wants an easy day at work. Rio just wants any excuse to see her.

Chapter Notes

Scroll down for TWs, skip for spoilers:

Some mentions of child abduction and murder. I may have to update the warnings on this fic :/

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Regretting one’s life choices is part of teaching, and Agatha resigned herself to it a long time ago. Still, there’s nothing like watching her students stare awkwardly at her in silence to really makes her wonder if she couldn’t do better joining an MLM. 

 

A young man in the second row raises his hand. Agatha points an accusatory finger in his direction. “No, class isn’t canceled if the guest speaker doesn’t show. Good try.” 

 

The boy lowers his hand. Agatha sighs, fishing her phone out of her pocket. 

 

3:17 PM

 

Big Mama Witch: where the hell are you 

 

Mommy Issues ™: New York :/

 

Big Mama Witch: you’d better be kidding. 

 

Mommy Issues ™: wish I was. I’m on my way back from an interview and traffic is awful. I’m actually thinking about turning around and just getting a motel room for the night at the rate we’re going.

 

Big Mama Witch: Alice I’m going to strangle you

 

Mommy Issues ™: okay that’s fair, but maybe save that energy for like…a few more minutes. For when you REALLY want to throttle me.

 

Big Mama Witch: excuse me? Why?

 

Read 3:18 PM

 

Agatha growls under her breath, internally calling Alice a long list of colorful names. Logically, she knows it’s not really her fault- murder is a fickle mistress and decent cops like Alice are but slaves to her whims. But since she can’t exactly curse out the witness (or whatever) that had dragged her get-out-of-teaching free card across state lines, mentally losing her shit on her roommate will have to do. 

 

She’s about to give up and call off the class (she’d made the dumb mistake of not preparing any notes of her own) when the lecture hall doors swing open. Boots click on the steps as a familiar, stupidly smug smile strolls down the aisles towards her. 

 

Agatha gapes. Shit. 

 

3:20 PM

 

Big Mama Witch: ALICE YOU CANNOT BE FUCKING SERIOUS RIGHT NOW. 

 

Mommy Issues ™: she just got there?

 

Big Mama Witch: I swear to whatever divine powers there are I’m going to turn you into one of your own case files.

 

Mommy Issues ™: is it worth saying I didn’t mean to? She kinda just like…overheard.

 

Big Mama Witch: I hate you. 

 

Two dark-nailed fingers push her phone down, forcing her to meet amused brown eyes. “Sorry, querida,” Rio hums. “I know I’m not who you were expecting. But hey, better than nothing, right?”

 

Agatha opens her mouth and closes it several times before giving up. She shrugs helplessly and gestures to the projector screen. “Uh, do you- do you have notes, or…?”

 

“Yep. Stole them from Alice. Check your email!” Rio chirps. Agatha makes a mental note to talk to her roommate about that later. Knowing her ex-wife, she could either mean that Alice lent them to her, or she could mean that she hacked into her email account and took them. “You’re looking for a perspective from a minority woman in law enforcement, right? I can cover that.”

 

Agatha crosses her arms. “Your perspective is very different from Alice’s. Do you even go out in the field anymore, or do you just send detectives who already have commitments out to do your dirty work?”

 

Rio shrugs, smile not faltering. “So have her come on a different day. That way you get both points of view, and you get out of coming up with a lesson twice. Win-win!”

 

Damn it, she still knows her too well.

 

Out of arguments, Agatha forces a smile onto her face. “Fine then. The floor is yours.” She spins, clapping her hands. “Ladies, gentlemen, and whatever else you’ve chosen this week! We have a change of plans. This here is Rio Vidal- FBI agent, homicide investigator, and overall pain in my ass. Please show her all the respect she deserves .”

 

She spins on her heel, enjoying the way her purple overcoat flairs dramatically out behind her ( damn, she really ought to wear this one more often) and chucks the remote to the projector at her ex, who catches it far too easily for her liking. Grouching to herself, Agatha sinks down in her desk chair to watch Rio talk. 

 

The most annoying part of it all? She’s pretty damn good at it. Rio has always been charismatic, and time only seems to have made her more so. She captures the class’s attention with the grace and charm of a siren, and holds it with more ease than any other guest speaker she’s ever had. Hell, even Agatha herself has trouble keeping her students as engaged as they are with her ex-wife. They’re fascinated by her, enthralled by her. 

 

Or hell, maybe that’s just Agatha.

 

She starts to relax, letting her mind wander as she listens to Rio talk. More like watches her talk, if she’s being honest- she’s not hearing a damn thing she’s saying, no matter how hard she tries.  But hey, ogling didn’t break any promises. 

 

Her eyes find velvet-soft lips, curved into a relaxed smile. The part of her that’s not totally lost wonders dimly if she still uses that same weird brand of lipstick- the one that Agatha had always complained tasted like the clay it was made with ( no matter how many times Jen tells her the benefits, she still can’t quite get behind mud as a cosmetic product ).  Those dimples Rio hated as a young woman are more prominent now, accentuated by the smile lines that life had chiseled out lovingly into her statuesque features. Had she finally stopped trying to hide them with makeup? Or had the river of time simply forced her hand?

 

  Agatha had always loved those dimples. 

 

She remembers kissing them years ago, feeling Rio’s cheeks warm under her lips as she tried to wiggle away. Agatha would simply pin her hands above her head and kiss her again, over and over and over. 

 

She’d felt like she could kiss her forever then. Sometimes she wonders why she’d ever stopped.

 

She flits her eyes up Rio’s ridiculously sharp jawline- mmm, yes please - then jumps as her eyes abruptly meet her ex-wife’s. Rio’s face has gone serious and she’s staring at her, something unreadable in her expression. 

 

What the hell? Agatha looks over her face, trying to figure out what had changed. Then, she realizes a girl in the second row is talking, asking a question. 

 

“The FBI assists in certain child abductions, right? I remember a case like that a few years ago, when I was still in grade school- there was a little boy involved, I think. Were you working here then, Agent?”

 

A cold spear goes through Agatha’s heart. Rio purses her lips before answering, but her response is drowned out by the ringing in Agatha’s ears.

 

Suddenly she remembers why.

 

When the class ends, she barely waits for her students to be gone before storming out herself. Rio calls after her, but she ignores her, stalking out to her car in painful silence. Rain pelts her face, cold and harsh like a thousand tiny cuts. Pounding her skin like stones. Like the eyes of a million strangers, staring her down. 

 

Accusing her. 

 

Blaming her.

 

~

 

“And finally, a sad piece of news as the search for a missing boy comes to a tragic end. At 6:34 AM on Saturday, October 13th, the body of five-year old Nicholas Scratch was discovered about four miles into the woods- just across state lines. Nicholas (affectionately known as Nicky) went missing Thursday afternoon and was presumably killed sometime on Friday- just four days before his sixth birthday. A source from the local police precinct confirms that foul play is suspected. Anyone with information on the case is strongly encouraged to reach out to detectives. Our deepest thoughts and prayers are with the child’s parents during this difficult time.”



Chapter End Notes

So...yeah. Sorry to all the people I tried to gaslight into thinking the news blurb wasn't about Nicky, I totally lied 🤷‍♀️ I’m hoping this doesn’t seem like it’s moving too fast (part of me doesn’t like it), but regardless I hope y’all liked it!

A couple notes- no funny ones today, I had some originally but decided I wanted the tone of this chapter to stay kind of serious, so I removed them. I’ll post them in a chapter or two! Sorry.

- This chapter takes place on October 14th- the day after chapter 5 and two days before Nicky’s birthday on the 16th. (He’s assumed to have died on the 12th)

- Agatha and Rio met very young (in their late teens) and ran away from homophobic families. Similar to in canon, they gave their son the last name 'Scratch' because they were starting a new life from scratch and wanted to give him a name all his own.

- Nicky's murder is unsolved and will remain so. Similar to Alice's storyline in canon, I went with that because I wanted to highlight the idea that life and death are both unfair, and sometimes bad things happen to good people. Not everyone gets the justice they deserve. Sometimes death happens for reasons we can't possibly comprehend, and it's not fair or just. It just is, and we have to learn to survive with that.

(wow that got dark :/ my headspace is...interesting today, sorry guys.)

Chapter 7

Chapter Summary

The English Language has a seemingly infinite number of words. Yet though there's many a term for a child who's lost their parents, there's still no word for a parent without a child.

Perhaps because it should never have to exist.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Thursday, October 11th. 2007.

 

“Mama! Look what I found!”

 

Agatha- sprawled out on her violet beach towel- props herself on one elbow to look at her son as he bounds up to her from the water. “What’s up, baby?”

 

Nicky beams as he holds out his sandy hands. “Look, look!”

 

She runs a finger along the wave-battered old shell he holds out. “A seashell, huh? Wow, that’s a pretty one. Did you show Mami yet?”

 

“No, look, Mama!” He tilts it so the opening is facing her. “A hermit crab! Can I keep him?”

 

Agatha gives him a wry smile. “Well sure, but can you take care of him? Where’s he going to live?”

 

Nicky bounces on the balls of his feet in a way that reminds her of Rio, like his joy is simply too much for his tiny body to contain.  “A fishbowl!”

 

“And where are you gonna get a fishbowl, huh?

“Auntie Jenny’s house! I saw one in the fridge when she helped me make cookies. It was all pretty and shiny and blue, and it had some pretty rainbow rocks at the bottom. Hermy will love it!”

 

Agatha makes a mental note to mock Auntie Jenny for that one later. For now, she tilts her head playfully at her son. “And what are you going to feed Hermy, hm?” 

 

Nicky frowns, his brow furrowed with all the intense concentration his five year old mind can muster. “I can catch him some fish from the pond by our house?”

 

He pouts as a thought occurs to him ( once again reminding Agatha so much of her wife ). “Oh wait. He’s a beach crab, and those are lake fish. Maybe I can’t keep him after all.”

 

Agatha laughs, tugging her little boy in for a hug. He whines and squirms in her arms. “Mama! You’ll squish Hermy!” 

 

“Oh, you’re right. Here, let me do this instead.” She lets him go and begins peppering his face with kisses instead, to which he pulls a face. She snorts and ruffles his hair. “Oh, I’m sorry. I forgot that big boys like yourself are too grown up to give their mamas a cuddle. After all, you’re almost six! Practically a man, right? Much too big for snuggles.”

 

Nicky grins his big, sunshiny smile up at her. “No, Mama!” Quickly, he sets the crab down and throws his arms around her neck. She scoops him up and pulls him close, nuzzling her face into his damp hair. 

 

No matter how many times she’s held him, she’ll never tire of it. From the very first time, wriggling and whining against her chest as every pain of bringing him into the world faded away, to the day when he’d toddled his first steps into her waiting arms, to here on the beach as his sandy hands grip the back of her neck. She sometimes thinks she could hold him forever and be happy. 

 

Her Nicky. Sometimes she can’t believe she of all people made such a perfect little human being. She’s never loved anything quite like she’s loved him. 

 

Footsteps crunch in the sand behind her. Her son unwraps his arms from around her to lean back, looking over her shoulder.

 

 “Mami, Mami!”

 

Strong arms curl around Agatha’s waist, gently brushing her bare stomach as Rio playfully nuzzles her hair. “Hi, mijo. What’re my two favorite people up to?”

 

“I found a hermit crab, Mami!” Nicky wiggles free from Agatha’s arms and scoops up the shell again, holding it out to Rio. “Look, look!”

 

Rio takes the creature, admiring it. “Very nice , mi cielito. Did you go look at the tide pools like Mama told you to?”

 

“Yes,” Nicky giggles. “I saw Patricks, Mami!”

 

“Did you? Why don’t you take your pail and go bring one to show us?”

 

Their little boy lights up. “Okay!” 

 

He scoops up his little plastic pail and takes off like a shot, running towards the tide pools. “Watch out for sea urchins, baby!” Agatha calls after him. 

 

“I will!”

 

“And stay where we can see you!”

 

“Okay!”

 

Agatha flops back against her wife, her sheer floral cover-up spreading out across her lap. “I swear sometimes that kid has more energy than a nuclear bomb. He gets it from you, you know.” 

 

Rio kisses her forehead, grinning. “My little clone.”

 

“You got that right. Still don’t entirely get how he came out looking exactly like you.” 

 

“I’m just that amazing.” 

 

Agatha grabs her hand and kisses her knuckles, watching their son splash in the tide pools. “And humble, too. But you helped me make a pretty cute little kid, so I suppose I’ll think about forgiving you.” 

 

Rio snorts, tipping the edge of Agatha’s floppy sunhat over her eyes. “You’ll think about it, huh?”


“Mmhm. You’ll have to earn it.”

 

“Mmm. How on earth will I start to do that, huh?” Her wife starts gently rubbing her back, stiff from the drive to the beach. Agatha purrs happily, stretching out like a cat. Rio draws soothing little circles on her shoulders with those sharp black nails as she sprawls across her lap, soaking up the sun and the sound of the crashing waves. 

 

If she could live in one moment, she decides then, it would be this one.

 

It’s something she’ll long for for the rest of her life.

 

Later on, she’ll wish she’d done something different. Batted Rio away and stayed awake, or followed Nicky down to the tide pools, or sat up to watch him more closely. Maybe even just lifted her hat from her face so that Rio could see that she’d accidentally drifted off to sleep. But Rio doesn’t see, and Agatha doesn’t hear when she tells her she’s going to throw away her empty drink.

 

Neither of them are watching. It’s only a few minutes, but that’s all it takes.

 

She’ll remember Rio’s panicked face until the day she dies. Shaking her awake, crying her name, more afraid than she’s ever seen her in her life. 

 

“ Agatha! AGATHA! Agatha, wake up, I- I can’t- I can’t find- where’s Nicky?!”

 

No. No no no.

 

She clings to Rio until the police arrive, nails digging through the thin fabric of her bathing suit. Then, what little sense she has left kicks in sharply and she shoves her wife in their direction. “You- you’ve gotta go help, baby. I- I need you to bring him home. Please , my love, please .” 

 

Rio gives her a solemn nod and a kiss on the head before running off to join the detectives. Agatha stands back, tears running down her cheeks. She waits there for hours until Jen (called by Rio to pick her up) finally finds her, frozen in the worst moment of her life. Still staring, as though if she looks away from the last place she’d seen her son, she’ll lose him for good. Her friend has to forcibly drag her to the car, clawing and crying and cursing at her as the tide pools Nicky had been splashing in fade away into the dimming sunset.

 

The house is silent when they get home. Her wife is still out searching. 

 

Bring him home, my love. Please.

 

Her sweet, precious little boy. Her own heart. Her world. She cries herself to sleep in his little racecar bed, clutching one of Nicky’s stuffed animals in her arms. 

 

Rio doesn’t come home at all until the second day. When she does, another officer accompanies her- one whose name Agatha doesn’t catch but whose face she’ll never forget. 

 

The messenger.

 

The stranger speaks, but their words are redundant. The moment Agatha sees her wife- red-eyed and wan, leaning against the doorway as she turns the bracelet Nicky had woven her in her fingers- she knows . Rio rushes in to catch her as she collapses to the ground with a wail, clutching at her own chest as though someone had reached in and torn her very soul out through her ribcage. 

 

May as well have. That might’ve hurt less.

 

She lies there sobbing on her kitchen floor- desperately clinging to her wife like a life raft in a sea of grief- until someone finally takes pity on her and tips a sedative down her throat. As the last of her consciousness mercifully ebbs away, a part of her prays to a god she’s sure abandoned her a long time ago. 

 

Please let this be a nightmare. Please let me wake up.

 

But no one, it seems, is listening, and her baby boy is still gone when she opens her eyes on the eve of his emergence into the world.

 

Sweet, kind Nicky, who’d lit up the lives of everyone he’d ever met. Who’d never met a creature who didn’t fascinate him. Who’d been the embodiment of everything wonderful she’d never seen in the world, who’d made his mother finally believe- for the first time in her life- that she really could be something good.

 

Who would now forever be five years old.

 

Instead of a birthday party, they plan a funeral. 



Chapter End Notes

*ducks for cover*

Chapter 8

Chapter Summary

An attempt to cheer Agatha up on her son's birthday goes horribly awry.

Chapter Notes

Breaking my rule of clearing my inbox before posting a new chapter because I'm sleepy. Will come back and reply to all my comments I promise but just know I cackled wickedly at all of them.

TW: (skip if you don’t want spoilers): Some semi-graphic violence and fighting, and some sexist language.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

On October 16th, by some luck, there’s a power outage at the university. Agatha had fully intended to spend the day wallowing in bed in a heap of feelings and wine, with no one to bother her and no ex in sight.

 

Lilia, it seems, has other plans. 

 

“Up you get!” The older woman barks, briskly yanking the curtains open. Agatha hisses in protest, curling into a ball.

“Go away, Lilia .” 

 

Lilia claps her hands, strolling to Agatha’s closet. “No. Come on now, up and at ‘em.”

 

Agatha throws a pillow at her. “Leave me alone. I don’t have work today, I don’t have to get up.”

 

“Oh yes you do.” Lilia tosses a black and white striped blouse in her direction. “Jennifer and I agreed that we’d frankly be awful friends if we simply left you here to rot all day. So the three of us are going to go to that lovely new bistro down the road for lunch, and then you and I are going to help her organize the new inventory she just got in. Capische?”

 

“For the record,” Jen chimes in, sweeping by the open door. “That last part was not my idea. I don’t want you messing around in my store any more than you do.” 

 

Lilia fixes her with a stern glare (the only one Agatha’s ever seen actually manage to cow Jen). The latter raises her hands in surrender. “Okay, okay, sorry. Couldn’t help it.”

 

Lilia rolls her eyes. “Well, bite your tongue and go call in a reservation for us, please.” 

 

Once Jen leaves, Agatha flips onto her side, stubbornly facing the wall. She keeps her gaze fixed forward, even as the bed dips next to her. Lilia rests a hand on her shoulder. “Agatha.”

 

“Mmph.” 

 

Lilia reaches down, gently brushing a stray piece of hair off of her face. Her tone has gone soft, almost motherly, and Agatha suddenly has to fight down a rush of very different emotions. “I know sometimes shutting the world out makes it easier, dear. But you can’t keep doing this forever. Tell me, how long has it been?”

 

“...a long time,” she mutters. “Over a decade.”

 

Over a decade of unspoken grief. Of wounds that had healed awkwardly, leaving ugly scars across her heart. Of coming together like a storm with the woman she’d loved more than anything, clashing together like waves hitting rock and leaving a trail of destruction in their wake. 

 

Lilia strokes her hair again. 

 

“I know it’s hard. But if you ever want to break free of this vicious cycle you two have trapped yourselves in, you need to start taking some steps in the right direction. And sometimes the best way to get that ball rolling is just to get out of bed.”

 

Agatha sighs petulantly, grabbing a pillow and curling into a smaller ball around it. “How much have you and Jen been talking about me, exactly?”

 

Lilia stops petting her hair briefly to swat her upside the head. “What are you, Teen? Gods forbid we care about you.”

 

“ Ow.”

 

Lilia tugs the pillow free of her grip and starts lightly hitting her with it. “Come on, little girl. We’re doing this out of love. Now up .”

 

“Who’re you calling ‘little girl’?” Agatha complains ( more like whines, but she won’t give Lilia the satisfaction of admitting it, even to herself) . From outside the room, she hears Jen snort. “Shut up!”

 

She rolls over, making a grab for Lilia’s weapon of choice- only to lose her balance and promptly fall right out of bed when she leans to the side and out of reach. Lilia leans over her with a smug little grin. 

 

“There we are, that’s a good start. Now go get dressed.”

 

~ 

 

Though she hates to admit it, going out does perk her up a little. She’s not the biggest fan of Greek food, but the place is nice enough- and watching Lilia struggle with the QR code for the menu is entertaining enough to make the trip worthwhile. Even being put to work in Jen’s store afterwards is sort of nice, in its own way. Though she’s still not trusted to help organize the displays, she’s given the task of breaking down the crates the supplies came in- something that takes both more force and more effort than expected, and therefore is significantly more satisfying. 

 

Particularly when a certain someone gives her a crowbar. 

 

“Since you refuse to go to therapy, ” Jen had drawled, “ try this instead.”  Annoying, but not wrong. Pretty much summed up Jen as a friend. 

 

By mid-afternoon, the dark cloud hanging over her has dissipated into a cool mist. Between the delightful destruction she’s been wreaking and goofing around with Lilia and Jen (who must be in a good mood too, because she actually snorts at the stupid joke Agatha makes about the box of jade eggs), she’s in a clearer headspace than she has been all week. 

 

So of course, it doesn’t last.

 

She’s holding the stepladder for Jen as she hangs a ridiculously large decorative resin piece on the wall behind her desk ( “Suddenly not so helpless when Alice isn’t around to lift things for ya, eh?”, and quickly ducks the ball of wrapping paper tossed at her head ). Both their backs are turned to the large store windows, focused purely on the wall ahead of them. 

 

Neither of them sees him coming. 

 

The bell on the door lets out a merry chime. Agatha glances at Lilia (fussing over some crystals in the backroom), then up at Jen. “I thought you locked the-”

 

Wham!

 

Something hard and heavy slams into her, knocking her off her feet. Jen screams bloody murder as she hits the ground with a thud, wincing as the breath wooshes out of her chest.

 

 “ Ow- what the fuck?!”

 

She blinks, trying to clear the stars from her eyes. “What’s going- holy shit! ”

 

Above her, Jen is struggling with a strange man- clearly the one who’d plowed her over in his efforts to get to her. One large, ugly hand has her by the hair while the other grips the collar of her dress hard enough to tear. She claws at his wrists, tears welling over in her eyes. 

 

“No- no, please- !” 

 

“ You wanna mess with my career again, little girl?! ” Spittle flies from his snarling lips, droplets splattering Jen’s face. “AFTER ALL THESE GODDAMN YEARS?!”

 

“ I didn’t!” Jen gasps. “ I swear, I didn’t- after I left, I never- I DIDN’T, PLEASE-!” 

 

The man growls, the corners of his mustache trembling. “LIAR!”

 

He cuts off whatever protest Jen is about to cry by closing his fists around her throat. Her strangled whimper is enough to finally snap Agatha out of her shocked stupor and she swears. 

 

“ Oh, you piece of shit- Lilia, call 911! NOW!” 

 

She staggers to her feet as the man drags Jen off the ladder, still furiously choking her as he spits venom in her direction. “ You reported me, you little bitch. Admit it! ADMIT IT! YOU TRIED TO GET ME FIRED, YOU-”

 

“Hey FUCKHEAD !”  

 

The man whips around to look at Agatha, who promptly swings her crowbar around to meet him. The cold metal hits his cheek with a satisfying crunch! and he releases Jen with a wail of pain. The beauty guru gasps for breath, desperately scrambling backwards away from him. 

 

“How the hell are you still standing?! ” Agatha barks, stumbling a step back. The man lets out an animalistic howl and lunges for her, tackling her off her feet again and into the remains of one of the crates she’s been dismantling. Damn it, her back is going to be killing her tomorrow. 

 

That’s the least of her worries, however. Before she can recover, he slams his head right into hers. Pain blossoms across her face and she feels hot blood spurt from her nose. The absurd thought that she’s going to ruin her shirt flashes through her head before he grabs her by the collar and starts to smash her head into the splintered wood around them.

 

“Augh!”

 

Once, twice, three times. She’s going to pass out- pass out or die, she’s not sure which. She has the brief hope that Lilia has thought to lock herself in the office so that at least one of them makes home to Teen in one piece when- 

 

There’s a sharp crackling sound. Her attacker goes rigid, then topples off of her as a blur of motion slams into him. Multiple women’s voices start to echo around her, shouting over each other in a way that makes them impossible to understand.

 

Or maybe that’s just her ears ringing.

 

Agatha groans, going limp in relief. Above her, an achingly beautiful (though still slightly blurry) face swims into view. Hands cradle her face, red-lined lips forming words she can’t quite understand. She doesn’t need to hear, however. She’s seen her own name on Rio’s tongue more times than she can count.

 

She grins in spite of herself, blearily nuzzling into her ex-wife’s touch. “Heyyyy, sweethe’ar,” she slurs, feeling blood dribble down her chin. “Great timin’.”

 

Rio strokes her hair, still gazing down at her with that intense worry she gets whenever Agatha does something truly stupid. Though she hears a person speaking in the background, it takes her a moment to realize it’s no longer Rio talking- though the voice is one she knows.  

 

Alice. Alice is there too. She turns her head weakly to see her crouched next to Jen, who- thank god- is conscious and sitting up in Alice’s arms, sobbing hysterically into her shoulder. The shorter woman rubs her back, murmuring soothingly to her.

 

“Hey, hey, hey. You’re alright. Look, Rio got him. She got him. He’s not gonna hurt you again, okay?” 

 

Agatha glances around. Sure enough, the man lies facedown on the ground in handcuffs, still twitching lightly from the effects of the taser Rio had used on him. Despite everything, he’s still hissing and spitting under his breath. 

 

“Worthless bitch. You’re nothing, Kale, you hear me? Nothing. All you were ever good for was a goddamn pretty face. If you didn’t have a decent ass, I never would have let you into my pro - ”

 

Thwack! 

 

The man squawks, then slumps over, unconscious. All four onlookers gawk at him, stunned, then up at the woman standing over him.

 

Lilia twirls Agatha’s dropped crowbar in her fingers, an incredibly satisfied smirk on her face. “Hope you don’t mind, but he was beginning to get on my nerves. Now, I believe you ladies have an arrest to make.”



Chapter End Notes

I fucking LOVE Lilia. Please don't emulate her at home tho that probably should have gotten her arrested (again)

WORLD NOTES! WE BACK BABY!

- Jen and Agatha literally cannot go five minutes without bickering, to the point where it weirds them both out like crazy if they’re being civil to each other. The very first time Nicky’s birthday rolled around while Agatha was living with the coven, Jennifer actually did try her best to be genuinely nice to her. Agatha promptly declared that she was creeping her out and demanded that Jen act normal, which she’s done for the most part ever since.

- yes, ya crazy sleuths, Jen's stalker was my take on the guy from Boston. Please hold for more details until the next chapter comes out!

- Jen and Lilia regularly visit and help out in each other's stores (strong female business owners have to stick together, after all). Lilia gets Jen deals on crystal suppliers and likes to come in to help her organize. Since Lilia's shop (a relatively small business that's about 70% selling oddities and various witchy supplies, and about 30% psychic or tarot readings) doesn't employ anyone but her, Jen sometimes comes in to watch the storefront when Lilia has multiple readings to do in a day.

- For the most part, the rest of the family is (lovingly) banned from helping either of them. Teen does his best, but often gets scolded for "throwing off the balance of the display" (he has absolutely no idea what he did wrong, but apparently it was something). Alice gets very nervous around anything breakable (for good reason, she once managed to knock an entire shelf over on her own head and nearly shattered a very expensive crystal ball) and therefore mostly stands there until given VERY clear directions on what to do and how to do it. Agatha is just...not allowed.

- Lilia keeps a bright green parrot in her store that likes nobody but herself and Jen (although Alice is tolerated on the very rare occasions that both Lilia and Jen are out of town). Agatha cannot STAND that bird.

- Rio happened to be driving when Lilia's 911 call came over the radio. She immediately swerved over the median and proceeded break half a dozen traffic laws on the way there, giving poor Alice (who was napping in the passenger seat) a FUCKING heart attack.

Side note: I'm literally so excited for chapters nine and ten to come out, y'all are NOT READY. Also, I got my hair dyed right around the time I started this work and a side effect of it being many colors is that my fingernails (and sometimes my hands on wash days) have been stained dark blue and purple. So while y'all were crying over chapter seven, I've just been sitting here wriggling my fingers like: hehehehe I possess the Darkhold :3

Chapter 9

Chapter Summary

After a hell of a day, the coven reconvenes to drink, unwind, and have some difficult conversations.

Chapter Notes

HUGE shoutout to spillentireuniverses for inspiring Jen's backstory. I took my own spin on it, but the idea was definitely inspired by their Jen/Alice fic. They're a brilliant writer and you should ABSOLUTELY go check out their works for this fandom!

TWs: (skip for spoilers) mentions of mental health issues and alcohol abuse

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“So that was the guy who’s been stalking you, huh?” 

 

Jen takes a sip of her wine, wincing as she swallows it. “Yeah. My old attending from back in Boston.” She touches the bruises forming on her throat, biting her lip.  “I wondered when I saw the pictures from the security cameras, but I hadn’t actually seen him in person until now. I sort of just let myself think it was the trauma talking.”

 

Agatha shifts the ice pack from her face ( nothing broken, the paramedics had said, but would hurt like a bitch for a few days at least ) to take a sip of her drink. “I still can’t fully believe it. I mean, it’s been over a decade. That creep’s still holding a grudge against you?”

 

“Apparently, enough of one to stalk me to another state and try to kill me,” Jen sighs. “God, that’s going to be another few years of therapy bills.” She sets down her drink and shuffles over in the booth to sit a little closer to Alice, dropping her head onto her shoulder. The latter wraps an arm protectively around her shoulders, shifting to the side to let Jen lean on her despite their height difference. 

 

“All that because of a years-old harassment complaint? That guy has issues. ”

 

“And one that didn’t go anywhere,” Jen mutters bitterly. “Tale as old as time. Pretty young resident meets puffed-up, dick-waving doctor with a god complex. He got to keep his job and his reputation, while I got run out of the program after reporting him for trying to kiss me in front of a freaking comatose patient.”

 

“It wasn’t just over that, you know,” Rio adds. The women all glance around to look at her. “I had a friend do some research while you all were getting checked out at the hospital. It turns out you weren’t the only person who reported him, just the first. The first in a long string of women, actually.” 

 

She takes a swig of her cider. “He got fired recently, and the hospital’s debating suing him. That’s what set him off. He only came after you because you were the easiest for him to track down.”

 

“Ugh.” Jen shudders, huddling closer to Alice’s side. “That’s so incredibly creepy. Maybe I need to take a break from social media.”

 

Lilia hums. “Well, break or not, he certainly won’t ever be doing that again. To anybody.” 

 

Agatha snorts. “Because he’s going to jail or because you bludgeoned him?”

“Both.” The older woman swirls her cocktail, looking a little too pleased with herself for the circumstances. “Let’s see if he ever underestimates a pretty face again.”

 

Jen chuckles and grabs her glass. “I’ll drink to that. To never underestimating a goddamn pretty face.” 

 

As the women tap their drinks together, she glances over at Rio. “Especially one that you’ve kind of indirectly been a bitch to. Who may or may not have saved your life.”

 

Rio raises an eyebrow at her. “Is that your way of saying sorry?”

 

“Sorry, thank you, whatever,” Jen mumbles, obstinately fixing her attention back on her wine. Alice gently pinches her arm and the beauty guru sighs.

 

“Fine. Sorry for being a bitch, and thank you for saving my life. Consider this a tentative olive branch.” 

 

Rio smiles at her, an actual genuine smile. Jen rolls her eyes, but there’s not nearly as much heat behind it as usual. Changing tact, she grabs their resident detective’s hand and squeezes it. “And I’m sorry to you too, Alice. You’ve been trying to protect me for weeks and I’ve been too stubborn to let you. I could have saved everyone a lot of headaches if I’d just let my pride go- especially Agatha.”

 

Agatha switches the hand holding her ice pack and gives her a thumbs up. “Oh, I’ll be fine. Just so we’re clear though, I fully intend on milking this guilt trip for as long as I possibly can. Starting with convincing you to get us another round.”

 

“Oh, come on- ” 

 

“The paramedics said the bruises will take weeks to heal, you know,” Agatha sighs dramatically. “I’ll have to face my coworkers, my students, my boy… all with these horrible, hideous things on my face. Poor Billy will have to see his godmother like this, you realize that?”

 

“Really?” Jen snarks. “You’re gonna use Teen as an excuse? Like he hasn’t seen us all do stupider shit before?”

 

“What, like stabbing a cop?” Agatha retorts.

 

“It was a butter knife!” Jen complains. “There was no stabbing!” 

 

“The scar it left says differently,” Agatha drawls, leaning around to poke at the aformentioned mark. Alice squeaks and tries to squirm out of her reach. Agatha cackles with surprised delight. 

 

“Oh my god, are you ticklish?”

 

“ No!” Alice protests, trying to bat her away. Jen grins wickedly, taking advantage of her position to dance her fingernails along Alice’s ribcage. 

 

“Oh yes she is.”

 

Alice shoves them both off. Agatha flops across Lilia’s lap like an uncooperative cat, while Jennifer wriggles out from under her arm and stands. “Okay, fine. I’ll be back. Maybe. ”

 

Agatha rolls back into her seat, chortling with the rest of the group. After the events of the day, a little laughter really is nice. And she supposes the company she keeps isn’t the worst either. For a moment, she lets herself drift back to a happier time, before horrible bosses and lost children. When she’d been able to sit and enjoy a drink with friends simply for the sake of spending time together, before going home to her-

 

Her family. 

 

A jolt of horrible, guilty realization goes through the pit of her stomach and she glances up at her ex. Rio stares back, an unreadable expression on her face. She doesn’t speak. She doesn’t have to. Agatha knows she’d heard.

 

I’ll have to face my coworkers, my students, my boy …Poor Billy will have to see his godmother like this, you realize that?

 

His godmother.

 

My boy.

 

Agatha swallows thickly, feeling a sudden, sobering weight crash over her. “I’m- I’ll be right back. I need some air.”

 

She shuffles her way out of the booth- ignoring both Alice and Lilia’s concerned questions- and heads outside.

~

 

Jen’s hip catches Agatha’s as she pushes through the crowd and out the door. She blinks in confusion, staring at her. “Wha- hey, Agatha! Where’re you going?

 

Agatha- as per usual when she’s in a bad mood- ignores her. Jen stares at her for a moment as she sweeps outside to stand alone on the bar’s outdoor patio, before turning around and returning to the booth. 

 

“Um, what happened to her? Because I know manners aren’t really her thing, but usually she’d at least take the drink I bought her before stomping off to sulk.”

 

“Be nice,” Lilia chides. “It’s been a hard day for her. Probably even more so than the rest of us.”

 

“I don’t know if I’d go that far,” Jen mutters, but doesn’t press the issue. She passes out the new batch of drinks before returning to her position leaning against Alice’s side. When the other woman doesn’t immediately drape her arm back around her, she nudges her impatiently.

 

“Hey. C’mere. You have two hands, you can hold me and your drink.”

 

Rio chuckles. “Subtle as ever, Jenny.” 

 

Jen rolls her eyes, flipping her the bird. “Hey, it has been an extremely long and incredibly traumatic day. And right now, I am just drunk enough to admit that Alice here-” she squeezes her friend’s arm pointedly- “-has very impressive biceps that I just so happen to enjoy having wrapped around me.”

 

Alice blushes, but obligingly pulls her close, taking a sip of her drink. “What’s in this? I know I told you to surprise me, but now I’m stumped.”

 

Jen shrugs. “Not sure. Think it was mango, raspberry, coconut, and some other ingredient I can’t remember.” She waves a hand aimlessly. “The little gooey things at the bottom.”

 

Alice takes another sip. “Ooh, lychee jellies. Good call, Jen.” She chews one appreciatively. “You know, sometimes I like being the sober friend. I get all the really fun drinks.”

 

Rio tilts her head at her. “You don’t drink?”

 

“Mm-mm.” Alice fishes out another jelly cube with her straw and pops it into her mouth. “Alcoholism kind of runs in my family, especially on my mother’s side. I promised myself pretty young I would never go that way.”

 

She chuckles wryly, a hint of bitterness creeping into her voice. “Gran took vodka, Mom took whiskey, and I take antidepressants. Breaking the generational curse, one day at a time.” 

 

Rio nods in understanding and Lilia leans over to squeeze her shoulder comfortingly. Alice gives her a small smile. “It’s okay. Besides, the silver lining is that you guys always have a designated driver. And, you know, I get a front row seat to whatever bad ideas you have when you’ve been drinking.” 

 

Lilia gives her a look (clearly still slightly concerned about her sudden change of tone). Jen, however, effectively lightens the mood when she scoots over and nuzzles into Alice’s neck, purring softly into her collar. “Bad ideas, huh? I can think of a few right now.” 

 

“Whoa-kay! Easy there, J.” Alice puts a hand on her forehead and gently pushes her away while the other two chuckle. “Jeez, what exactly is in your drink, huh?”

 

Rio snorts. “She’s always been a flirty drunk.”

 

“Mmm, who says I’m drunk?”

 

“You did,” Alice reminds her. “Here, give me your glass. You’re going to spill if you keep this up.” 

 

She wrestles the cup from Jen and sets it down on the table, glancing at her watch as she does. “Oh, damn. It’s getting late. We gotta get home and get you- ” she prods Jennifer lightly- “-in bed before Teen comes home from Eddie’s house.”

 

Jen promptly snatches the glass back from her and downs the remainder. “Only if you come with me, baby.” 

 

Lilia rolls her eyes, finishing her own drink. “You’re all ridiculous, each and every one of you. Someone go retrieve Agatha and we’ll get going.” 

 

Rio stands. “I’ll go.”

 

“Talk about bad ideas,” Jen snipes. Rio gives her a cold look. Jen (ever the mature adult) sticks her tongue out at her. 

 

“Please. Lose the act. Don’t pretend like your intentions are all pure and innocent, not when we all see the way you look at her.” She looks Rio up and down contemptuously, though Rio can’t help thinking that there’s a little playacting involved. “You look like you’re planning to swallow her whole. Like some kind of creepy freaking python.”

 

“Romantic,” Rio replies sarcastically. “Though I suppose it’s not the most inaccurate thing you’ve ever said.” 

 

“Ugh, whatever.” Jen rolls her eyes again, slinging her arm around Alice’s waist. “Not sure when it happened, but I’m somehow both too drunk and not drunk enough for this.”

 

Alice sighs, awkwardly hauling them both upright. “I’m gonna go with too drunk- although honestly, I’m not sure when it happened either. You had like…two and a half glasses of wine.” She glances between Lilia and Rio. “Look, I’m gonna go close out our tabs and then take Little Miss Red Wine Supernova here out to the car. I don’t care what you two do about Agatha, I just want everyone waiting here when I come back. Okay?”

 

Rio raises her hands in surrender, throwing Alice a thumbs up. Alice gives her a dubious look, but quickly gets distracted by Jen starting to sing softly to herself. “Okay, nope, that was not a prompt. C’mon, let’s go”

Rio laughs mirthlessly. “Then you know I loved her. I still love her.” She shrugs. “Even though she’s kind of a bitch sometimes.”

“Good intentions don’t heal a wounded soul,” the older woman counters.

Gods know I’ve gone and pined after my fair share of pretty girls throughout the years, even fallen in love with one or two. I remember I kissed the most beautiful woman at a concert in the eighties.” She frowns. “Might’ve been the seventies, actually. She did ask me to run away with her to a commune in California.”

She glances over her shoulder at the woman still standing there, hands now in her pockets and a cold expression on her face. “I’m sure Agatha and Rio will be along in a moment.”

 

Rio growls under her breath and whirls on her heel, stalking out towards the door. Alice shakes her head minutely as she watches her go. 

 

“And people ask me why I don’t make friends with coworkers.”

Rio finds her wife ( no, ex-wife, she reminds herself. It’s been so long, yet she still has to remind herself) standing outside on the patio, leaning against the railing and gazing up at the nearly full moon. It’s so bright it could practically be day, illuminating Agatha’s slightly battered but absolutely beautiful face as she breathes in the night air. After the attack, she’d changed into a black halter top and a fringed cardigan embroidered with roses, the latter of which hangs loosely around her arms. 

 

Lady Time has been more than kind to her , Rio notes. Though there are more lines on her brow, her cheeks have also hollowed out somewhat, making her jawline stronger and giving off the impression that Rio’s eyes are devouring a priceless Roman statue rather than her ex-wife. Like an expensive cask of wine or a work of art, age seems to have only made her more wonderful.  

 

She’s as stunning at forty-nine as she was at nineteen.

 

Rio tilts her head, admiring the way the moonlight shines off of her ex’s long, messy dark hair as she leans back upon hearing her approach. She resists the urge to tangle her fingers through it as she walks up behind her, instead gently tapping her nails along the curve of her spine. 

 

“Hey.”

 

Agatha inhales, back arching against her fingertips. “Hey yourself.”

 

The air between them is thick with tension, charged with words vibrating on the tips of their tongues. Not trusting herself to speak quite yet, Rio hovers her fingers at the spot where Agatha’s shoulder blades marry to her neck, silently asking for permission. Agatha nods, eyes fluttering closed as Rio’s fingers brush against her bare skin. She makes a noise somewhere between a hum and a soft moan as Rio’s nails stroke small circles along her back.

 

Rio’s heart speeds up just slightly. Agatha is leaning into her touch, not away. Tentatively, she pushes the envelope a step further, resting her full hand on Agatha’s shoulder.

 

Does she know how much she’s missed her touch? How even the slightest contact feels like rubbing alcohol on every wound in her soul, like ice on a fresh bruise, like stepping into a steaming hot bath after hiking the world’s tallest mountain- a sharp sting, then comforting warmth as her every pain begins to fade to the background of her mind. 

 

Lilia had called her a drug. Rio would call herself an addict. 

 

All she wants is her.

 

As if reading her mind, Agatha opens her eyes and spins on her heel. She hesitates, brushes some stray hair out of Rio’s eyes. Then- to the other woman’s complete and total surprise- she pulls her into a tight embrace. 

 

Oh. 

 

Rio’s own eyes slide closed in a mixture of ecstasy and sheer relief as her hands slip up to hold Agatha in return. Her wife’s fingers gently cup the back of her head, tenderly pressing their faces together. Chest to chest. Cheek to cheek. She’s so dizzyingly close and Rio is afraid to breathe, afraid to frighten her away.

 

She sways in her arms, moving gently to and fro with each rise and fall of her chest. Her ex-lover doesn’t tighten her grip, doesn’t try to restrain her or keep her immobile. Agatha has never tried to stifle any part of her, not the parts that can’t quite keep still or the ones that can’t quite keep friends. No, Agatha would never. Instead, she rocks with her, burying her face in her hair to ground herself against the motion. 

 

God, Rio loves her.

Rio exhales sharply, the reminder coming through harsh and cold in the warmth of Agatha’s embrace. “Mmm. I wondered a little. He doesn’t look anything like Alice.” Upon feeling Agatha’s brow furrow against her cheek, she adds a clarification. “When I asked, she told me the boy I saw on the roof that night was her son. Think it was the first thing she thought of.”

 

Agatha snorts softly. “She’s a good friend.”

 

“And a terrible liar.”

 

Agatha chuckles, then sighs and pulls away slightly. Rio’s hands remain stubbornly on the small of her back, refusing to let her go. Not yet, her heart pleads. Not yet. Agatha indulges her, letting Rio cling to her as she moves to cradle her face in her hands.

 

“Are you angry at me?”

 

She truly does consider it for a moment, but ultimately decides against it. “No. Maybe in the morning. But not tonight.” She lets go of Agatha with one hand, trailing it around to squeeze her wrist. “I can’t be mad at you tonight.”

 

Agatha nods, gazing into her eyes. In this light, her eyes look even more brilliantly blue than usual. “Nicky’s birthday.”

 

Rio nods. “We still have a few hours left.”

 

They don’t fight on their little boy’s birthday. They’d agreed on it years ago, on a night when Rio had found an excuse to visit her in that dump of a town she’d moved to for awhile. It’s the one time when- if they’re together- they can forget all the bitterness and resentment and just be . A part of her knows it isn’t healthy, isn’t even sustainable- but she intends to cling to it until the last sparks die. 

 

She kisses Agatha’s knuckles, then the scar on her palm from when she’d cut herself on the birthday present she’d been wrapping for her. Handcarved silver inlaid with little jade moons, gemstones woven throughout the handle. Rio still has that beautiful blade . “So you’ll go to Switzerland with me tonight?”

 

Agatha strokes her hair, looking at her softly. Fondly. Almost tenderly. If Rio focuses, her expression almost seems different from the usual bouts of desperation and lust that land them in this position. 

 

She quickly stamps down the twinge of hope that twangs within her like a clear, sweet note plucked on a freshly tuned violin and squeezes Agatha’s hand. “Sweetheart? Yes or no?”

 

Agatha closes her eyes and takes a deep breath. “Yes.”

 

“Yes?”

 

“Yes.” Her ex-wife leans in close, stopping just before their lips meet. “Yes. Let’s get out of here.”

Chapter 10

Chapter Summary

In which the train wreck lesbians kiss a little and then live happily ever after...oh wait, we're only halfway through the story.

Chapter Notes

So...personal update! I still have a couple chapters prewritten, but my upload schedule/response time may slow down a little...because my family got a puppy 🖤 she's ten weeks old and a precious little baby, but will very much be taking up my attention for awhile lol

 

Now, for work related stuff...TWs for this chapter are (skip to avoid spoilers): some mild sexual content and some implied religious bigotry. I literally could not think of a better way to phrase that :/

See the end of the chapter for more notes

———————

 

She’d met Agatha Harkness for the first time in 1993, singing in the church choir their mothers had forced them into. They’d been seventeen and eighteen, sneaking glances at each other over their sheet music as their dreadfully droll director had prattled on and on for what had seemed like an eternity- Agatha in that stupid checkered dress her mother always made her wear, Rio in her bejeweled choker and velvet skirt. Agatha had run her fingers along that skirt when they’d had a moment alone, fascinated by the texture. Rio had simply held her breath, as if any sudden movements would frighten away this bold girl, with her gentle touch and piercing blue eyes.

 

The other girls had always stared at her like she was strange. Agatha stared at her like she was beautiful. 

 

Her mother had been so pleased that she’d made a nice, normal friend. Just a nice, God-fearing choir girl from a decent family. She’d made Agatha snort when she’d told her that, the bars on the Ferris Wheel’s cage shaking with her laughter as the lights of the fair flashed across their faces.

 

“ I’m not that nice,” Agatha had whispered, shuffling across to sit next to her on the bench. The green striped carriage (one Agatha had compromised on, so long as Rio agreed to ride in the purple teacup with her) had rocked under her feet, swaying with Rio’s stomach as Agatha looped a finger through the ring on her choker and pulled her close. “ Not that nice at all,” she’d repeated, and kissed her as the ride began to crest its peak. 

 

They’d snuck kisses over and over again throughout the night- at the apex of the roller coaster, on the downwards swoop of the pendulum ride, waiting until just the right height to lean across on the carousel. Rio had gone in for one as they stumbled off the tilt-a-whirl and completely missed, just about face-planting in the sawdust as Agatha guffawed and gripped the back of her shirt to keep her steady. Then she’d pulled her up by the scruff and kissed her again, and everything had been alright.

 

Though they’d met young, Agatha hadn’t been her first love. From the first time she’d kissed her, however, Rio had known she’d be her last. 

 

Always you. Always yours.

 

She kisses her again now as her hips grind to a delicious halt, even as her lungs ache and her head begins to spin. Her chest struggles and heaves, bare breasts pressing repeatedly against her lover’s skin. Still, she kisses her desperately, insistently even, as Agatha’s fingers begin to gently slow. Fuck it. She needs Agatha more than she needs oxygen. 

 

Agatha, however- much to her ex-wife’s chagrin- doesn’t cooperate. She breaks the kiss when Rio’s eyelids begin to flutter, snorting fondly at the little noise of protest it gets her. 

 

“You’re ridiculous.”

 

Rio whines and tries chasing her lips. Agatha leans away, smirking at the way Rio’s wrists strain under her hands, struggling to lift off of the mattress to reach her. She only releases her when Rio goes limp under her, gazing reverently up at her as though she’d hung the stars in the sky. 

 

I love you. 

 

She flops onto her side, nestling her head under Agatha’s chin as soon as she rolls over to lie next to her. The other woman nuzzles into her hair, draping one arm over her waist and the other around her back in a tender embrace. 

 

Rio presses her cheek against Agatha’s collarbone, closing her eyes to fully soak up the sensation of her chest rising and falling underneath her. How long has it been since someone held her like this? Since anyone had touched her at all, outside of the occasional tackling of a suspect or a handshake from a colleague. She hadn’t realized how starved she’d been for a little warmth and affection until Agatha had hugged her outside the bar that night. Now, she can’t get enough.

 

When she finally opens her eyes, she notices Agatha craning her neck slightly, trying to look out the window of her motel room. Rio nudges her lightly. “Looking for something?”

 

Agatha tilts her head down at her, giving her her trademark little smirk. “What, you jealous? Can’t take my attention off of you for five seconds, can I?”

 

Rio cocks her head, refusing to let Agatha distract her just yet. The latter woman rolls her eyes. “Fine, fine. Alice may or may not have mentioned that you have a motorcycle now. I was curious . Sue me!”

 

A slow grin begins to creep across Rio’s face. “You always did want one, didn’t you?”

 

Agatha snorts. “Yeah, maybe when I was twenty-one. Don’t know if you’ve noticed, sweetheart, but I’m definitely not that anymore.”

 

Oh, she’s absolutely noticed. She loves it. She loves her . Rio props herself up on her elbows, stroking Agatha’s hair out of her face. 

 

“Come on. I’ll show you.”

 

“Wha- no, are you nuts?!” Agatha splutters, though her body follows obediently as Rio tugs her out of bed. “It’s nearly midnight and I have no idea where my panties are , I’m not going outside!”

 

Rio scoops up the discarded cardigan and wraps it around the other woman’s bare figure. “We’re at the edge of town, cariño. The roads outside this place are practically abandoned , no one will see us.”

 

Agatha crosses her arms petulantly as she watches Rio fish a leather jacket out of the pile of clothes in her suitcase ( though whether her sulking is because of the suggestion or the zipper  closing up, it’s unclear ). “If you’re wrong and some gross drunkard gets a look at my tits-”

 

“I’ll shoot him,” Rio replies without any hesitation. Agatha’s mouth drops open in a startled little oh and Rio smirks. Good to know she can still catch her off guard.

 

Agatha shrugs and clears her throat, clearly trying- and failing- to recover some of her bravado. Adorable . “Well, I suppose if you promise… ”

 

“Oh, I swear, baby,” Rio croons. She grabs Agatha’s skirt and tugs it over her own hips, privately delighting in the way she swallows. “Cross my heart and hope to die.” 

 

She offers Agatha her hand. Her gorgeous professor ( mmm, she likes that ) hesitates a moment longer, then takes it. 

 

“Fine. Fine, fine, fine.”

 

Rio beams and kisses her. Then, ignoring Agatha’s squawk of protest- “Hey hang on, my shoes-!” - she tugs her out the door.

 

~

 

The moon glints off of the polished metal of the motorcycle as Agatha runs her fingers along the leather seat. Rio leans on the handlebars, trying not to seem like too much of a peacock. Still, she can’t help but puff up her chest the tiniest bit when her ex-lover whistles appreciatively. Though it certainly hadn’t been her main objective when she’d dragged Agatha outside, she does love to show off the machine. She doesn’t indulge in things too often- never having been one for flashy or expensive possessions - but her bike, with its shimmery green wrap and the flowers hand-stitched into the cushion, is her one exception. 

 

Agatha raises an inquisitive eyebrow at her. “I thought green was supposed to be an unlucky color for these things?”

 

Rio laughs. “Darling, I was born in a graveyard on Día De Los Muertos. You know I thrive on bad luck.” 

 

She’s only half joking, and Agatha knows it. Though she had come into the world halfway through the festivities, she’d actually been born in the back of her parents’ car on the way home from visiting her abuela’s grave. Years later, after her mother and stepfather had thrown her out, she’d taken Agatha to the cemetery and fucked her on that same grave. 

 

Good times.

 

She nudges Agatha’s arm gently. When she arches an eyebrow and moves it, Rio lifts the seat and fishes out a shiny purple object. 

 

“Here. Put this on.” 

 

Agatha gives her a dubious look. “Why?”

 

“Because I like that witty mind of yours better when it stays inside your skull.”

 

Her ex-wife rotates the helmet in her fingers, examining the white pentagrams decorating the shimmery violet paint. “First of all, this is extremely unsexy. When I asked you if you brought protection, this was not what I had in mind.”

 

Rio rolls her eyes. “Quit complaining and put the helmet on. I’m not taking you anywhere until you do.”

 

“And where exactly do you plan on taking me at this hour, hm?” Agatha snarks, though she starts to twist her hair into a bun anyways. She drops it after a moment, mumbling something about not having a hair tie, and simply tugs the helmet on over her messy dark curls. Rio takes a moment to admire her ( the damn thing looks as good on her as she’d hoped when she’d bought it) before grabbing her own helmet and straddling the bike.

 

“For a ride. Come on.”

 

There’s a large, almost always empty field behind the motel, one she’d discovered her first night in town. A dirt path cuts through the center- not entirely smooth terrain, but one that she can easily navigate with a little care- leading to a small clearing in the woods beyond.

 

She rides that path with an easy twist of her wrist, reveling in both the purr of the machine underneath her and the way the motion presses Agatha against up her back. Agatha’s arms are wrapped tightly around her waist, providing her with the delicious contact she’s still craving. The helmet Rio had given her digs lightly into her shoulder as the bike’s tires skip over a small dip in the gravel. She laughs as Agatha tightens her grip slightly. 

 

“Little jumpy there, mi vida?” 

 

“Shut up,” Agatha mutters, readjusting herself against her. “This is much more nerve wracking than it looked twenty years ago!”

 

“I like it!” Rio chirps. She tugs the handlebars up to make the bike jump, pleased when it earns her a soft squeak and a squeeze from her ex-wife. “Really gets the heart pumping. Good for clearing the mind.”

 

Agatha is too busy clinging to her to retort. Rio can feel her hair blowing to and fro against the back of her neck, fluttering like soft fingers against the curve of her spine. She regrets ( not for the first time since that night) that she only has two hands. She’d love to run one through that wild dark mess, feel the tangles she’d put in it throughout the night pressing against her fingers. Alas, she needs both her (regrettably few) hands to drive. 

 

Those hands immediately begin to wander as soon as she pulls the motorcycle over in the moonlit clearing. One leans back to cup Agatha’s head (though the latter shakes it off briefly to wrestle the helmet off), while the other pins her hand to her waist. Agatha rolls her eyes against the back of her neck.

 

“Clingy.”

 

“Mmhm.” She doesn’t even bother to deny it, fingers twining through Agatha’s as they dismount together. Rio pops open the storage compartment once more and pulls out a thick plaid blanket, which she tosses onto a relatively smooth patch of grass after probing around with her toe. She plops down onto the blanket and curls up, dragging Agatha along with her. Not for the first time in the past few weeks, she remembers, and smiles to herself.

She flops one arm out, allowing Rio the space to burrow against her side. Rio nuzzles her cheek happily, snuggling in close to her chest as Agatha wraps her arms around her.

 

“I’ve missed this.”

 

Agatha snorts into the top of her head. “What, the drunk sex or the naked stargazing?”

 

“Mmm. That too, but no.” Suddenly feeling significantly more sober, Rio tilts her head up to look Agatha in the eyes. “I’ve missed you. I’ve missed us. ”

 

Agatha’s smile falters and she turns her head away. “There is no us. We may be in Switzerland tonight, but that’s only for tonight.”

 

Rio grasps her chin, forcing her to meet her gaze again. “There was. There could be again, querida.”

 

“Rio...”

 

“ Agatha.”  

 

She sits up and flips over so that she’s lying on top of Agatha. Not unlike that asshole from earlier today, and the thought makes her sick. Nevertheless, she swallows and continues. “We can’t keep doing this forever, amor. You know it, I know it, your friends know it…”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes, looking away to mask what Rio knows to be discomfort. “Don’t tell me Lilia actually got under your skin. Gods, I wondered what you two were talking about.” She flashes Rio a halfhearted attempt at a cheeky smirk. “Don’t know if you’ve noticed, sweetheart, but she’s a little on the batty side. That’s not an age thing, either, she’s just sort of always been like that.”

 

“Maybe so,” Rio concedes. “But at least some of what she said was right. For years, we’ve been stuck in this cycle of love and hate and grief…and it hasn’t happy, but it’s been something, so we didn’t care. I didn’t care. But there will be a day where one of us decides that she can’t keep living her life like this, and I don’t want to lose you when that day comes.”

 

I almost lost you today forms and then dies on the tip of her tongue, the thought too horrible to even vocalize. There’s a sudden pulse of emotion running through her, the alcohol and the adrenaline from the day beginning to wear off. She can still practically smell the blood Agatha had coughed out earlier, can still practically hear the sounds of that filth beating the life out of her. There had been a moment when she and Alice had rushed in, when she had seen Jen lying there half-strangled and heard Agatha’s pained cries, that she’d been terrified that she’d been too late. 

 

Not again.

 

Never again. 

 

She presses her forehead to her ex-wife’s, closing her eyes to hide the tears brimming in them. “Don’t make me lose you again, my love. Please .”

 

There’s a beat of silence, with nothing but the sound of their breath echoing between them. After a moment, Agatha reaches up to meet her, cradling her face in her palms.

“You don’t have to be afraid of anything , ” Rio murmurs. Pleads, even. “You just have to love me.”

“You aren’t saying no.”

 

Her ex-wife exhales tiredly. “I’m not saying anything right now. In case you haven’t noticed, I’m kind of drunk.” She lets go of Rio’s face and settles back into the blanket. “Come back here, you’re shaking.” 

 

Rio lies back down, trying to ignore the pit forming rapidly in her stomach. She rests her head on Agatha’s chest, choosing instead to listen to the sound of her heartbeat. 

 

“Promise me we’ll at least talk in the morning.”

 

Agatha nods, absently stroking her hair. “Yeah. Yeah, sure, we can do that.”

 

It’s not much, but it’s something. With that knowledge (a little hint to hang onto, something to stave off the nagging fear that she’s running out of time) Rio closes her eyes. Ignoring Agatha’s annoyed murmuring about how they can’t sleep out here, she lets herself start to drift off in her love’s embrace. Agatha sighs softly underneath her as her own breathing starts to even out, despite her best efforts. 

 

If nothing else, at least Rio gets a night in her arms.

I’m really hoping you already know this, but I guess I’ll remind you just in case, because apparently you and Jen are both a little more drunk than I thought- Teen has the day off school tomorrow for a teacher work day, and since you and I managed to both have the day off too, we all promised him we’d stay in and let him teach us how to play DND, remember? Jen and Lilia are both closing up shop and everything, he’s- shit, hang on.”

 

“...”

 

“Uh- yup, everything is fine. Just a work call, nothing too exciting.”

 

“...”

 

“Yeah, of course he can. Eddie is always welcome. Just- let us know what time he’s coming over, okay?”

 

“...”

 

“Yeah, me too. You are absolutely right, it is gonna be awesome. …Okay, goodnight!”

 

“...okay, I think he went downstairs. Agatha, I’m not kidding, you’ve gotta get your ass home. He’s really excited about this, I think he’ll actually be heartbroken if you don’t show. Look, I- I’m not Jen, I really am NOT concerned about what you’re doing with Rio- actually, I’d like to hear a little less about it. But I love that kid to death and I know you do too, so just- just call me as soon as you get this, okay? I’ll come get you whenever, even if you don’t see this until the middle of the night. I’m actually gonna leave my ringer on in case I fall asleep, so please, please …just call me back.”

Though it’s not the first time she’s ever woken up hungover in a field, October 17th is certainly the first time it’s happened since she’d turned forty. Agatha groans in protest at the shaft of sun that practically blinds her as she begins to stir. 

 

“Oh my god, that’s awful. Oh, my poor eyes.”

 

Rio (who’s somehow snuggled even closer to her in her sleep), coos happily and noses at her neck. “ Mmm . Buenos días, querida.”

 

Agatha mumbles under her breath but obligingly kisses her forehead, keeping her eyes screwed shut. Between the bright sun in her face, the grass under her back, and the sudden warmth stirring in her heart, she’s feeling more than a little discombobulated. 

 

Gods, she’d hoped the surge of affection she’d felt towards her ex-wife had just been the result of getting the shit beaten out of her. The fact that she’s still feeling it in the daylight is something she’s not ready to confront. 

 

Gingerly, she tries opening her eyes once more. This time, thankfully, a cloud has shifted in front of the overhead sun, keeping it mercifully out of her eyes. She sighs in relief. “Oh, that’s so much better. ”

 

Rio kisses her jaw, seeming perfectly content regardless. Agatha toys with her hair absently, grumbling when the clouds shift and the light catches her eyes again. “Ugh, why is it so bright out at this time of day? I know nature is beautiful and all, but this is just plain ridiculous.”

 

As she squints, a sudden thought occurs to her- one that makes her gut twist with dread. She sits bolt upright as though someone had shoved a steel rod through the base of her spine.

 

“Wait, what time is it? Where’s my- shit, I don’t have my phone… ”

 

She spins towards Rio. “Please tell me there’s a clock display on that bike.”

 

Her ex-wife sits up, a hint of annoyance crossing her face. 

 

“Got somewhere you need to be all of a sudden?”

 

“Not that it’s any of your goddamn business, but yes,” Agatha snaps, jumping to her feet. “Is there a clock display or not?”

 

Rio crosses her arms stubbornly ( a bit petulantly, in Agatha’s opinion ). Agatha groans, jumping up to look herself. 

 

Her stomach drops to somewhere in the sewers.

 

1:15 PM.

 

Shit. Shit, shit, shit. 

 

“No, no, no,” Agatha hisses. “Okay, I gotta go. I gotta go now.” She darts back to the blanket and grabs her helmet, reaching to tug on as she straddles the bike once more. 

 

 Rio, however, doesn’t move. 

 

Agatha glares at her, crossing her own arms. “I do not have time to fight you on this today, Rio. ¡Ándale! Move your ass!”

 

 “You promised we’d talk today,” Rio reminds her. Her black-tipped fingernails dig into her own arms as she tightens up, preparing to dig in her heels. Agatha squeezes her eyes shut, pinching the bridge of her nose. 

 

“Yeah, well…not the first promise I’ve broken today. You can get in line with everyone else who’s going to be pissed at me.”

 

Rio laughs mirthlessly. “So what, you’re just seriously going to run away from this?” Her voice takes on a venomous, ugly edge. “What’s the matter, Agatha? Did I actually make you feel something for once?”

 

Cold, hard anger twists in Agatha’s gut. She drops the helmet, watching with petty satisfaction as it bounces and nearly strikes Rio’s shoulder. “ Don’t fucking do that.”

 

“What?” Rio sneers. “They’re your words. Doesn’t feel so good to hear them directed at you, does it?”

 

“You-”

 

Rio throws up her hands. “You know what? Fine. Lo que sea. Go spend time with your shiny new replacement family.” She gives Agatha a cold smirk. “That’s where you’re going, right? Don’t need to be a detective to figure that one out.”

 

Agatha splutters, caught slightly off guard. “I- wait, what? That’s what you’re fixated on here? Are you jealous? ” She laughs in disbelief. “I’m in my forties, living with three roommates and a teenage boy, and you’re jealous? What the hell is wrong with you?”

 

“You love them!” Rio bursts out. “You love them, and you hate me. Why don’t you want me?!” 

 

She glares bitterly in her ex-wife’s direction. “All I want is for you to admit you love me. The one thing I want, and you’d rather spend your time giving it to another woman’s son! And you’re asking what’s wrong with me?!”

 

Agatha growls under her breath. “Leave the kid out of this. Believe me, my raising him was the last choice for both of us. Don’t punish him for getting stuck with me.”

 

“Why do you talk about yourself like that? ” Rio demands. “God, Agatha, do you have any idea what I-”

 

“Because I’m not a good person!” Agatha snaps. “I- I’m not a good person. And we both know I’m not a good mother.”

 

She quiets a little as she says it. 

 

Gods, she means it, doesn’t she?

 

In an instant, the energy between them changes, both reeling from the confession. Rio’s face drops, all the anger melting out of her. “Agatha, baby, no. No, no, no. Sweetheart.”

 

She stands and tries to put her arms around Agatha. The latter steps away, shaking her head. 

 

“Don’t.” 

 

Rio purses her lips, but obediently lets her go. Agatha takes a few deep breaths to steady herself. A part of her heart- the part she’d left behind the first time they’d had this conversation- stings with an old, familiar pain. 

 

Having a decent reason to do it this time doesn’t make it any easier.

 

“I’m not a good person,” she repeats. She turns to look Rio in the eyes. “I’ve burned every bridge I had, and I did it for you . I’ve moved towns twice, turned my life upside-down, blown up more friendships than I can count, all because I could never get over you.” 

 

She leans over and grips Rio’s jaw in her hands- not tightly, but none too gently either. “And if it weren’t for the kid, I wouldn’t hesitate to do it all again. Gods, if it weren’t for him, I probably would’ve brought you inside that first night and screw what Jen had to say about it. But he’s here, and he’s a good kid, and he doesn’t have anyone else but me. And I...”

 

She lets go and steps away. “I’d ruin myself for you in a heartbeat. But what little decency I have left, whatever scraps didn’t die with Nicky…can’t let this kid get caught in the fallout. So this here? This is the end of the road.” 

 

Rio opens her mouth, but Agatha cuts her off. “No. We’re done. And I mean it this time. You’re going to finish this case, and we’re going to avoid the hell out of each other until you do. And when you leave, after however long it takes…” She takes another harsh, slow breath. “I don’t want to see your face. Ever again. We clear?”

 

Another flash of anger crosses Rio’s face, but it quickly cools and settles into resignation. “Crystal.” 

 

Agatha nods, trying to ignore the way it stings. “Good. ”

 

She turns and begins to walk away, starting down the path they’d come from. She stops in her tracks, however, when Rio calls after her. 

 

“Agatha?”

 

She pauses and glances over her shoulder, more out of reflex than anything. “Hm?”

 

Rio crosses her arms, staring daggers at her. “That boy isn’t yours, you know.”

 

Agatha’s lips twist into a frustrated snarl. Without another word, she spins on her heel and stalks off down the path.
