Friendly reminder to anyone threatening my life that I can't finish the story if you kill me

Chapter 12

Chapter Summary

Agatha suffers the consequences of her little...escapade, alongside the rest of her family

Chapter Notes

as per usual, working on replying to comments and getting close to wrapping up the story! The infant hound is keeping my hands full, and I've actually had a birthday since I saw y'all last, so I've been BUSY lol.

On an unrelated note, would y'all be interested at all if I made a couple playlists to go along with this story (as in a few sets of songs for each chapter)? And if I did, would you want me to post the playlists for the unposted chapters along with them, or should I post them ahead of time and let y'all puzzle over them? 👀

See the end of the chapter for more notes

It takes her far longer than she cares to admit to reach the road. The path seems much longer than it had last night, though walking barefoot certainly isn’t helping.

“What the hell was your plan here, Harkness?”, she mutters to herself. “God, I don’t even have a car .”

 

BEEP!

 

Agatha yelps, startled, as- right on cue- someone nearby slams on a car horn. She whips around, ready to unleash hell in the direction it had come from. She’s not even in the road, for crying out loud. 

 

Ahead of her, there’s a familiar gray car. 

 

Teen’s car.

 

She swears, clutching her cardigan closer around her. Gods, the last thing the kid needs is to see her like this. In her brief panic, it takes her a moment to recognize that it’s not, in fact, her godson behind the wheel. When the face of the driver registers- exhausted and angry, but very much feminine- she exhales a sigh of bitter relief. 

 

At least it isn’t Billy.

 

Her old friend pulls over to the shoulder and unlocks the door. Agatha hops into the passenger seat with a tired, humorless chuckle. 

 

“Gotta say, I honestly can’t believe you still came to get me. After all these years, I probably would’ve made me walk.”

 

Jennifer Kale stares straight ahead, eyes still firmly fixed on the road. “ I can’t believe I actually found you lying in a ditch .” 

 

“More of a field, really.”

 

Jen doesn’t reply, instead whipping the car back into drive. When they reach the next stop sign, however, she reaches down into the console and hands Agatha something. Agatha blinks, startled. 

 

“My phone? Wait, how did you-”

 

“Same way I found you,” Jen answers in a clipped tone. “When neither of you answered your phones, Alice made some calls and found out where Rio’s been staying. Then I drove over to look for you and thankfully happened to spot you two lovebirds snuggled up in your little nest when I drove past. Your ass was completely visible from the road, by the way.”

 

Agatha winces, and Jen rolls her eyes as she continues. “Once I watched for a minute to make sure you weren’t dead , I decided to head to the motel to wait for you- and when I got there, I realized you idiots left the door unlocked. So I ducked in, grabbed all your shit, and came back to get you.”

 

Sure enough, when Agatha glances down at the footwell, her clothes are folded in a neat pile. She squirms them on awkwardly but gratefully, though she curses under her breath when she realizes Rio still has her skirt. 

 

“ Shit. Ah, any chance you have a spare pair of pants or something?” 

 

“Pocket on the back of your seat.” 

 

She almost makes some snarky remark about Jen carrying an extra outfit at all times, but swallows it back upon glancing at her face and simply tugs on the leggings she’d found. “Thanks. So how much trouble am I in, huh?”

 

“Check your phone,” Jen replies coldly. Agatha raises an eyebrow, but does as directed.

 

You have: 20+ new messages

 

You have: 13 missed calls

 

Shit. 

 

She swipes through her messages, stomach dropping when she reads the final one from Jen. “Shit, shit, shit.” She thumps her phone against her forehead. “ Damn it. Is Teen okay?”

 

Jen laughs bitterly. “You somehow managed to simultaneously bail on the plans he’d made and give him flashbacks to the day his family died. What do you think?”

 

“ Jen.” 

 

The other woman sighs, perhaps realizing she’d pushed a step too far. “Fine. Sorry. Last I heard, he’s doing a little better since he heard you were okay. Alice managed to calm him down a bit, and his boyfriend had just gotten there when I left. They’re taking care of him.”

 

Agatha exhales, letting her head thump against the back of her seat. “Good. That’s good.” She glances over at Jen. “How angry is he?”

 

“Pretty damn angry,” Jen replies bluntly. “So is everyone else. At this point, the only one you haven’t pissed off is the bunny.”

 

“Pretty sure I pissed him off too,” Agatha mutters grimly, slumping down in her seat. “Unless someone else remembered to give him his salad this morning.”

 

Jen gives her a look. Agatha presses her eyes closed, though whether it’s out of shame or simply to avoid looking at Jennifer, she truly isn’t sure. “Billy fed him, didn’t he?”

 

“Yup.”

 

“Made him an Instagram-worthy little bowl and everything?”

 

“And sent you a picture.” Jen cocks her head at Agatha’s cell, forgotten in her lap. “He sent it to me too. I actually thought this one was pretty cute."

 

Against her better judgement, she picks her phone up and swipes up to the photo. Her young ward- as he always did when left in charge of her beloved pet- had clearly taken his time preparing the bunny’s breakfast. Agatha can practically picture him waltzing around the kitchen, humming like a damn Disney princess while he picked out, cleaned, and arranged the various vegetables in an aesthetically pleasing manner worthy of Jennifer Kale herself. She’s reminded him a billion times over that the rabbit doesn’t care if it’s pretty- she herself prefers to just toss it in a tupperware and shake it until Señor Scratchy- an asshole who very much takes after his mama- can’t pick out what he doesn’t like) but the kid, with his heart of friggin’ gold, still makes it part of his routine. 

 

For fuck’s sake, he’d even sprinkled some dried flowers onto this one. 

 

Agatha covers her face with her arm. “Aaaaannnnddd I’m officially a piece of shit.”

 

“Yep.”

 

After a moment, Agatha sighs, peeking out at Jen from under her arm. “Well?”

 

Jen raises an eyebrow. “Hm?”

 

She crosses her arms over her chest, bracing herself. “Well, you’re obviously going to yell at me. No way you got it all out of your system already. Wanna get it over with before we get home?” 

 

Jen rolls her eyes again. “You sound like a five year old.”

 

“Just trying to save time.” 

 

Her friend’s fingers clench briefly on the steering wheel, but- to Agatha’s surprise- uncurl after a moment. Her shoulders slump and she sighs, suddenly sounding more drained than Agatha’s heard in a long time. “Honestly? I don’t see the point.”

 

Agatha tilts her head. “ Really? Well that’s a first, isn’t it? You love yelling at me.” 

 

Jen shakes her head minutely. She doesn’t so much as roll her eyes, and Agatha finds herself more than a little bit unnerved. “That’s just it, Agatha. How many times have we done this since I’ve known you? You get lovestruck and stupid, I get angry, you get your heart broken, and we do it all again in a year or so.” 

 

She sighs again. “Don’t get me wrong, I am angry at you. Incredibly angry . But right now, I honestly don’t feel up like arguing. I’m exhausted, hungover, and frankly still a little shaken up from nearly getting strangled yesterday.  And clearly neither of us is going to change anytime soon, anyways.”

 

Agatha scrunches up her nose, thumping her head back against the headrest. “Oh my god, that almost feels worse. It’s like the equivalent of ‘I’m not mad, just disappointed’.”

 

“Well, if it makes you feel better, I’m definitely both,” Jen replies dryly. “And I’m sure I’ll find it in myself to be angry at you again later.

 

Agatha sighs, rubbing her forehead. Her bruises- though slightly less ugly in the daylight, thank god- are starting to sting again, and she briefly considers asking Jen to stop at a nearby gas station to grab an advil. Then, she remembers the bottle Teen keeps in his glove compartment (kept there mostly for the sake of Alice and her chronic migraines) and fishes it out, shaking a couple tablets into her palm and swallowing them dry. 

 

After all the events of yesterday, she supposes she should be grateful she isn’t feeling worse. Well, physically, anyways.

 

She tucks the pills away and closes her eyes again, flopping back against her seat. After a moment, however, she cracks her eyelids and peeks at Jen. 

 

“I ended it with her, you know.”

 

Jen glances at her out of the corners of her eyes. “Yeah?” 

 

“Yeah.” In her mind’s eye, she can still see Rio fading into the distance, can still picture the angry, pained expression on her face. She’d looked at her the same way she had the first time, all those years ago.

 

“I can’t do this anymore.”

 

“Querida, I-”

 

“No. Just don’t.”

 

She gazes at her feet, clutcing the blanket closer around her shoulders. “I want a divorce.”

 

Rio stares at her, a million expressions crossing her face. Hurt. Confusion. Anger. 

 

In an ironic twist, Agatha feels nothing.

 

When she glances over at Jen, she almost laughs. Her old friend, too, looks at her the same way. The same mixture of exhaustion, pity, and something else- as though she’s been bracing herself for so long, she doesn’t remember how to stop. 

 

Jen leans against the doorframe, eyes red and swollen. Her braids are dripping onto her kitchen tiles, and her sweater is still soaked from the rain- but for once, she isn’t complaining. She’s watching over the couple like a hawk, as if afraid to look away.

 

The officer who’d responded to her call from the bridge and had escorted them back to Jen’s apartment- a kind Asian woman a little younger than them, whose name Agatha hadn’t paid attention to- puts a comforting hand on her shoulder. “Jennifer, right? Come on, let’s give them a minute. I’ll sit with you, okay?”

 

Jen hesitates, but ultimately gives the woman her arm. The officer gives her a small but warm smile and gently turns her around, guiding her into the kitchen. 

 

Jen glances over her shoulder as the pair walk away. 

 

Agatha can’t bring herself to meet her eyes.

 

Now, all these years later, Jen simply shakes her head and turns the car onto their street. “Good for you, I guess. Try and do it right this time.” 

 

She jerks her head towards the porch, where two people- a boy and a woman- sit together side by side. “For his sake.”

 

 Agatha’s stomach sinks at the look on Billy’s face when he looks up to see their car. His eyeliner is smudged and- despite having at least six inches on her by now- he looks incredibly small huddled against Alice’s side. 

 

The latter has an arm protectively around him, letting him lean on her shoulder. As the two in the car step out and approach, Alice straightens up and nudges Teen’s arm, whispering something softly to him. She then stands and holds out a hand to Jen, pointedly avoiding Agatha’s eyes. 

 

“Come on. Let’s give them a minute.” 

 

Agatha snorts before she thinks, earning herself a glare from both other women. Deja-fucking-vu. 

 

Jen squeezes Teen’s shoulder with her free hand as she brushes past, letting the screen door slam behind her. This time, she doesn’t look back.

 

After the other two women are gone, Agatha takes a deep breath and sits in Alice’s abandoned spot next to Teen. She glances over at him. 

 

“So…should I start by apologizing, or do you want to get it all off your chest first?”

 

“I want to know why ,” Billy replies after a brief pause. He’s still stubbornly staring at his shoes. “I think I figured out the rest.”

 

She exhales sharply. “That…isn’t easy to explain, kid.”

 

“ Try.”

 

Agatha raises her hands placatingly. “Okay. Okay, I can try.” She chews on her lip momentarily, reaching for the correct words. 

 

“Sometimes, when a relationship ends badly, no one gets closure. Especially when it ends in a way no person wanted. And when you don’t get closure, those feelings can linger and turn into something…ugly.”

 

She touches some place vaguely above her heart. “When that happens, especially when- and I’ll admit it just this once- you’re hotheaded like I am, it can get messy. Love goes from love to hate like-” she snaps her fingers. “-that. And hate born from love is a monster that leads people to make some stupid decisions.”

 

“Like you just did?” Teen snaps, looking sharply at her. Agatha nods, digging her fingernails into her palms.

 

“Yeah, like I just did.” She sighs. “Like I’ve been doing for a long time now. I don’t have an excuse, a reason, or even really a promise to give you that’s worth anything. But-”

 

Her godson stands abruptly, crossing his arms. “Well, you’re right about that last part.”

 

 Agatha half rises, but pauses partway, unsure whether to follow him or not. “For what it’s worth, I ended it today. For good this time. She…won’t be part of our lives again.”

“And how am I supposed to trust that?!” Billy bursts out. “How am I supposed to trust you , Agatha?! From everything I’ve heard- and don’t go blaming anyone else for this, no one told me on purpose, but you’ve promised that to a whole lot of people. ”

 

“I…”

 

“Tell me!” he demands. “ Tell me how I’m supposed to trust you. ”

 

A million thoughts run through her mind, words flitting to the tip of her tongue. Something reassuring, maybe, or something comforting. Something loving, even ( motherly, if she dared to think it) . 

 

How do I tell you how different you are?

 

How do I tell you how important you’ve become to me? 

 

How do I tell you why ?

 

I want to tell you why. 

 

I want to tell you about him. 

 

But her brain- stupid, hungover, heartbroken brain- freezes in possibly the worst moment she can imagine. 

 

She says nothing. 

 

Billy stares at her accusingly for a second, sad, puppy-brown eyes practically begging her to say anything. When she flounders longer- completely dumbstruck, emphasis on the dumb - he turns on his heel and storms off into the house. 

 

Wham! 

 

Agatha’s goddamn brain finally catches up with her when the door swings harshly shut behind him. “Shit. Shit, shit, shit!”

 

She leaps upright- nearly tripping over her own feet- and chases after him. “Wait. Wait! Billy, I’m-”

 

The basement door slams. Agatha reels back as though it had sent off a shockwave, wincing. “…sorry.”

 

“Wow,” Jen’s voice echoes dryly through the open porch door. “That went impressively badly. Like, somehow even worse than I expected. Honestly, you should get some kind of award for lack of tact.”

 

“I see you’re feeling better,” Agatha snaps, running her fingers through a few of the tangles in her hair. She glances over and sees Jen and Alice curled up together on the porch swing, with Lilia sitting in a nearby chair. 

 

She pads over and stands in the doorway, for the first time getting a decent view of Alice’s face- and winces. The younger woman somehow looks even more exhausted than Jen had sounded earlier, dark rings standing out under her unusually dull brown eyes. 

 

 Agatha briefly thinks back to Alice’s words, read from the transcript of the voicemails she’d left last night. Had she really waited up all night for her? She must have, judging by even a glance at her face. Christ, she looks terrible. 

 

Alice’s jaw drops and Agatha- apparently determined to fulfill Jen’s little declaration- realizes with an instant pulse of regret that she’d said the last bit out loud. She starts to splutter out an apology- perhaps to blame the lack of sleep or the pounding in her head- but Alice gives a disbelieving, mirthless laugh and cuts her off. 

 

“ Wow. Okay, you know what? You can go to hell, Agatha.”

 

She wriggles out from under Jen’s arm and stalks off, shoving roughly past Agatha on her way. Jennifer jumps up to follow her- though whether to offer comfort or simply commiseration, Agatha isn’t sure. She can practically hear the latter if she thinks about it, however.

 

Sorry, hon. Believe me, I know she’s a bitch. I really don’t know why I brought her into your life.

 

  Lilia- the last remaining on the porch- pinches the bridge of her nose. “Well that was an outright disaster on all counts.” 

 

Agatha makes some vague noise of agreement, crossing the porch to flop down at the edge. She glances over at Lilia, raising a quizzical eyebrow. The latter simply shakes her head, an almost sympathetic look on her face.

 

“I’m not going to lecture you too. I doubt there’s anything I could say that would be more punishing than what’s already been said.”

 

“You just feel bad for me,” Agatha mutters. She’s sure she looks a sight, sitting there a bruised mess in yesterday’s top and Jennifer’s spare leggings. Completely pathetic, like one of the strung-out, drain soaked stray cats Lilia likes to feed. The older woman rolls her eyes. 

 

“You do have a habit of digging the hole deeper when you can, don’t you?”

 

Agatha chuckles humorlessly. “It’s a talent.” 

 

“It’s a coping mechanism ,” Lilia scolds sternly. “And one you ought to change, if you truly do intend to better yourself. So you listen up, cupcake!”

 

She grasps Agatha’s shoulder and turns her none-too-gently to face her. “You’ve got a family here. It may not have been the one you wanted, but you’re stuck with it. And this family will not break unless you really force it. So I suggest you stop this little dance of yours, take a breath, and learn to change your tune.”

 

Agatha twists around, staring petulantly at her feet. Lilia lets her, though she leaves her hand on her shoulder. “Alice has a kind heart. She’ll forgive you once she gets some rest. Billy too, though he might take more time. Boys his age always do hold a grudge. But he loves you, and that will win out in the end.”

 

She doesn’t respond, focusing instead on fighting back the sting in the corners of her eyes- though it would be obvious enough to anyone who looked. Lilia squeezes her shoulder gently, clearly having noticed but kindly choosing not to comment.

 

“I’ll leave you alone for a minute. Then you ought to come inside and eat something.” 

 

Agatha nods. Lilia lets her go- apparently satisfied that she’d made her point- and walks away, leaving her to her own devices. The screen door- in desperate need of an oiling- lets out an unpleasant shrrrk! as she slides it closed behind her. Then- perhaps as an act of mercy or perhaps simply out of habit- she glances back and closes the heavy glass doors as well. 

 

Whatever the reason, Agatha finds herself oddly grateful. The dam she’d put up when she’d woken that day (no, earlier, she realizes- as far back as yesterday, when Rio had come blazing into Jen’s store to save her life) is starting to strain now. She buries her face in her hands as something inside her cracks painfully.

 

At least now, though the clear porch doors only offer a pretense of privacy, no one can hear her as she starts to cry.




Chapter End Notes

Jen’s demeanor here may seem odd, but it was meant to mimic her attitude in the last couple episodes (before she realized Agatha was the one who bound her). She’s had a long, traumatic couple of days and by the time she actually got to Agatha in person, she’d lost some of that vitriol that we got from her texts (don’t worry, her rage is just charging). Also, a part of her was relieved that Agatha really HADN’T been murdered. Shocking, I know.

With that choice out of the way…

notesnotesnotes (guess which key on my keyboard is sticking lately challenge) .Will all of these be important later? Will any of them? Maybe. I dunno. We'll see. I'm in a mood. What kind? That'd spoil the surprise.

- Teen runs an Instagram account for Señor Scratchy. (He also has a TikTok page where he documents the various happenings in their household, which only Alice and Jen know about)

- Alice takes a mild interest in cop shows. On the other hand, Jen HATES medical dramas (or at least, she really, REALLY likes complaining about them)

- Jen and Alice met through work.

- Jennifer is allergic to cats

- Though Lorna Wu DOES have an actual gravesite, it's considered a public monument. Alice has a private place where she buried some artifacts from her, that she goes to when she wants to visit her mother.

- Rio makes a hobby of insect taxidermy and collecting other oddities.

- Teen hates seeing ANYONE in his family cry, but it hits him especially hard when it's Alice.

Chapter 13: Thanksgiving Special

Chapter Summary

We deviate from our normal angsty programming to bring you a special holiday episode, with only a LITTLE bit of feelings involved.

Chapter Notes

It's technically Black Friday actually, because I started this impulsively at 9:30pm on Thanksgiving. but happy Turkey Day anyways! I'm thankful for you guys, even though I'm still working on replying to your comments :)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

November 21st, 2021.

 

Agatha probably should have thought about this earlier. No, she definitely should have thought about this earlier. In her defense, however, she’s had a lot on her mind the past couple weeks. 

 

“This Thursday is Thanksgiving. As in four days from now. ”

 

Curled casually up at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee, Alice crooks an eyebrow at her. “Yeah? You just went on Thanksgiving break, that’s kind of a given.”

 

Agatha snatches a mug out from the cabinet and slams it. “Oh, did I? How much detective training did it take you to figure that out?”


Alice tilts her head. “Did you forget?”

 

”Shut up.”

 

In point of fact, she actually had forgotten. She’d taken a couple weeks of leave after…the accident, and with that had somewhat lost track of time.

 

Swooping in from behind her, Jen nudges her out of the way to get to the sink. “Okay, and what’s the big deal? Not like we really celebrate.”

 

Agatha grabs her by the shoulder and whirls her back around, dropping her voice to avoid the kid in the basement hearing. “But Wanda did. ”

 

Jen frowns. “Wanda? Who-” Her eyes widen and she nearly drops her glass. “Oh, shit. Billy’s mom?”

 

Behind her, Alice chokes on her coffee.  Agatha nods, releasing Jen’s shoulders. “ Yeah. Girlfriend was big on family holidays. Think it had something to do with wanting her boys to have what she didn’t growing up.”

 

“And this is Billy’s first holiday without her,” Jen murmurs. “Oh, poor kid. ” 

 

Alice sets her drink down. “Jeez, yeah. Okay, so what do we do?”

 

“If I knew that, we wouldn’t be in this situation, would we?” Agatha cocks her head at her. “What did you do your first holiday after losing your mom?”

 

Alice winces. “How about we don’t do that, and don’t talk about it either?”

 

Agatha shrugs. “Okay, so back to my original question.”

 

“That was my question.”

 

“Technicalities.” Agatha waves an airy hand in Alice’s direction, earning herself an eyeroll. “Any suggestions on how we pull this together? Anyone know how to cook a turkey?”

 

Jen leans against the counter, crossing her arms. “I don’t think we could even have a turkey defrosted by Thursday.”

 

Agatha blinks. “Wait, how long does that take?”

 

“At least four days, I think.” 

 

“Thursday is technically four days.”

 

“Four whole days, Agatha.” 

 

Agatha rolls her eyes. “How do you know that, anyways? You really expect me to believe you’ve ever cooked a turkey in your life?”

 

Jen opens her mouth to protest, then closes it. Agatha nods smugly. “Mmhm.” 

 

Alice frowns. “Maybe Lilia has?” 

 

“Maybe Lilia has what?”, the older woman calls down the stairs. Agatha shushes her, leaning through the doorway gesturing her to come join them in the kitchen. She frowns but obeys. 

 

“Now what are you three whispering about in here?”

 

“Thanksgiving,” Alice replies, popping up to pour her a cup of coffee. “We’re sort of realizing none of us knows how to do…well, any of it, actually. I mean, we were talking about turkeys, but…” 

 

She shrugs. “I don’t know about you guys, but I’ve never really made stuffing or pie or anything either. The only thing close I can think of is my mom’s dumplings, but I haven’t made those in years.”

 

Lilia raised an incredulous eyebrow, turning to face the others. “You too? Both of you?”

 

Agatha shrugs. Jen shakes her head, looking awkwardly at her feet. 

 

“My governess taught me how to make green bean casserole. I don’t know if I remember how, though. I was pretty little.” 

 

“And my mom wasn’t exactly the type of person to sit and bond over family recipes,” Agatha drawls. “Although I guess your mom wasn’t either, Jen, considering your nanny gave you hers.”

 

Lilia shakes her head, taking the mug from Alice. “You are both in desperate need of parenting.”

 

Agatha snorts. “What do you think we keep you around for?”

 

“Don’t get distracted,” Alice chides. “Lilia, what did you do as a kid?”

 

Lilia hums fondly. “My aunties always had a potluck. Everyone brought something except the host. Maestra said that everyone should contribute to the meal except the person whose table we ate from.”

 

“I like that plan.”

 

“Agatha, we live together.”

 

“And I hate cooking.”

 

“ Okay!” Alice claps her hands. “So unless anyone has objections, what I’m getting is that our best bet is to all try to individually contribute something. Text what you’re making to the group chat, and Lilia and I should be able to figure out the rest.”

 

Agatha shrugs. “All I’m hearing is that you’re taking charge of this whole thing, and I’m down for that.”

 

Alice rolls her eyes. “Agatha, it’s just one dinner. You’re making it sound like some kind of sting operation. It’s gonna be fine.”

 

Tuesday, November 22nd.

 

House Chat Thing

 

Mommy Issues ™: Huh

 

Mommy Issues ™: This might be a stupid question, but do we still make stuffing if we’re not actually making anything that we need to stuff?

 

The Worst Type Of Vegetable: Oh my god.

 

~ 

 

Thankfully, by the time the holiday rolls around, they’ve actually started to pull their shit together. Agatha grins in spite of herself as she passes by the kitchen, watching Jen and Lilia bickering over whether pecan pie should be served chilled. Alice ducks between them to pull a dish out of the oven and nearly trips over Señor Scratchy. 

 

“Ack! Agatha, can you come get your rabbit? And double check if Billy has any allergies, would you?” She frowns, setting the pan down. “Probably should have done that before we started cooking.”

 

Agatha snorts, but picks the bunny up and snuggles him under one arm. “C’mere. Let’s go find Billy before someone breaks something.” 

 

She pads down the hall and strolls down the basement stairs, rapping her knuckles against the bedroom door. “Hey, kid- oh, sorry, Teen. You in there?”

 

No answer. She frowns, trying again. “Billy? Are you- huh?”

 

The door swings open, revealing an empty bedroom.

 

 “Um.” She turns around in a small circle, as if she’s going to discover her godson hiding behind the drapes like he had when he was little. No luck. “Well, shit.”

 

Alice glances around at her as she heads back into the kitchen, giving her a quizzical look. Agatha shrugs with the arm not holding her rabbit. 

 

“So…I take it none of you have seen the kid, then? He’s not upstairs or anything?”

 

Jen snorts. “What, did you lose him?”

 

“Well, unless you’ve seen him, then…”

 

Jennifer looks over her shoulder, eyes widening at the bemused look on Agatha’s face. “Wait, did you actually? Oh my god, is he not here? Did he sneak out? When?! ”

 

Agatha shrugs again, trying to clamp down her own growing anxiety. “You live here too, Jen! Your guess is as good as mine!”

 

Alice dusts her hands off, turning to face them. “Are you guys being seri- oh, you really are. Okay, uh… crap.” 

 

She pinches the bridge of her nose. “Okay, okay. When’s the last time we saw him? And he can’t drive, can he?”

 

“He’s thirteen, Alice!” Agatha snaps. Alice throws her hands up.

“I’ve known him for maybe two weeks, I’m still learning things! He has a bike, right?”

 

“Where could he possibly bike to?” Lilia demands, crossing her arms. Jen grits her teeth, shaking her head. 

 

“Kids can move fast when they’ve got someplace they want to go. But where would Billy want to-”

 

The realization hits her. Agatha snaps her fingers. “Okay, wait. I’ve got an idea.” 

 

She snatches up her car keys, starting for the door at a run. “Give me forty minutes or so, then either call me or the police. I think I know where he is, but it’s going to take me at least half an hour to get there.”

 

~

 

She makes it there in twenty minutes. Sure enough, Billy Maximoff sits alone on the steps of his old home, bike abandoned at his feet. Agatha breathes a quick sigh of relief, firing off a quick text to Alice once she’s parked. 

 

A seedy-looking man in an ugly teal turtleneck approaches her as soon as she clears the edge of the driveway. “Oh good, ma’am. Are you that boy’s guardian?”

 

“Who’s asking?” Agatha grumbles, fighting the urge to shove the man aside. He runs a self-important hand through his over-gelled hair ( probably leaving it uncomfortably sticky, like the hands of a child who doesn’t quite know how to handle a popsicle but insists on holding it anyways).  

 

“Well, I’m a very well known realtor, you see. I’m taking a prospective buyer to tour the home, and we can’t have an unaccompanied minor just loitering on the steps while we do, I’m sure you understand.”

 

“What, is he bringing down your precious property values by just sitting there?” Agatha snaps. She decides she’s over decorum and pushes the man aside. “For your information, bozo, that kid is actually the legal owner of this house. So technically, you’re the one who’s loitering. Now get the hell off his property, asshole, or I’ll make my roommate come arrest you.”

 

Ignoring the realtor’s protests, she struts around him and sits next to the young teen. Billy refuses to look at her, probably expecting a scolding for sneaking out. In reality, she probably should scold him- but she’s never been a by-the-book parent, and the circumstances are certainly odd.

 

 Besides, the kid looks like a scraggly stray kitten. Even she can’t really bring herself to kick him right about now. 

 

Billy sniffles a little. With his hood pulled up and still stubbornly facing away from her, Agatha can’t actually see if he’s crying or not. After a moment, he reluctantly turns around to look at her.

 

 “Are you mad at me?”

 

She snorts. “No.”

 

His lip trembles. “O-okay, good. Because I…I…” 

 

Agatha sighs. “Oh boy. Alright, c’mere, kid.”

 

The boy flops over into her arms, whimpering quietly into her shoulder. She pats his back, glaring at the realtor who’s still rubbernecking at the edge of the yard. Take a fucking hint, dude. 

 

By the time the kid calms down, the sun has started to sink below the rooftops. Finally, he sniffles once more and lifts his head from her shoulder, taking a few deep breaths.

“M’sorry for running off. It’s just…it’s Thanksgiving, and I just…I just…”

 

Agatha gives him a squeeze. “Shh. No one’s mad.” She tilts her head. “Well, Jen might be a little mad. But I’m pretty sure that’s just her natural state of being.”

 

Billy gives a watery little laugh. “Okay. Can we go back…there, then?”

 

Agatha chuckles, standing and stretching. “There?”

 

The boy winces. “Sorry. I’m just…getting used to thinking of somewhere else as home.”

 

Damn, if she’d ever regretted a comment faster.

 

 Billy hops up and starts for her car. Before he gets far, however, Agatha catches him by the shoulder and spins him around to face her.

 

“Hey. Don’t ever apologize for how you’re feeling in this household, okay?”

 

The young teen’s eyes well up again, just slightly. Agatha leans over, letting him press his forehead against hers. 

 

“You’re going through hell , kid, and I know there’s nothing anyone can do or say to make it better. So don’t you dare apologize for how you’re coping, you hear me? Scream, kick, run, fight- whatever you need to do to survive this, you do it, and you do it without worrying about what anyone else thinks. You are the only one whose feelings should matter to you right now. Okay?”

 

Billy nods, giving her the hint of a smile. Agatha grins ( a bit relieved ), grabbing him by the shoulders and shaking him gently in a way that had always made him laugh when he was small. 

 

“ Okay?!” 

 

“Okay!” Billy laughs, pushing her away. “Okay!”

 

He hooks an arm through hers as they begin to walk back to her car. “So wait, what are we doing for Thanksgiving?”

 

“Shoot, I knew I was forgetting something.” She sighs, patting his arm. “We really need to add you to the house group chat, kid. Sorry, Teen. ”

 

“Can I name it?”

 

“The group chat? Be my guest.”

 

Teen giggles eagerly and Agatha wonders briefly if she’s going to regret that decision. He squeezes her wrist slightly before letting go to climb into the passenger seat. 

 

“Hey, Agatha?”

 

“Hm?”

 

The boy drops his head onto her shoulder as she clips her seatbelt in. 

 

“Thanks.”



Chapter End Notes

They're all disasters and I love them

liiiiiittle bit of notes:

- I have literally NO idea why I started writing this and decided that Jen gave me "rich kid who grew up kind of bitchy because her parents hired someone else to parent her" vibes, but I did. So now she grew up with a 'governess', which Agatha mocks her constantly for- not because of the existence of the governess, necessarily, but because she refuses to ever call her her nanny.

- For Christmas that year, Lilia (who was raised by a flock of aunties, with her main caregiver being the woman she lovingly called Maestra) dragged all her chicks into the kitchen and taught them a few of her own family recipes.

Side note- this chapter has barely been edited and has not been beta read. If it's shit, don't tell me!

Chapter 14

Chapter Summary

An unexpected tragedy forces everyone to reevaluate their perspectives.

Chapter Notes

There is something SO PURE about a puppy that literally doesn't know anything but how to be happy. like seriously, she doesn't know anything except that she loves treats, her siblings, and her people, and she's just so HAPPY about it all. I can't even be mad at her for doing stupid shit like chewing up my laptop cord because I start to scold her and she just wags her tail because I'm talking to her and that makes her happy. I am but a slave to her whims. If she wants me to hold her instead of writing, I have literally no choice but to do it. That is why this chapter is late and I cannot bring myself to apologize for it.

Anyways with that being said, TWs for this chapter (skip for spoilers) include:

- child death and mentions of child death
- references to child abduction
- references to drunk driving and car accidents
- and just general dark thoughts.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

For at least a week afterwards, the atmosphere in the house remains thick and heavy, simmering with hurt and anger. Billy- in what Jen, on a day when she’s in a decent mood and actually chooses to talk to Agatha, refers to as a truly teenage move- has apparently decided to take out his own feelings on everyone in the household. Each morning, he leaves with a slammed door and not a word to anyone. If she makes it back from work any later than he comes home from school, she doesn’t see him at all until they repeat the cycle the next day. 

 

A few brief conversations with the other adults in the household confirm he’s acting the same way with them too- though the energy between them is similarly tense. Alice and Jen (who’s clearly recovered herself somewhat) have formed an odd- and sort of bitchy, in her rather biased opinion- alliance, seemingly taking it in turns to give Agatha the cold shoulder ( to the point where she almost wonders if they’re actually making a damn schedule of it). Lilia, at least, has thankfully decided not to hold much of a grudge.

 

At least not everyone in the household hates her. (Though Lilia still does remind her from time to time that she’s also angry at her, which she supposes is fair).

 

Although the upset starts to slowly cool into something a little less ugly after the first week or so- no one can stay that angry forever, after all- remnants of those disastrous two days linger in other ways. A small inflatable mattress starts to appear around the house, usually jutting awkwardly out of the basement stairwell. Agatha finds herself swearing at the damn thing more than once when she stumbles over it in the middle of the night, wondering why the hell it’s there. 

 

Then one night, she goes to get a drink and finds the dots waiting there for her to connect. Alice’s bed neatly made and unslept in, the stupid plastic pillow gone, and the basement door- leading to Teen’s room- left a crack ajar.  Memories of the nightmares he’d had when he’d first arrived stir in her mind, and she finds herself suddenly very grateful that Alice is a light sleeper.

 

She doesn’t ask either of them about it, knowing full well neither is in the mood to talk to her. But when Alice finds the air mattress quietly replaced with an actually comfortable cot, it’s Agatha she glances at. 

 

Señor Scratchy hops up to her later that night, delivering a thank-you note written in Alice’s slightly messy handwriting tied loosely around his neck- and when Agatha creeps past the living room where Alice and Jen are watching a movie, the former glances up and waves her in to join them. Jen gives her a dubious look, but Alice simply settles herself more comfortably against her side and returns her attention to the cheesy horror movie they’re watching. So Jennifer shrugs, and Agatha sits, and things slowly begin to return to normal.

 

Or at least, sort of normal. 

 

Teen still refuses to talk to her.

 

And that’s alright, really. She knows she can’t blame him, nor can she force him. She simply lets him be, and tries not to wonder if he’ll ever think of her the same way again. 

 

And then one day Alice comes home from work in tears, and everything changes once more.



~

 

Rain- not warm, soothing autumn rains, no. This was cold, stinging winter rains, bordering on snow, but not quite so- spatters the umbrella above her head. Rio collapses onto a nearby rock and buries her face in her hands, abandoning her aegis and letting the water hit her face. For the first time since she’s come into town, her ex-lover is the last thing on her mind. 

 

It’s so cold. Too cold for little bodies to be outside without a coat.

 

Her temporary partner ( at least, she assumes the person approaching is Alice- she doesn’t bother looking up) trudges through the mud and sits next to her, sniffling softly. She nearly slips when she sinks down- she’s not dressed for the weather, neither of them are. They’d been caught in the car when the call for an emergency search party had come across the radios, and- in a moment of wordless agreement that Rio’s not entirely sure she’s ever had with a colleague before- hadn’t bothered with going to get the proper attire.

 

Every second counts when a missing child is involved, and she knows it better than anyone. Por dios, she practically lives and breathes it- on both sides of the figurative coin.

 

But sometimes, even seconds are too late. 

 

“The techs say it looks like an accident,” Alice whispers after a moment. “The coroner will have to do a full report, but there’s no signs of foul play.”

 

She swipes at her eyes, accidentally smearing eyeliner along the back of her hand. “Her older sister said she liked to go look for fairies whenever it rained. She probably snuck out when she wasn’t looking and walked down to the lake. The rain would have made the banks slippery, and…”

 

Abruptly, she chokes on her words, a little sob escaping from her throat. “And- and…and she couldn’t swim.”

 

Rio digs her nails into her own forehead, as if trying to dig the memory out of her mind. It’s no use, however, and she knows it. The image of the sweet-faced little girl, floating face-up in the water as though she’d simply been lying in the bathtub, will no doubt join the ranks of faces who haunt the private recesses of her mind. 

 

She’d had beads woven into her locs. The braids- fanning out around her head like a halo- had been tipped with spots of pink and blue, with little white butterflies placed throughout like they’d been fluttering about her head. Someone had been so thoughtful with them, had no doubt sat with her for hours- maybe telling her stories, or playing a game, or watching a cartoon with her- while gentle hands plaited her curls into those small, beaded braids.

 

So much like every child Rio could never save, someone had loved her. 



~

 

Teen finds her curled up on her office floor later that night, giving Señor Scratchy a much needed brushing. Agatha glances up at him as he pads in, briefly pausing in her grooming of her beloved pet as the boy sits next to her.

 

“Hey.”

 

“Hey,” Teen replies, giving her a cautious look. “Can we talk?”

 

Agatha raises an eyebrow at him. “Sure.”

 

He nods, but doesn’t speak right away. The bunny thumps his feet impatiently and Billy reaches over to pet him. Agatha offers him the brush. 

 

“Here. Good for stress.”

 

Teen takes it and resumes preening the bunny’s fur. Scratchy snuffles happily, twisting around to hop into his lap. The little guy’s always been a sucker for a good brushing. Some shelter volunteer told her when she’d adopted him that most rabbits hated being groomed. She supposes that hers is simply exceptional.

 

Agatha watches her boys for a few minutes before breaking the silence. “So what’s going on? You still a little wound up from earlier?”

 

Billy nods quietly, focusing on a particularly thick patch of loose fur on the bunny’s hindquarters. Truth be told, they’re probably all a little shaken up. Nothing like one member of the household coming home from a doomed search for a lost little girl to really get them to forget about fighting for awhile. And Billy- even more so than everyone else- has always hated seeing Alice cry. 

 

Outside the window behind them, headlights flash- likely the aforementioned detective on her way out. She’d mentioned earlier that she had somewhere else she intended to go, though Agatha hadn’t caught where. Not that it matters much- Alice, out of all of them, is probably least likely to be up to anything stupid. Still, Agatha makes a mental note to at least try and wait up for her to come home. 

 

After all, Alice would do the same for her. 

 

Teen turns slightly to face her as Alice’s car fades into the distance. “Okay. For starters, I think Jen’s right.”

 

She tilts her head. “Right about what, exactly?” 

 

“That you need to go to therapy. And ,” he continues, holding up a hand to cut her off before she can start to protest. “Because I know you’re going to fight us on that like crazy, I want to talk to you in the meantime.” 

 

“Oooo-kay,” Agatha mutters slowly, trying not to sound petulant. “So back to my original question, then. About what?”

 

“I want to understand,” Billy replies. “I…I think it’ll help me be less mad at you. So I want you to tell me what you can about your past so we can both start to move on from it. Okay?”

 

Agatha takes a deep breath and lets it out slowly. Just a month ago, she would have told the kid in no uncertain terms to back off about her history. Hell, just a month ago, she had . But things have changed since then and she knows it. 

 

“Okay. Okay, we can do that. Where do you want to start?”

 

Teen sets the brush down and looks her straight in the eye.

 

 “Tell me about your son.”

 

~

 

The rain has started to clear by the time a rap on the window startles Rio awake. She rolls off the top of the crappy comforter she’d been dozing on- having not even bothered to take her shoes off before passing out, much less crawl under the covers- and awkwardly stumbles to the door, cursing the way the heel of her combat boots sink into the too-plush carpet. 

 

The realization that she probably should have looked through the peephole before opening the door hits her too late, with the latch already slid open and her hand halfway through turning the knob. Thankfully, when she swings it open, the face on the other side is friendly (if a tad bit awkward).

 

“Hey. Sorry to wake you.”

 

“No te preocupes. I was expecting you, I should have already been awake.” 

 

Rio steps aside, allowing Alice Wu-Gulliver to enter. The shorter woman pauses, no doubt trying to figure out what to do with the coffees in her hands. 

 

“Uh…”

 

“Oh, here.” Rio flips open the top of the fold-out desk against the far wall and takes the drinks from her, setting them down on the wooden surface. “Sorry, there’s obviously not a ton of space.”

 

When Alice- seeing the state she’d been in after leaving the scene- had asked if she’d wanted to meet up and talk later, after they’d both had a chance to calm down, Rio’s first thought had admittedly been a bar. The detective had reminded her patiently that she didn’t drink and had suggested they go get coffee instead, which would have probably been a better plan if they’d been meeting earlier in the day. But they aren’t, so here they are instead, in Rio’s one-person motel room. Not a ton of space is sort of an understatement. 

 

Alice doesn’t seem to mind, however. She simply shrugs, picking up what Rio assumes to be her own cup and taking a sip before settling into one of the barrel chairs across the room. “It’s a motel, it’s not like it’s supposed to be huge. Besides, I pretty much spent my childhood in hotel rooms. I’m used to it.” She glances around with a detective’s curious eye. “Although I still kind of can’t believe you’ve actually been living here for a month straight. If this case keeps dragging on, you should upgrade.”

 

Rio chuckles dryly and plops down onto the bed with her drink. She refrains from making a comment about how it’s a shame that they couldn’t go to Alice’s place instead, and Alice doesn’t mention the heat she’d no doubt caught from Jen about coming here to check in. They’ve been walking on eggshells, the pair of them, tiptoeing around what Rio assumes to be an emotional minefield- although she doesn’t actually know for sure. Alice carefully doesn’t bring up her home life, and Rio doesn’t ask, and the two of them have managed to maintain relative peace in that way.

 

Lost in thought, Rio at first doesn’t hear Alice speaking to her. When Alice says her name, she stirs and turns to her. “¿Que?”

 

Alice raises an eyebrow. “I asked if you’re doing okay. Guess that’s kind of an answer in itself.”

 

A knot twists in the pit of her stomach. For a second there, she’d actually managed to forget why Alice came. 

 

“I’d say I was, but I’m generally not in the practice of lying to colleagues. Are you?”

 

Alice shakes her head, twisting her cup in her fingers. “No.”

 

Rio nods, raising  her own cup to her lips- a hot chocolate, she hadn’t felt like any caffeine this late- and taking a swallow. It’s thick and overly sweet in a way that would make most people turn up their noses, but that stirs something oddly nostalgic in her heart. She searches for a moment before placing the memory. The bodega around the corner, where she’d stop sometimes on her way to school and buy a treat with her pocket money. She wouldn’t have been much older than the girl they’d seen today, the girl who loved fairies and the rain. 

 

She takes another sip, sighing softly at the way the too-hot confection burns her throat just enough to be soothing. “So was this your first?”

 

“Dead kid, you mean?” Alice’s fingers dig into her cup, so hard that Rio briefly wonders if she’s going to pop the lid off. “No. I’ve responded to one before.”

 

Rio raises an eyebrow, wordlessly asking her to elaborate. Misery loves company, after all, and she finds herself surprisingly hungry for it. 

 

Alice exhales, slumping back in her seat. She gets a few false starts in on her sentence before shaking her head and forcing herself to continue.

 

“Three years ago, right after I made detective, I was on my way home and happened past one of the worst accidents I’ve ever seen. Some guy was driving drunk, swerved across the median, and hit a family of three- Mom, Dad, and their teenage son. Threw them right off the road and into a tree. He came out without a scratch, but them…”

 

She rubs her arm with her free hand, as if the memory still brings goosebumps. “It was awful. The father and son were gone by the time I pulled over and got out of my car. I did what I could for the mother, but…”

 

She stares at her hands. Rio nods again, but stares questioningly at her still. 

 

There’s more, isn’t there? Something you’re not telling me?

 

Then she shrugs internally and repeats the question out loud.

 

Alice glances at her, not speaking at first. Rio can practically hear her weighing the choices in her head, though she’s not actually sure exactly what they are. After a moment, she sets her coffee cup down and rubs a hand across her forehead.

 

“Okay, I’m only telling you this part because you’re pretty much the only coworker I have who has virtually no chance of spilling this to my people.” Rio chuckles dryly and Alice gives her a small smile. “Sorry. But on the off chance that you end up talking to any of them…”

 

Rio makes a zipping gesture with her fingers. Her colleague- not quite partner, but not quite coworker either. Colleague had felt best, she’d decided- takes a deep breath and lets it out. 

 

“Okay. So I managed to get into the vehicle, right? There was no way I could have gotten the mom out, but she was in pretty bad shape, and I didn’t…I wanted…well, she could see me, and I didn’t want her to think no one was doing anything to help her. So I reached in through a window and…I at least held her hand.”

 

Her fingers are trembling, as if the muscles are remembering the feeling of the woman’s hands in hers. “She kept pulling at this locket she was wearing. I thought it was choking her, so I helped her take it off. I remember I was still holding it when they…took her away.”

 

RIo tilts her head. “What was in it?”

 

“Family portrait,” Alice murmurs. “That was why she was trying to show it to me. The family had another son at home. We wouldn’t have known to go looking for him if I didn’t open it. I didn’t go at all, actually, someone else did.”

 

“So, what, you feel guilty for that?”

 

Alice shakes her head. “No. No, that’s not it.” Now she lifts her gaze, looking Rio in the eyes. “I went home, cleaned up a little, then passed out on the couch. I probably would have slept there all night if Social Services didn’t come knocking on the door. Looking for Agatha. ”

 

Rio inhales sharply. Alice nods, confirming her assumption. “Yeah. You remember when she lived in Westview, right? Did you ever meet her next-door neighbor?”

 

“The bubbly red-head,” Rio replies quietly. “With the twin boys. I saw them in passing once or twice, I think- the kids more than the mother, I remember I almost ran over one when he chased his dog out into the street. But I never knew that they…that she…”

 

Alice laughs softly. “Neither did Agatha, actually. Or at least, she didn’t know the parents had legally named her the boys’ godmother. From what I heard, neither of them had any family above ground, and Agatha had become pretty fast friends with the mom when she moved in. So when they were asked about a godmother, the husband panicked and named her.” She sighs quietly. “Obviously, neither of them ever expected…well, you know.”

 

She picks up her cup but doesn’t drink, beginning to fidget with it again. “I never told Billy. It took me an embarrassingly long time to make the connection anyways, and then…he was just a kid, you know? I had no idea where to even start.” 

 

Rio takes another swig of her drink, processing the onslaught of new information. The cocoa is cooling now, going from delicious molten chocolate to something sludgier and entirely less pleasant. She wonders if Alice will find it rude if she gets up to reheat it.

 

“The boy didn’t find out when the case went to court? You would have been called as a witness, no? Unless the driver pled guilty, I suppose.”

 

Alice shakes her head. “The driver pled guilty, then was killed in prison for some other stuff he was involved in. That’s the most awful part. His death set off this explosion of legal red tape, and now everything even remotely related to him is buried behind it. So now I can’t even get any of Billy’s family’s things out of evidence.”

 

She runs a hand through her dark, disheveled hair (impressively so, really- Rio sometimes wonders how she manages to tame it into that smooth bun each morning), sighing with a heaviness of an officer at least fifteen years older than herself. “And I guess it’s not like he’s missing any of it. The stuff, I mean- he’s got plenty of their things. It just…would have been nice to be able to do that for him, you know? He’s…he’s a good kid, he deserves at least that much.”

 

Rio stares quietly at her, thoughts swirling around her head like crackling leaves in a gust of autumn wind. A few small, stray pulses of emotion run through her, but she pushes them down for the moment. She can feel those once Alice leaves.

 

For now, she crosses her legs under her, allowing the pressure to ground her. 

 

“Can you tell me more about him?”

 

Alice blinks. “About…Teen? You want to know about him?”

 

“Sí.”

 

Truthfully, she doesn’t. She frankly would rather forget that this boy (this boy who’s taken her son’s place and who has no idea how lucky he is) even exists , much less listen to what his life is like. 

 

But some small, reluctant part of her knows she needs to. 

 

~

 

Teen passes his fingers over the creased faces in the picture. He’s careful not to actually touch the paper, and Agatha’s grateful for that. She really ought to have laminated the photograph years ago, but some raw, still-grieving part of her refused to take it out of her wallet. 

 

“He looks like you.”

 

Agatha snorts. “No, he doesn’t. Somehow that kid came out of my body looking exactly like R- like my ex. I mean seriously, barely even a hint of me. Even Jen thought so, and she’s the kind of person who just thinks babies look like aliens.”

 

 Teen tilts his head. “Jen knew him?”

 

Agatha laughs, a hint of genuine humor creeping through into the sound. “She did. Aside from you, I think he was the only kid she’s ever liked. Here, look.” 

 

She plucks her laptop off the desk behind her and opens it. Though most of her photos of Nicky are kept in a small album under her bed, she’s digitized a handful over the years- including the one she pulls up now. Billy laughs in delight as she turns it towards him. 

 

“Oh my god, that’s awesome. She really let him do that?”

 

“Under duress,” Agatha chuckles, looking over the photo herself. In it, her son is perched on the lap of a much younger Jennifer, giggling happily as he pastes a thick layer of glittery gold lipgloss over her mouth. Her oh-so-perfect face is in an absolute state , eyelids and cheeks both smeared with a brilliant rainbow of colors that Nicky had gotten from one of her fancy highlighter palettes. At the little boy’s request- “Can we match, Auntie Jenny? Pretty please?”- she’d used the same one to similarly decorate his face before they’d gone out for dinner that night. Agatha had made sure to wait until Nicky had run out of the room to find his coat to bust out laughing at her.

 

They’d both laughed so much more then.

 

 There had been some kind of occasion, if Agatha’s thinking is right. She frowns, trying to remember what it had been. Perhaps misinterpreting her expression, Teen touches her arm. 

 

“Agatha? You okay?”

 

“Huh? Oh, yeah.” She bats his hand away with a wave of her own, blowing out a puff of air. “Not melting down just yet, kid. Just trying to remember- ah, here we go. Jen!”

 

The beauty guru pauses where she’s passing by in the hallway, giving her an unreadable look. Without Alice there to balance her temper, it’s a toss-up as to how she’ll respond. 

 

Agatha spins the computer towards her before she can think on it too long. 

 

“You remember this?”

 

Jen tilts her head- curiosity getting the better of her- and crosses the room, crouching gracefully next to her to look more closely. She inhales gently as she recognizes the people in the photo, eyes softening immediately. “ Oh.  Oh, of course I do.” She runs her fingers along the screen. “Wow. That must have been…during those first few weeks after I moved back from Boston, I think.”

 

“ That’s it!” Agatha snaps her fingers. “I couldn’t remember why Nicky was so determined to doll you up that night.”

 

Jen laughs quietly. “Yeah.” She glances over at Teen, chuckling at the fascinated look on his face. “After my residency fell apart, I moved back to New Jersey in a total rush. I ended up spending a couple weeks crashing on Agatha’s couch while I looked for an apartment.”

 

“She was a complete mess,” Agatha adds helpfully. “Seriously, I had to hide the ice cream for awhile.”

 

Jen rolls her eyes. “I was depressed , Agatha. And Nicky noticed.” She hums fondly. “I remember he wanted to do makeup because he knew that was what made me happy. And then later, he made Agatha take me out to dinner at-”

 

“At Chuck-e-Cheese, because that’s what made him happy,” Agatha finishes. “And so obviously that was the best way to cheer you up. Although I think it was at least a little bit because he just wanted to go to Chuck-e-Cheese.”

 

Jen shrugs. “I mean, it worked. It’s pretty damn hard to be sad while playing skee-ball with a glittery four-year-old.” 

 

Teen laughs. “I can’t believe anyone got you to go out like that, even a little kid.”

 

“Oh, shush.” Jen swats his arm good-naturedly. “Nicky just had that kind of effect on people. He really was-”

 

~

 

“…the kind of kid that it’s hard not to love.”  Alice smiles affectionately down at her phone. “And he makes us all better, in a way. I mean, we were close before he moved in, but now? He really did bring us all together.”

 

She laughs to herself, selecting a picture. “He loves planning stuff for us to do together, too. Family kind of stuff, you know? Here, look.” She scoots over and passes Rio her cell phone. “This was at a cat cafe he brought Jen and I to one day. He had just gotten his license and got so lost that it took us nearly two hours just to get there. And after all that, that turned out to be how we found out Jen’s allergic to cats. But Billy at least got to pet a few kitties before the Benadryl kicked in and we had to go home, so he said he was still happy. He’s a good sport like that.” 



Rio stares at the curly-haired boy in the photo, cradling two kittens under one arm and using his free hand to support a third in the hood of his sweatshirt. He’s got more cats than he does hands. He’s grinning happily at the camera- probably goofing around with Alice as she took the picture, jokingly calling for her to help him. She wonders if he’d begged to take one home. Probably not, if Jennifer really is allergic- but then again, boys who loved animals almost always did.

 

At least, in her experience.

 

She swipes to the next photo without asking, this one taken in what looks like a pumpkin patch. This time, it’s the boy- no, Billy- who’s holding the phone, posing for a selfie with Alice. The latter has her hands full, laughing with delight as she hoists a baby goat up to the camera like a prize marlin. In the very edges of the frame, she can make out Agatha wagging a finger at them, her lips parted in what had to be mid-scolding. 

 

Rio laughs softly in spite of herself. “You’re not bringing that thing home, you know!” She mimicks under her breath. “No way . The neighbors would riot!”

 

Alice snorts. “Yeah, pretty much. Although it actually turned out that our next-door neighbor, Mrs. Davis, ended up adopting it.” 

 

Rio blinks at her. “Really?”

 

“Yup.” Alice shrugs. “The other neighbors complain every once in awhile, but honestly, I’m happy for her. Sharon was pretty lonely for awhile after she lost her husband. If putting ribbons on a little goat and growing it treats is what makes her start to feel like herself again, I’m all for it. Actually, I’ve even been thinking of getting her some chickens to go along with it.” 

 

She grins a little mischievous grin. “The HOA might protest a little, but our house makes up nearly half the votes on the block. Plus, I’ve been hoarding I-O-Us from all the people who’ve urgently needed a favor from the neighborhood cop. I think the Proctors owe me five times over by now.”

 

Rio chuckles obligingly, but her mind has started to drift. She digs her fingers into her own wrist to ground herself. 

 

“So no goats in your house, hm?”

 

Alice laughs. “No. Just one stupidly spoiled bunny.” She takes the phone, swipes to another app, and hands it back. “Look, Billy made him an Instagram.”

 

“Ay, por dios,” Rio murmurs, looking over the page. “As if that rabbit wasn’t pampered enough by Agatha alone.” 

 

She stops on a photo of Agatha and the teenager, curled up together with the rabbit stretched across their laps. She’s grinning and ruffling his hair while he dozes, head nestled against her arm. 

 

She’s smiling so warmly. So happily.

 

A cold, sharp spike of sadness goes through her heart. That should have been her son. If the world was fair…

 

But then the world wasn’t fair, was it? Sometimes little boys and girls died. Her little boy had died. And that hadn’t been fair. There had been nothing fair about it, nothing she could even do to make it fair. 

 

There had been a child in this world, with not her blood nor her bone, but her smile. She’d sometimes told her lover that he smiled like her because hers was the first she’d seen.  

 

But it hadn’t been the last, had it? No, the last face had been a stranger’s. A face Rio had never known, would never know, would spend the rest of her days chasing. The only person she thinks she could possibly pursue with more vigor than Agatha. 

 

The stranger who had taken everything from her.

 

She stares down at the boy’s face, squished up against her ex-wife’s shoulder. He had had a stranger too, she realizes. He had had everything taken from him too.

 

Is it worse to be the one who left, she wonders, or to be the only one left behind? She supposes she doesn’t know of the former, neither of them do. But she knows far, far too much of the latter.  Loss is a monster always lurking in the corners of her vision, one she’d stopped leaving at home when she’d asked her old commander for an unimaginable transfer all those years ago, to a unit where so many faces- like the sister of the little girl who’d loved fairies, the girl who would wonder ‘what if?’ for the rest of her days- reflected her own pain like the surface of a finely polished blade. It had closed its jaws around her when Nicky had gone, swallowed her down when Agatha had gone too. Now it consumes her, the acid eating away a little more of her every time she comes home to her cold, empty apartment. 

 

She wonders, from time to time, when it will dissolve her away entirely. Perhaps it already has. If the young woman in the velvet skirt and the choker- the one who’d kissed Agatha Harkness at the top of the Ferris Wheel- saw her in the mirror, would she recognize who she’d become?

 

Alice grabs her arm. “Whoa, hey. Where’d you go there? You okay?”

 

Rio blinks, then blinks once again, startled by herself. There’s something wet on her eyelashes. 

 

 ~


The tears spill over before she realizes they’re there. Jen- now sitting next to her as well- drops the photo she’s holding and puts an arm around her. 

 

“Hey. Hey, breathe.”

 

Agatha grips her wrist and leans into her, chuckling wetly. Some part of her is still surprised that, after all this time, after all the times she’s hurt her with her own stupidity, Jen’s still around to comfort her. She’s grateful for it, though, ( although she’ll never be able to tell her that with a straight face ). 

 

On her other side, Billy nestles himself against her shoulder. “Agatha? Agatha, we can stop if you need to.”

 

His voice is so earnest, so sweet. She lets out a half-laugh, half-whimper before she can stop herself.

 

“You know, it’s when you say things like that…”

 

A real sob bubbles up in her throat and she covers her mouth with her free hand, trying and failing to force it back down. Jen gives her a gentle squeeze. 

 

“That he reminds you of Nicky?”

 

She exhales sharply and closes her eyes. It’s true that she’s been thinking it, but hearing the words out loud- especially now, with Teen at her back and her photos scattered across the floor in front of them- still stings like a bitch . 

 

Billy grasps her arm. 

 

“Agatha?”

 

He needs to hear her say it, she realizes. She takes a deep breath. 

 

“Yeah. Yeah, you do. If- if he’d had the chance to grow up, I think he would have been a lot like you.”

 

Teen nods quietly. “And that makes you sad?”

 

Agatha sighs. “Sometimes, yeah. But that’s not your fault.” She closes her eyes, leaning into Jen’s embrace. “I have a shit ton of feelings that aren’t your fault, Teen. Sad is one of them.”

 

“Sad is better than angry. Alice told me that once.”                                                 

 

“Did she now?”

 

“Yeah,” Teen replies earnestly. Jen nudges him. 

 

“You forgot the part where Lilia told her that first. Also, any context at all.”

 

“But I don’t know the context!”

 

 Agatha gives a watery laugh, patting his hand. 

 

“I’m not too surprised. Lilia has her quirks, but she’s a pretty wise old bat.” She frowns, thinking of their friend taking a crowbar to the back of a handcuffed man’s skull in front of two cops. 

 

“Well, sometimes, anyways.”

 

Teen laughs and Jen rolls her eyes. She starts to pull away, but Teen grabs her arm and holds her in place, keeping Agatha sandwiched between them. The latter knocks her head gently against his, careful not to dislodge the rabbit from her lap. 

 

“Mmph. You’re squashing me, kid.”

 

“Squashing you with affection ,” Billy retorts. “And I’m going to keep doing it. Unless you really do want us to go away, I guess. We can keep talking later either way.” 

 

Agatha considers, but ultimately decides against it.  “I suppose you can squash me a little longer. Just this once.”




~

 

“You’re sure you’re gonna be okay?”

 

Alice tugs her hood over her red-streaked hair, but doesn’t leave quite yet, glancing over her shoulder as she hovers in the doorway. Rio shrugs. 

 

“No sé. But I need to be alone, anyways. No offense.”

 

Alice turns back to face her, concern written all over her face. It gives Rio pause, if she’s honest. It’s been a very, very long time since since someone looked at her like that. 

 

“You spend a lot of time alone, don’t you?”

 

She cocks her head, caught off guard by the question. “¿Perdóname?”

 

Alice shakes her head, face flushing. “Er- never mind, sorry. Just…forgot my filter for a sec.” She fidgets with her sleeve. “But seriously, I don’t mind staying a little longer, if you need a friend. It’s been a rough day. For both of us.”

 

Rio blinks, contemplating the offer. Had it been anyone else, she’d frankly probably assume she was being propositioned. But Alice’s offer, she knows, is out of genuine kindness and concern- and for a moment, she truly does consider it. 

 

A chirping noise sounds from behind them in the room. Alice pats her pockets, features shifting into a mixture of annoyance and embarrassment. 

 

“Oh, damn, my phone…”

 

Rio unfreezes, turning to grab the cell from the desk where her colleague had left it. The screen lights up on her way and she sneaks a glance as it leaves her hand. 

 

9:38 P.M.

 

Big Mama Witch: just checking to make sure you’re alive. And that you don’t need a ride back from wherever you ran off to.

 

Well, it doesn’t take a detective to figure out who that message is from. And Rio knows then- despite the hint of involuntary bitterness that sparks in her heart- that she can’t take Alice away from her, no matter how tempting the idea is. 

 

She hands the phone back with a shake of her head, offering her a small smile. 

 

“You’re very kind, but no. You have people to get home to, so…” 

 

She makes a shooing motion with her hands. Alice wavers for a moment longer, clearly anxious about leaving her on her own in this state. Then, after a moment- much to Rio’s surprise, again-   she leans up on her toes and gives her a quick, chaste peck on the cheek. 

 

“Take care of yourself, Agent. I’ll see you tomorrow.”

 

Then she’s gone, and Rio is left alone with her thoughts once more. 

 

After Alice’s headlights fade away into the distance, she finally shakes herself out of her stupor and heads to the bathroom, finally shucking off her mud-stained dress clothes. The water in the shower is cold and suspiciously metallic- Alice is right, she really should think about upgrading lodgings- and the pressure stings her back. The chill is grounding, however- good for gathering her thoughts- and by the time she gets out and changes into her pajamas, she’s made up her mind.

 

Sitting on top of the too-thin comforter, face illuminated by the moon, she picks up her phone to make a call.



~

 

A few days later, an unexpected package arrives at the door. It’s a plain green box, wrapped neatly in striped twine and with no return address- in fact, there’s no address at all. Only a name, written across the front in neat, swooping cursive.

 

“Huh,” Agatha mutters, carrying it inside. “Teen, did you order anything?”

 

The boy glances up from the card game he’s playing with Alice and Jen. “No?”

 

She shrugs and sets it in front of him. “Well, it’s addressed to you. So here.”

 

Teen tilts his head, untying the twine. “Ooh, spooky. I wonder-”

 

He gasps as the top of the box falls away, examining the contents. “ Oh my god!”

 

Agatha glances over, startled. “Whoa, whoa. What’s wrong?”

 

Billy grins- though he looks a little teary-eyed- and holds up a shiny metal chain. “It’s my mom’s locket. The one with the family picture in it.” He sets the necklace down and rifles through the rest of the objects, snuggled carefully into a nest of soft paper. “And look, my dad’s watch, and my brother’s tamagotchi, and… wow.”

 

Agatha gapes, exchanging a startled look with Jen. “How in the hell? I’ve been trying for years to get that shit back.”

 

“I know,” Jen replies, staring over the mysterious gifts. There’s a few more pieces- from where Agatha’s standing, she can see a keychain, a bracelet, and what maybe looks to be a bobblehead. Things she didn’t even know were in that goddamn impenetrable evidence locker. 

 

Jennifer shakes her head in disbelief.

 

“Alice, did you do this?”

 

“No,” Alice murmurs. But she looks less stunned than either of them, Agatha notices, more…amazed. “I…have no idea how this happened.” 

 

Agatha’s brow furrows in confusion, her head tilting in Alice’s direction. She’s lying- one barely even needs to know her to know that. She always hesitates when she bluffs, starts to open her mouth before she’s fully formulated the words and freezes like a deer in the headlights. She’s gotten better at covering it since they’d met, but not by much. 

 

Terrible liar, a voice in Agatha’s head whispers. She pushes it aside, pretending not to hear it for now. That’s one connection she’s not ready to make, no matter how obvious it is. 

 

 Billy shrugs, still beaming. “Well, I think the how doesn’t really matter. I’m gonna go show Lilia!”

 

He runs off with the box in his hands. Jen shakes her head, sitting back in her seat. “I’ll be damned. I wonder who sent those?”

 

Alice shrugs, scooping up the cards and shuffling them. “Like I said, no idea. Agatha, you wanna take his hand?”



Chapter End Notes

this got away from me SO FAST-

seriously y'all, in my original drafts, Alice and Rio were bonding over Alice bringing her a cupcake for her birthday. How we got here, I have literally no idea. Sorry if it got too dark or intense, but that was just kind of where the story led me.

Also, side note, just in case I need to clarify- this is NOT the start of a love triangle. Alice's thought process before kissing Rio on the cheek probably went something along the lines of:

1. This woman has no friends.
2. This woman needs a hug.
3. This woman will almost certainly maim me if I try and hug her.
4. If I kiss her on the cheek instead, I have a slightly higher chance of escaping with all my limbs intact.

(Rio does desperately need a hug, but they're not quite at that level of friendship yet.)

Finally, just as one last note- some of y'all might have noticed that I missed a few comments while replying to everybody's. If I didn't reply to yours or just replied with emojis, just know that this does NOT mean I don't love you- it's just getting increasingly hard to reply to everything without dropping spoilers. Love y'all!

Chapter 15

Chapter Summary

Agatha Harkness is slowly finding that she really, REALLY hates throw pillows.

Especially the ones in her therapist's office.

Meanwhile, Rio navigates the simultaneous joys and perils of having a friend who understands her a little more than she'd like.

Chapter Notes

Y'ALL. I AM NOT DEAD. THIS CHAPTER JUST TOOK FUCKING AGES.

In a twist I should have seen coming, these witches need so much therapy that it's actually increased my chapter count. Like I've already had to break this bit into two chapters and I might actually make it three. This one alone is literally twenty pages. 💀

Now this is the part where I remind you that I am neither a licensed psychiatrist nor a law enforcement professional. The therapy scenes are all based on my personal experience in therapy and with my own therapist. Others may have different approaches or different experiences. Also, though I did a fair amount of research while working on Rio and her career, I am (again) not a law enforcement professional, and have taken my fair share of creative license with writing an FBI agent. That being said...enjoy!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“You know what I’ve never understood? Throw pillows. Especially these weird froofy ones.” 

 

Agatha picks up one from next to her on the couch, tugging on the stringy tassel. “I mean, seriously, what’s the point of this? The texture’s awful, it’s too lumpy to sit on, and the pattern is hideous . Looks like something the witch who lives in my attic would wear.” 

 

She snorts at her own joke. At least she thinks she’s funny.

 

 Across the coffee table from her, the curly-haired woman with the notebook folds one leg under herself and tilts her head, pointedly not responding to the jab. Agatha grumbles quietly, dropping the pillow. 

 

“You’re no fun, you know that?”

 

“I do. Unfortunately, it’s part of my job.” The woman gives her a patient smile. “Now, let’s redirect a little, alright?”

 

“Fine.” Agatha flops over onto the couch, not bothering to correct the very unladylike way her legs splay out. “So, where do you wanna start, doc? My dead kid or my shit mother?”

 

The psychiatrist bounces her foot idly, a motion that reminds her far too much of her ex-wife. 

 

“Why do you think you’re so resistant to therapy, Agatha?”

 

She rolls her eyes. “What’re you talking about? I’m here, aren’t I?”

 

“Physically, sure. You definitely made it to my office, tacky furniture and all.” The doctor rests her chin on her hands. “But mentally, you’re more checked out than a library book.”

 

“That’s a stupid comparison,” Agatha mutters. 

 

“I know.”

 

“I don’t like you.” 

 

“You don’t have to.” She gives her another knowing, calm smile. “But we aren’t here for me, right?”

 

Agatha groans. “Right.”

 

“Good.” 

 

“I don’t like that either.” 

 

“I know.”

 

~

 

Much to her annoyance, the door jams behind her when she stalks into the house. Agatha growls, twists the handle, and slams it again.

 

“Ugh!”

 

Even without looking, she can tell Jen rolls her eyes. “Hello to you too, Agatha.”

 

“How was therapy?” Teen pipes up eagerly. “Did you like the doctor?”

 

“Oh, she was great, I’m sure,” Agatha replies sarcastically. “But her decor was so loud, I couldn’t understand a word she was saying. Why do throw pillows exist, anyways? And why do we have so many?”

 

She knocks the offending pillows onto the floor in one dramatic swoop. Alice- snuggled up on the beanbag under the window with Señor Scratchy- dodges one and tosses it back. 

 

“I thought her office was kind of cute. Better than some of the other psychiatrists I’ve seen in my life, anyways.”

 

“Says the woman who dresses like a Hot Topic reject on her days off,” Agatha snipes. “You’ll have to excuse me if I don’t exactly trust your taste.”

 

Alice gives her a dirty look, stroking the bunny’s fur. “Oh, so you’re in that kind of mood. Got it.” 

 

Agatha flips her the bird, then faceplants onto the couch. A set of footsteps pads in from the outside- Lilia’s, she’s guessing, since she’d seen the woman in one of her preferred chairs on the porch- and hovers next to her.

 

“Agatha.”

 

“Mmph.” 

 

Hands- ridiculously strong for her age- grasp her shoulders and forcibly turn her over. She grumbles and bats halfheartedly at Lilia, who rolls her eyes. 

 

“Stop that. Now, are you being unkind because you were actually uncomfortable with her? Or  is it because she was good at her job and you didn’t like that?”

 

“ Ugh .” 

 

“I’m going to take that as the latter, then.” Lilia lets her go and Agatha flips back over onto her side. “So did you book another session?”

 

“Ngh…”

 

“ Agatha Harkness.”

 

“Yes,” Agatha mutters. “I’m going back in two weeks. Happy?” 

 

Lilia pats her cheek. “Good girl. We’re all very proud of you.”

 

Agatha snorts. “What am I, a dog? You gonna give me a cookie for going to see a shrink?”

 

The older woman rolls her eyes again, thumping her lightly upside the head. “Well, maybe this woman can also help you learn to bite your tongue from time to time. Get off the couch and go call the ramen place, would you? And you owe Alice a thank you for helping you find a therapist.”

 

Agatha rolls over, giving her a halfhearted glare. “You’re taking this whole mom friend thing too seriously, you know that?”

 

“Oh, am I?” Lilia crooks an eyebrow at her, tossing her cardigan over one shoulder as she turns and heads out of the room. “You want to see too seriously, little girl? Keep pushing and I may just find it in myself to put you over my knee.”

 

From the other room, Jennifer laughs. “I would pay good money to see that.” 

 

“You would, ” Agatha shoots back. “Freak.” 

 

“Children present!” Alice reminds them. Teen cocks his head curiously. 

 

“Children, plural?”

 

Alice thinks it over, then covers Señor Scratchy’s ears. “The bunny counts.”

 

~

 

“I like the new place! Definitely an upgrade.”

 

 Rio snorts, setting the drink carrier down on the table. “You don’t have to be nice. Anything was an upgrade from that place.”

 

Alice chuckles, picking her tea up from the carrier and poking a hole through the lid with a straw. “Fair. But this one really is cute! And it has a pool, right?”
