“Sí.” Rio cocks her head curiously. “Actually, I’m not sure if I’m allowed to use the pool, since I’m technically not an actual tenant. But either way, thank you.”

 

At Alice’s suggestion, she’d reached out to a local landlord, who’d been having issues renting an apartment due to some…previous issues. Once she’d assured them she really didn’t mind the lingering smell of enzyme cleaners in the living room, they’d agreed that she could pay them their monthly rate for however long she needed to stay, so long as she promised not to bring work home with her (so to speak). The place is nothing fancy, but it’s furnished, and it’s a welcome upgrade from the motel she’d been staying in. At the very least, it has a decent hot water heater. 

 

Alice pulls her phone out of her jacket pocket as the federal agent settles across from her. “It’s no big deal. Besides, I owed you one, anyways.”

 

She spins the screen around and slides it across the table, grinning. Rio raises an eyebrow, picking it up. 

 

In the picture, Billy Maximoff sits with Jennifer Kale and Lilia Calderu, showing them a colorful little piece of plastic that hangs off of his wrist on a short chain. He’s practically glowing with joy, pointing to the pixels on the tiny screen as Lilia tilts her head in confusion. Jen- settled on Lilia’s other side, resting her chin on the older woman’s shoulder- holds a small green box in her lap. 

 

She smiles in spite of herself. There’s so much joy in that boy’s face, even after everything he’s been through.

 

When she looks up, Alice is grinning even wider. Rio forces her own smile away and shrugs casually, passing the phone back. “No idea what you’re talking about.”

 

Alice snorts. “Sure, sure. I didn’t tell anyone, though, in case you’re worried about that.” She pockets the phone with a playful roll of her eyes. “Not that anyone would have believed me. Pretty sure the rest of the our squad thinks that the chances of the precinct being run by aliens are higher than Agent Vidal having a soft spot.” 

 

Rio laughs, and her colleague laughs with her. They’ve developed an odd sense of comraderie, the pair of them- almost a tentative friendship, if she dares to say so herself. Alice is nice, genuinely nice , and she really does seem to enjoy Rio’s company. It’s a development she hadn’t predicted when she’d come, but a surprisingly welcome one nonetheless. 

 

She’d told Alice so the day after that night at the motel, in her usual blunt, completely socially unacceptable way. 

 

“ I like you. ”

 

The detective had taken it in stride, simply tilting her head.  “ Don’t look so surprised.”

 

“ I am. ” 

 

“ Gee, thanks.”

 

Rio had shrugged. “ I tend to be surprised when I like people. Expect the worst, be pleasantly surprised by the best. But you’re not half bad. ”

 

In the passenger seat beside her, Alice had laughed and crossed her arms. “ You either. ”

 

“You’re the only one who thinks so. ”

 

~

 

“Can I ask you something, Agatha?”

 

She chuckles, tossing one of those awful throw pillows in her hands. 

 

“Pretty sure you just did, Doc.” 

 

Her therapist pushes her glasses up on her nose and tilts her head in her direction. “Have you ever noticed that you have a tendency to be…adversarial in conversation?”

 

Agatha wrinkles her nose. “Excuse me?”

 

 “You like to fight.”

 

“Oh, no shit. ” She rolls her eyes, trying for a smirk. “Sorry, how many years did you go to school for that? Maybe wanna ask for a refund there.”

 

The psychiatrist raises an eyebrow. Agatha grumbles, slumping back over on the couch. 

 

“Okay, fine. What’s your point?”

 

The doctor rests her head on her fist, leaning on the arm of her own sofa. “Well, I’m just wondering how it affects your relationships. What do you think?”

 

“I-”

 

She starts to reply, then stops, clenching her fists around one of those stupid tassels. Her therapist cocks her head, listening patiently.

 

 “I think I want to talk about something else. Literally anything else.”

 

The doctor doesn’t press her, but still, her words refuse to leave her mind. She practically sleepwalks out of the office, ruminating on what she’d asked. 

 

How do you think it affects your relationships? Probably pretty badly, if you’re asking for my professional opinion, but I’m a sadist and want to hear you say it.

 

(Alright, so maybe those hadn’t been her exact words. She’d never been one to pay that much attention to those anyways.) 

 

She’s about halfway home when she has a better idea and whips the wheel around, heading to a different destination. The doorbell ( stupid, stupid doorbell- why the hell hadn’t Jen taken that down? ) chimes happily as she bustles into Kale Kare. Her old friend is busy charming some prospective clients- funny, she’s pretty sure at least one of the girls is in her Wednesday morning class- but her signature salesgirl smile slumps slightly the moment she spots Agatha waving jauntily at her.

 

“Hiya, hon! Ooh, sorry, don’t mean to interrrupt. I just wanted to know where you keep your jade eggs. I’m fresh out and my pelvic floor is all over the place. ” She pokes her head in between the young, boho-chic lesbians, grinning toothily at them. “You gals wanna go splitsies on a set? Sisterhood of the traveling kegels, anyone?”

 

Across the counter, Jennifer rolls her eyes. “ God , Agatha, I haven’t seen you since…breakfast this morning, I think. What could you possibly want from me that couldn’t wait a few hours until I came home?”

 

Agatha grits her teeth and reluctantly lets the show drop. “I need to talk to you, okay? Like…coming straight here from therapy because I don’t want the kid to hear kind of need to talk.”

 

Jen’s eyes soften just slightly. She sighs and gestures for one of her employees- a slight little blonde thing who Agatha’s fairly certain has at least a few raunchy fantasies involving the boss and the back room, judging from the way she comes running when Jen beckons- to take her place. 

 

“Ari, hon, can you handle the rest of this consultation for me?” 

 

The woman nods eagerly. “Of course, Miss Kale!”

 

Jen pats her arm in thanks, smiling warmly at her customers. “Ladies, I’m so sorry, but I have to step out for a moment. In the meantime, though, my associate here will take excellent care of you. She’s an expert in perfumes. Really, I don’t think I could be leaving you in better hands.” She winks. “Except for maybe mine, of course. But that’s just my opinion.”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes as the scent-seekers laugh and the blonde practically drools, delighted by Jen’s praise. If that girl had a tail, it would be wagging. Before she can chime in, however, Jennifer grabs her arm and steers her away into her office. 

 

“Okay, talk.”

 

Agatha sighs, flopping onto the couch across from her desk. “You still keep that bottle of cheap-ass red in here?”

 

“No,” Jen grumbles.  “I caught some of the underage weekend kids stealing from it and decided that’s a lawsuit I don’t need. Sorry, but we’ll have to do this sober.”

 

“Great,” Agatha mutters. She supposes getting drunk wasn’t the best idea- especially after where it had landed her last time- but she hates these emotional conversations, especially with present company. 

 

Aforementioned present company frowns at her. “So?”

 

Agatha grumbles under her breath, picking up one of Jen’s little throw cushions- seriously, were those things everywhere? - and tossing it a few times before reluctantly setting it down. 

 

“My therapist says I like to fight.”

 

Jen raises an eyebrow. “Well, duh. That’s like…half the reason we’re friends.”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes again. “Thanks. So helpful.”

 

The beauty guru cocks her head, hopping gracefully up to perch on the edge of her desk ( something she’d never permit from Agatha) . “I mean, that’s just a fact. You like to fight, I like to fight, we like to fight with each other, and it pretty much all boils down to our parents messing us up in the head when we were kids. Is that what’s got you all worked up?”

 

“Parents? I thought you were raised by a nanny- oh sorry, a governess. ”

 

Her old friend shoots her an annoyed glare. “Did you prove your therapist’s point like this in the session too? Or did you just save that energy for me?”

 

Agatha starts to snap back, then bites down on her lip to avoid giving Jen the satisfaction. She winces as she tastes iron and runs her tongue along the inside of her mouth to soothe it. 

 

“Okay, fine. Point is, the doc thinks it’s fucking up my relationships. As the only relationship I’ve had that I’ve actually managed to keep, I wanted your opinion.”



Jen’s eyebrows lift almost comically high on her forehead. After hesitating for a moment- no doubt waiting for Agatha to laugh and drop the punchline- she puffs out her cheeks in a slow and deliberate exhale, shaking her head. “Oh boy.”

 

Agatha groans, draping a hand over her eyes. “ Wow , okay. Guess that’s that answered.”

 

“Well, you’ve been better about it since Teen came around,” Jen offers carefully. She shifts side to side on her desk, no doubt choosing her words carefully. “But…yeah, I would say it’s messed up your relationships a bit.”

 

She purses her lips sympathetically as Agatha groans again. “Sorry, but it’s true. I mean, you remember your sorority? And that research job you got right after Nicky was born? And-”

 

“Point made!” Agatha snaps. “I completely fucked up my own friendships, work relationships, and my marriage. Got it.”

 

Jen frowns. “Agatha…”

 

“No, you’re being defensive,” Agatha snarks sarcastically. “That’s what you were going to say, right? That it’s all some messed up defense mechanism, or that I’m afraid of vulnerability, or whatever bullshit that your bougie, overpriced therapist told you that’s straight out of some awful Hallmark movie?”

 

The beauty guru crosses her arms. “You done?”

 

She sighs and bites back another retort. After a pause, Jen unfolds her arms. “Okay. Well, for starters, that was a weirdly self-aware little rant you just spouted. Not really sure what you needed me for, since you got there all on your own.” She taps her fingers along the polished wood of her desk.  “And I wasn’t going to say any of that, you know.”

 

Shit.  

 

Agatha wrinkles her nose, trying to act nonchalant. “Oops. So what were you going to say?”

 

Jen gives her an all-too-knowing look. “That I didn’t say anything about your marriage. You came up with that all on your own.”

 

Double shit.

 

Jen springs off of the desk, heading back out into her store. “Maybe bring that up to your therapist next time. Just a suggestion.”

 

Then she’s gone, leaving Agatha with nothing but those stupid throw pillows for company.

 

She’s really starting to hate those things.

 

~

 

Thankfully, when the message from her supervisor comes in, Alice is the one driving. As it is, she nearly swerves out of shock when Rio bursts out in a string of Spanish curses, cell phone falling from her hands in frustration.

 

“Whoa, whoa, whoa! What’s wrong?”

 

“I failed my psych eval,” Rio hisses. Alice’s eyebrows lift, but she doesn’t otherwise react- though she hastily does her best to look surprised when her colleague glances over at her. Rio rolls her eyes. 

 

“Please, don’t mask your shock on my behalf.”

 

“Sorry,” the detective murmurs sheepishly. “So what does that mean?”

 

Rio rubs her temples. “It’s not an official evaluation, so I’m not being taken off duty. But my division lead is strongly recommending I undergo some psychiatric treatment before my next official evaluation.”

 

Alice tilts her head at her. “So…you have to go to therapy.”

 

“Sí.” 

 

To her surprise, Alice giggles . Rio stares at her, dumbfounded. “Something funny?”

 

Her friend- she’s finally beginning to feel comfortable calling her that- shakes her head, trying and utterly failing to conceal her smile. “Nothing. It’s just…nothing.”

 

Rio crosses her arms, sitting straight back against her seat. “You’re being weird.”

 

“Am not.”

 

“We are two grown women and officers of the law, and that’s the best comeback you muster?”

 

Alice rolls her eyes good-naturedly. “I’m trying to concentrate on driving. Here, there’s a list of department-recommended psychologists in my bag. Why don’t you look some of those up?”

 

“And you have this why, exactly?” Rio lifts an eyebrow, fishing the paper out. 

 

“No reason.”

 

Rio frowns, but shrugs it off, retrieving her dropped cell phone in order to do as Alice had suggested. 

 

“No…no…no… definitely no. Who puts that on their business website?”

 

“Oh, that one? I keep telling the sergeant that she should take him off the list.” Alice shrugs. “But I guess he’s popular with the older guys.”

 

Rio makes a face. “ Eugh. ”

 

“Right?”

 

She shakes her head, returning to her research. “Hm. This one looks decent enough, but these photos of her office are awful . I mean, those throw pillows are hideous. ”

 

Alice blanches. “Oh, uh- maybe not that one.”

 

“Huh? Why?”

 

“No reason.”

 

“You are just the worst liar, you know that?”

~

 

“Have I ever told you that I was married?”

 

Her therapist- dressed in a blue and white skirt with a pattern that looks like it belongs on a teacup- crosses her ankles and leans forward. “Not explicitly, but I had an idea.” 

 

Agatha folds her arms over her chest. “You had an idea, huh? How’d you know that? What, are you a psychic?” She chuckles to herself. “A psychic psychologist. Now that’s an idea.” 

 

The doctor tilts her head, still giving her that infuriatingly calm smile. “Was that a question you’d like an actual answer to, or was that more of a gut response?”

 

Agatha groans. “Gut response.”

 

“Good. Now, did you want to talk about your marriage?” 

 

“ Want is a strong word,” Agatha mutters. She props her heels up on the doctor’s coffee table, glancing over for a reaction but getting none. Annoyed, she swings them back down and leans on her knees instead. “Frankly, it’s that mess that landed me here in the first place.”

 

She gives her therapist a brief overview, starting with the chaotic mess that had been the past few years, then- at her prompting- going back to the beginning, from meeting Rio in the choir to marrying her in a spur-of-the-moment handfasting ceremony in the woods right after she’d proposed. With no family to speak of between them, they’d quite literally tied the knot themselves. It had come out a bit clumsy (in fact, everything about their wedding had been a bit clumsy- Rio had planted her knee in a hole when she’d knelt down to propose and had briefly been unable to stand back up, while Agatha had slipped in the mud and fallen on her ass) but it had been beautiful all the same. They’d been so in love then that it hadn’t mattered. All that had mattered was being together. 

 

“We didn’t have much then, but we had each other,” Agatha mutters. She laughs bitterly. “It sounds so corny when I say it out loud, but it’s true. All we had was a car that was technically stolen, a cheap-ass apartment, and a whole lot of student debt. But I loved her so much that I didn’t care. I…don’t think I’ve ever loved anyone else quite that way. Except for…”

 

“For your son?” the doctor asks gently. Agatha swallows thickly, nodding. 

 

“Yeah. Except for him.”

 

There’s an ache in her heart, one that’s almost physical in nature. She rubs a hand over her pendant, pressing it against her chest in an unconscious motion. Sometimes she wishes she could push it right through her skin, absorb the last of her baby boy into her body and shelter him there again. 

 

“To be your mother is to gaze upon the truest manifestation of love, for that is what you are,” Agatha murmurs. Her psychiatrist tilts her head.

“Hm?”

 

“It’s from a poem- sort of a poem, anyways. Not one you’ve read, mind you. Rio wrote it for Nicky while I was...”

 

Sudden tears prickle at her eyes. She blinks them away as subtly as she can, but more pool in to take their place. “While I-”

 

She blinks again, and suddenly she’s fallen back twenty years. Her hand rests on her distended belly, listening to the soft squeak of her rocking chair as her sore heels gently push her back and forth.

 

 Rio sits at her feet, art supplies spread haphazardly around her in a rainbow of organized chaos. The setting sun shines off of her raven hair as she works, head bowed, but bowed in concentration (rather than the grief that would weigh it down in later years). As Agatha watches, she opens a box and gently lifts the delicate body of a butterfly from inside. Carefully, with a mother’s oh-so-tender hands, she pins those fragile wings to her canvas. It’s surrounded by a handful of small, shimmery freshwater pearls, and framed by a half-finished script written in swooping calligraphy.

 

Beautiful.

 

Rio glances back at her for approval. Agatha- too content to speak up in response- simply offers her a thumbs up. Her wife laughs and dips her fountain pen back in the shimmery metallic ink, murmuring her words out loud as she paints them onto the canvas. 

 

“I spoke no spell. I said no incantation. Yet you, mi hijo, are the most magical being I have ever laid eyes upon. Know that I love you with every step I take, with every breath I breathe, with every pulse of that black heart-” she glances up at Agatha and blows her a little kiss- “-that your mother brought to life.”

 

Agatha laughs. Inside of her abdomen, her son’s feet thump against her ribcage. He seems to like the sound of his mamí’s voice.

 

“The road of life is winding and wild,” Rio continues, returning to her work. “And you will stumble and lose your way. But remember this, my darling. No matter where you are, your mothers’ love will be there in your soul- for you, my son, were created from our love. No magic, no trickery. You were made-”

 

~

 

“...from Scratch,” Agatha finishes. She blinks her eyes once more, and she’s back in her therapist’s office, so many years from that day.

 

  Her wife is gone. Her son is dead. And here she sits, on the couch with the hideous throw pillows, freshly streaming eyes fixed on the box of tissues that the ever kind, infuriatingly patient doctor offers her. 

 

“That’s beautiful. Was that where his name came from?” 

 

She can’t catch her breath. That ache in her chest is growing stronger now, and she almost wonders if she’s having an actual heart attack. 

 

“I don’t want to talk about this anymore,” she chokes out. 

 

“Agatha-”

 

“ No!” She digs her fingers into her hair. “No, no, no. No, I’m done talking about this. I’m done talking about my son, I’m done talking about my wife, I’m done. I can’t do this. I can’t face this right now.”

 

The doc raises her hands placatingly. “Okay. I won’t force you, I promise. Can you take a few deep breaths for me?”

 

She does, letting sweet oxygen flow through her body. After a moment, she composes herself to wipe her eyes with the sleeve of her sweater. 

 

“She doesn’t matter anymore, anyways. As far as I’m concerned, she’s in the past.”

 

Convincing. She almost believes herself. 

 

Her therapist hums noncommittally and nods. “Okay. Let’s call it a little early so you can take a few minutes before you go home, alright? I’m going to have you come back in a week.”

 

Agatha wrinkles her nose as she grabs her bag. “I feel like being called in to see your therapist sooner isn’t a sign of progress.”

 

“Progress isn’t linear, Agatha,” the doctor replies, smoothing out her teacup-esque skirt. “Nor is it anywhere near as fast as we’d like. I’ll see you next week.”



~

 

“Higher, Mamí! Higher!”

 

Rio laughs, catching the rope swing in her hands and pulling it back again. “Mijo, any higher and you’ll be flying off into the sky!”

 

Nicky giggles, pumping his little legs. “I know! I wanna see the birds, Mamí! I wanna fly with them!”

 

She kisses his nose, pulling the swing higher. “Oh, alright. But hurry back, mi tesoro. Your mama and I will miss you too much.”

 

He giggles again and promises. With that, she hauls back and pushes the swing with all her might, listening to her son’s joyous laughter as he soars into the air. She squints against the afternoon sun, gazing at her baby boy as the light frames him. 

 

Soaring above her, glowing. Like an angel.

 

She doesn’t want to take her eyes off of him. She knows what’ll happen when she does. She’s had this dream before, had it a million times over, but it still hurts every time. 

 

Eventually, however, the sun burns too brightly. Her eyes slip closed, just for a second. A second too long.

 

When she opens her eyes, an empty swing falls back into her hands. She crumples to her knees as though it’s struck her in the stomach, beginning to weep softly. From behind her, a  familiar voice calls out to her. 

 

“Rio? Rio!”

 

There’s a hand on her shoulder, shaking her. She can’t look around, knowing what she’ll see. Accusing, pain-filled eyes. Begging her to do the impossible. Hating her for what she can’t. Or maybe that’s her own self-hatred, reflected back at her through those blue eyes.

 

The voice speaks again, but this time it’s different. Softer, kinder, maybe slightly higher.

 

 “Rio? Hey, you’re dreaming.” The hand shakes her again- more gently this time. “C’mon, wake up.”

 

The person isn’t Agatha. Confused out of her stupor, she turns to face her and-

 

 Suddenly, her eyes are open. Startled, Rio peels her face off of her keyboard- oh god, there’s indentations pressed into her cheek. How long was she out?- and blinks up at Alice. The latter woman smiles sheepishly down at her. 

 

“Sorry. I was gonna just let you sleep, but you started talking to yourself, and it kinda seemed like you were having a bad dream. Figured it was better to wake you up.”

 

Rio rubs her forehead, absorbing the embarassment. “ Ay, Santa Maria. We’ve reached that stage in the investigation already?”

 

Her partner laughs wryly. “I sort of just live in that stage. Can’t remember the last time I got more than three nights of decent sleep in a row.”

 

She crosses to the other side of the desk and begins gathering up her own things. “C’mon, we should call it for the night. I’ll drive you home, okay? No offense, but…” She glances Rio up and down, from her rumpled dress pants to the abandoned jacket crushed behind her on the chair. “You’re in no shape to be out there on that bike of yours.”

 

“I’m fine, ” Rio murmurs halfheartedly. The detective raises an eyebrow.

“Sure you are. By the way, you’ve got a little something…” She thumbs the corner of her own mouth. 

 

Rio frowns, standing with a stretch. “Huh?”

 

Alice cocks her head at the mirror across the room. Rio walks to it, examining herself. 

 

“Oh, damn it.” 

 

There’s lipstick smudged across the corners of her lips and onto her cheek- no doubt on her laptop as well. Had she actually been drooling? Probably. She’d been out cold, after all.

 

Alice taps her shoulder and offers her a tissue. Rio rolls her eyes and takes it, ignoring the detective’s smug little grin. 

 

“So…I think you’ll be taking that ride, yeah?”

 

“ Callate . Get your shit and let’s go.”

 

During the time they’ve been in the office, it’s started to rain again. Rio leans her cheek against the window of the car, watching fat droplets slide down the glass as Alice flips her headlights on and pulls out of the parking lot. She’s getting tired of the rain. 

 

The glow of the stoplight glints off rapidly forming puddles in the street, reflecting in a blur of color before their tires cut through it. Alice slows to a careful halt, rolling her head on her neck with a soft sigh once they’ve stopped.

 

“Hey, you went to see that counselor again today, right? How did that go?”

 

“Mmph.” 

 

Alice chuckles sympathetically, grabbing her water bottle from the cupholder. “Rough?”

 

“Just a little, yes.” A lock of her hair pinches in the seatbelt and she grumbles, sitting up to tug it loose. “I guess they weren’t that bad. But I don’t particularly like psychiatrists who brand themselves as law enforcement specialists.” 

 

She shakes her hair loose and rolls her eyes. “You know how they opened the session?”

 

“Hm?”

 

Rio rolls her eyes again, adopting a dramatic fake smile. “Did you know that it is a universally acknowledged truth that a lady cop cannot be good at her job and have a healthy personal life at the same time?”

 

Alice chokes. “ Seriously?! ”

 

“ Seriously,”   Rio drawls. “You should have seen their face when I told them I’m divorced.”

 

Alice shakes her head, looking half amused and half scandalized. “I’m sure that’s not what they meant.”

 

“No,” Rio replies, smile sliding from her face. “No, it wasn’t.”

 

She lets her head drop back against the window, staring into the streetlights. Alice glances over her shoulder at her as she turns the wheel. “Lack of tact aside…they have a point, don’t they?”

 

“Rude.” 

 

Alice sighs. “Sorry. But your job is kind of hard to leave at the office.”

 

Rio laughs dryly, leaning her cheek on her hand to take some pressure off her jaw. “I don’t trust anyone who says they can fully disassociate from the homicide unit. They’re either lying to you or themeselves, and that usually leads to a breakdown in the field. ” She hums. “That’s why I like working with you. You’re nice, but not so nice that you’re not honest.”

 

In the window, she can see Alice frown. “Honest, huh?”

 

“Mmhm.” She chuckles again. “What, have you been keeping secrets from me?”

 

“No. But that’s sort of a funny stance, you know, considering-” she pauses to flick her turn signal on. “Considering you’ve been keeping secrets from me since we met.” 

 

Rio winces internally, but keeps her face stoic for now. It’s not that she’s wrong, but she’d rather not admit it right away.

 

  “What makes you say that?” 

 

Alice rolls her eyes. “I didn’t become a detective for nothing. Try again.” 

 

Rio purses her lips. “Okay. What exactly do you think you caught me lying about, hm?”

 

“What do you think?”

 

There’s a brief, charged pause in the conversation, leaving them trapped in an awkward stalemate. After a moment, however, Rio laughs quietly. Her partner raises an eyebrow at her. 

 

“What’s so funny?”

 

She rolls her eyes, grinning in spite of herself. “The fact that this conversation started with us talking about work-life balance, and yet we’re sitting here using basic interrogation techniques on each other. There’s something ironic about that, no?”

 

Alice bites her lip stubbornly, but lets a smile tug at the corners of her mouth after a minute. “Okay, fine. That is pretty funny.” She sigh, shaking her head again. “Okay, I’ll give.” 

 

She turns the wheel, letting it spin back under her fingers. Not strictly legal, but neither of them are traffic cops, are they?

 

  “I know you’re straying out of your department on this case. Honestly, I’m not sure how you expected to keep that from me.”

 

Rio exhales softly. Damn. She’d suspected as much, but had hoped in spite of herself.  “I didn’t. Anyone halfway decent at your job would have caught on. I just didn’t bank on it mattering to you.”

 

Alice crooks an eyebrow. “Really? I thought you knew all about me before you came.”

 

Rio shakes her head, smiling wanly. “No. I knew what I could see in your personnel file, nothing more. I had no idea you had any connection to my wi- ex-wife until I met you in her kitchen.” She spreads her hands upon receiving a skeptical look. “Look, I may be a little bit lovestruck, but I’m no fool. I wouldn’t have intentionally gone into this with you if I knew that would be hanging in the air between us.”

 

Alice sighs, clearly not convinced but choosing to abandon the issue. “Okay, whatever. The point is that I know you don’t work homicide anymore. You haven’t worked homicide in years.”

 

Rio closes her eyes. In her mind, an image of a little girl flashes past. A girl with beaded braids, who’d loved fairies and the rain.  

 

“No, I haven’t. Your point?”

 

Alice glances over at her. Through the corner of her eyes, Rio can see that same kind, concerned look- the one that nearly made her buckle under the overwhelming pressure of having someone care about her. “Look, this might be out of line, and it’s definitely out of my expertise, but…you don’t sometimes wonder if working in child abductions isn’t good for you?”

 

“It’s not good for anyone, ” she mutters. “But someone has to.” 

 

(Because that little girl had never found her fairies, in the end. But some did, didn’t they? She’d had a case a few years ago like that- a little boy with autism, who’d been terrified of the boogeyman. His father- a single man whose child had been his whole world- had made him a little bottle of lavender infusion to carry around with him, just to make him feel better. Monster spray , he’d called it. Melts the monsters away. 

 

A week or so after he’d told her that, Rio had arrested a man who smelled of lavender.)

 

“Someone has to, yeah,” Alice replies. “But how long has it been you?” 

 

She doesn’t answer. Alice purses her lips, trying another tactic. “How many have you lost, Agent?”

 

Rio rubs her forehead, reluctant to answer. Her partner, however, simply waits patiently, and she gives in after a moment.

 

 “...two hundred and seventy-nine.”

 

Alice nods quietly. “And how many of those were stranger abductions? Or custody disputes?”

 

Rio leans her head back against the seat, staring up at the sky through the windshield. There’s a moment of silence, only filled by the click of the turn signal. 

 

The car hums under her. Outside, the world turns. 

 

Alice pulls into the parking lot of her apartment building and switches the car out of drive. With her attention no longer occupied by the road, she turns to face Rio. 

 

“Look, this obviously is a conversation that should happen in therapy. But…it seems to me like this job isn’t exactly helping you heal, is it?”

 

Rio doesn’t answer her. Alice sighs, chewing on her lip. 

 

“Did you know my mom’s band had a reunion tour ten years ago?”

 

Rio blinks. “¿Que?”

 

“Just answer the question.”

 

She raises an eyebrow. “Yes, I did.” 

 

Alice sighs again, leaning her chin on her hand. “Okay. Well, they invited me to join them. At least just to travel with them, even if I didn’t want to perform.” 

 

She glances over at Rio. “Do you know why I didn’t go?”

 

“Because you had a life?”

 

Alice rolls her eyes minutely. “Okay, that too. But I also didn’t go because the last time I traveled with them was when I lost my mom.”

 

Rio turns to look her in the eyes. Alice chews on her lip, fidgeting with her sleeve. “If I had gone, sure, it would have made a lot of people happy. I do love my mom’s bandmates, they played a huge part in raising me. And her fans would have gone absolutely nuts.” 

 

She picks a string loose from the seam at her wrist and flicks it aside. “But I also knew that going on that tour would have reopened so many old wounds- the kind that would have bled me dry if I’d let them. If I put myself in a situation where I was reliving my worst memories every night, I would never have healed from them. So I stayed behind.” 

 

She cocks her head at Rio. “Do you get why I’m telling you this?”

 

“No.”

 

Alice shoots her an annoyed look. Rio laughs, opening her door. “Lo siento. Yes, I get the sentiment.” 

 

She gets out of the car and gives her a little wave. “You don’t need to worry about me so much, Detective. But I appreciate it nonetheless. Te veo, my friend. Get home safe.”

 

~



Chapter End Notes

I'm really sore and I can't think of any witty chapter notes (I was going to write about the case Rio and Alice are working, but I don't have the brainpower). So have some fun facts about Señor Scratchy and his bond with the coven instead.

- After Agatha, his favorites in the house go in this order:

1. Billy
2. Alice
3. Lilia
4. Jen

Agatha thinks this is hilarious.

- Teen and Jen once had a competition over who could get more likes on a post (Jen's Kale Kare account or Billy's Señor Scratchy fanpage). Billy and the rabbit won, and Jennifer is still salty about it to this day.

- Alice regularly gets in trouble for feeding the bunny from the table (she likes to nibble on veggie trays when she's working and Señor Scratchy is constantly harassing her to share her snacks). Agatha claims she's the reason he's such a beggar.

- Scratches, for some inexplicable reason, is OBSESSED with Lilia's meditation chanting. He'll happily sit and stare at her for upwards of an hour at a time, just...enraptured. Even Billy admits it's a teeny tiny bit odd, but it makes Lilia incredibly happy.

- The bunny has a weird sense of who in the household is sick, sad, or just had a bad day. He'll hop up and demand attention from whoever he deems upset, and won't leave them alone until they pet him. Alice swears up and down that he once predicted a migraine attack coming on and preemptively came to sit in her lap (no one but Lilia believes her on this)

- Sharon occasionally comes over to share some of the veggies she's grown for her goat. Señor Scratchy gets INCREDIBLY EXCITED to see her coming from the window (he has a little basket that Teen made him).

- Most of the time, Señor Scratchy sleeps in Agatha's bed. At least one night a week, however, he goes and sleeps on Teen's pillow.

And that's a wrap for today! With the holiday season coming up, I'm pretty damn busy rn, so please be patient with me as I work on getting the last of this story finalized and released :) . But we're coming up on my favorite storyline, so keep your eyes peeled! :) :)

Chapter 16: Christmas Special

Chapter Notes

I've gotta stop writing these holiday episodes at 3am before I have work. This may be awful and I will no doubt be exhausted tomorrow! Merry Christmas to those who celebrate!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

December 24th, 2015. 

 

This is a mistake. One shitty, gigantic mistake. 

 

No, actually, it’s a whole series of mistakes that she’d made. At the moment, however, the biggest and most pressing of them all is that Agatha’s managed to be out shopping for children’s toys on Christmas Eve. 

 

Rookie-goddamn-mistake.

 

“ Come to Chrismas dinner, Agatha, please, ” she mutters to herself, doing what she thinks is a fairly passable imitation of Wanda Maximoff. “ No one should be spending it alone! Come be with us! Why in the hell did I say yes to that, huh?”

 

She picks up an incredibly creepy looking doll with big, hyper-realistic eyes and wrinkles her nose. “ Ugh. That looks like it’ll kill you in your sleep. Pass.” She briefly considers its partner- a puppy toy that promises real tail-wagging action - but leaves it be. In all the times she’s babysat the twins (which, despite her best efforts, are rapidly increasing in number), she’s maybe seen four or five stuffed animals between them. They’ll probably be far more  interested in something with mechanics and speakers and flashing lights. In other words, something decidedly more expensive.

 

Ugh. 

 

“Why am I even getting Christmas gifts for these kids, anyways?”, she grumbles, not bothering to watch her volume this time. “Aren’t they Jewish? Little shits are double-dipping in holidays here.” 

 

She gets a dirty look from another last minute shopper and ignores it, instead picking up a Lego set and examining it. Certainly large enough to entertain the twins for a good, long time, and with more than enough pieces to maintain her status as a certified pain in Vision’s ass. (Against her better judgement, she’s grown rather fond of Wanda. Her husband, on the other hand? Not so much.) She has an idea, however, that the man might just suggest that the boys ask Auntie Agatha to build it with them- and that idea, she’s not a fan of. She’s never liked Legos. 

 

(Nicky had. And she doesn’t like to think about that.) 

 

She rolls her eyes and crosses another aisle over. This one is more promising, full of flashy-looking toy cars. Tommy, at least, might like them. Billy is trickier, but she’ll cross that bridge when she comes to it. 

 

Not for the first time since she’s gotten here, Agatha kicks herself for not thinking of it sooner. She’s been invited to Christmas dinner with her neighbors and their two little kids, of course she needs to bring gifts. Had it just been Wanda and her man, she might have been able to get away without them. But with the boys- who absolutely adore her, for reasons she can’t quite fathom- there’s absolutely no way. 

 

The parents had been easy enough to shop for (a nice bottle of wine would do the trick, and she’d probably end up drinking it one way or another). Billy and Tommy, however, have been another story entirely. The boys have completely opposing tastes and the toy aisle is totally picked over by now. Not a great combination in the slightest. 

 

Agatha picks up a firetruck and turns it in her hands. Another one that Tommy would love, but not Billy. Before she can set it down, however, one next to it catches her eye. Holding the firetruck in her free hand, she picks up the toy. Though there are others like it, the cop car is unique in that the sirens are on the inside . 

 

An unmarked car. Like the one that had so often been parked in her driveway. 

 

She examines the car more closely. Like the firetruck, there’s a little figurine included. This one is dressed in a prim little suit and stands with a hand on its hip. There’s a cocky grin on its paintedface, one that makes her smile a little. It’s familiar, in a way, and makes her heart warm in spite of herself. 



That warmth lasts all of ten seconds, until she looks more closely at the box. Behind the proud miniature officer, there’s a cartoony painting in the background. It’s nothing too detailed- certainly nothing to frame on the fridge-but it’s there, and now that she’s seen it, she can’t look away.

 

Inside the box, there’s a perfect little house, complete with a white picket fence and a cement stoop- two steps, only two- leading to a red front door. And leaning out that door- held carefully ajar by his mother, who leans around him- is a boy. 

 

The boy is waving, waving his parent goodbye. His mother smiles so happily . Even with such little detail to her face, she looks so goddamn happy.

 

Agatha’s knees go weak. The toy cars go clattering from her hands as she sits down hard in the aisle, pressing the heels of the hands to her forehead. 

 

She’d thought she was past this stage. It’s the only goddamn reason she’d agreed to go to the Maximoff’s Christmas dinner. But that unmarked car, that happy family, that red front door , and what little progress she thought she’d made goes out the fucking window. 

 

A snatch of a song plays in her head- where is it from? The voice is female, that much she remembers. But all she knows for sure is that reminds her of the holidays, and it makes every wound in her already scarred heart burst open anew. 

 

She grabs her phone out of her pocket and dials a familiar number. It rings once, then goes straight to voicemail. Agatha had known it would, of course. The owner of the number had blocked her months ago, after an explosive argument which she thinks may have ended one of the longest friendships of her life.

 

“ I can’t fucking do this anymore, Agatha! I can’t keep helping you pick up the pieces of your life if you just keep shattering them again! Sooner or later, my own hands are going to start bleeding, and I don’t intend to stick around for that long. ”

 

“What, did your fancy-ass therapist tell you that?” Agatha had retorted, and the look on Jen’s face had been a kind of anger she’d never seen before. She’d thought for a moment that the younger woman was actually going to hit her, and Jen’s little friend (Annie or Ali or something- Agatha knows she ought to know her name by now, but hasn’t quite gotten a read on whether she’ll be sticking around for long enough to bother.) had clearly made a similar assumption. Agatha remembered vaguely that the girl was some kind of law enforcement, and she’d certainly jumped between them accordingly. Only an honest-to-god riot policewoman could break up a fight between Jennifer Kale and Agatha Harkness. The thought makes her snort, even only just a little. 

 

Jen won’t be returning her voicemail, of course. She hasn’t since that fight back, all the way back in October. Probably shacked up with that pretty cop of hers . Agatha leaves one anyways.  Frankly, she’s not entirely sure that anything coherent rolls off of her tongue- all that she hears is a whole mishmash of words that bubbles out of her throat like vomit- but it feels good to say something nonetheless. Get it out of her system, anyways. Sort of like screaming into a pillow.

 

Agatha calms down after a minute, tells Jen that she’s a bitch but that she still hopes she’ll call her back one of these days, and hangs up. She waves off the store clerk that’s come by to ask if she’s alright or if she needs him to call anyone with a scoff and a muttered, “I’m leaving, I’m leaving.”

 

Nothing worth buying here, anyways. She has another idea.

 

~

 

Agatha comes within an inch of cancelling on her neighbors. There’s only two things that really stop her, in the end. A pair of small faces that she can plainly see peeking through the front window from time to time - she only lives a couple houses down, but the boys still await her arrival like she’s some long-lost relative- and the yappy little surprise that’s currently getting fluff all over her living room. Setting those hopeful smiles aside (she lies to herself that she might be able to ignore them if she tried), she’s not exactly sure what she’d do with that if she cancelled. So here she is, prompt on time at five o’clock in the evening, with a bottle of wine in one hand and trouble in the other. 

 

Wanda laughs when she opens the door, though she hastily covers it by pursing her lips in a poor imitation of someone disapproving. “Oh, you are bad. You are so bad.”

 

Agatha cocks her head and grins. “Well, I figured the rugrats got enough toys from Santa. Wanted to be unique and all.” 

 

Wanda rolls her eyes at her, but bends to let the puppy Agatha’s holding lick at her face. “Ooh, look at you…” She clucks her tongue. “Oh, Agatha. Vision’s going to kill us both .”

 

Agatha chuckles. “Then let’s show the boys before he sees, shall we?”

 

As she’d expected, there’s a delighted chorus of squealing when she enters the living room. Tommy goes straight for the puppy now held by his mother, but Billy- though clearly just as eager- stops briefly to hug Agatha first. She hugs him back, slightly surprised. 

 

“Hey, kiddo. Merry Christmas.”

 

The young boy lifts his head from her middle. “Merry Christmas, Agatha!” He pauses a moment, then adds, “I’m glad you’re here.”

 

She blinks, lost for a response. Billy lets go of her and runs off to join his brother, and her friend walks over to put a hand on her shoulder. 

 

“Agatha? Are you okay?”

 

There’s tears in her eyes, she realizes, ones that aren’t quite falling yet but are no doubt the source of Wanda’s concern. She wipes them away hastily with the back of her hand. “Oh, I’m fine. Just allergic to…to…”

 

To the innocent kindness of a child who doesn’t yet understand heartbreak? To being shown love by a little boy who reminds her far too much of the one she’d lost?

 

Yes to all of the above. But of course, she can’t say any of that.

 

She glances around for a passable excuse, but finds none. The room is beautifully decorated, looking like something out of a multicultural crosstitch. The menorah is still perched in its place of honor above the fireplace, but colorful lights from the Christmas tree glint off the polished gold. She vaguely remembers Wanda explaining to her why her family celebrates both- something about falling in love with the holiday through watching American sitcoms- but regardless, she’s done a good job marrying the two into one pretty scene in her living room. Agatha turns back to her, shrugging.

 

“To…bad excuses?”

 

Wanda laughs softly, but squeezes her shoulder. “Okay, but are you alright?”

 

Agatha sighs, chewing on her lip. For a moment, she considers confiding in Wanda. She glances over the boys, however- now giggling to their father to come see what Auntie Aggie brought them- and forces a smile instead. 

 

She’s happy. May as well avoid ruining that. 

 

“Just my heart growing four sizes, or some shit like that. You need help in the kitchen?”

 

~

 

Her heart certainly does not gow four sizes that day. But by the end of it, she’s willing to admit that it’s opened up just the smallest bit. It’s nice to sit around a table and laugh again, nice to eat a home-cooked meal ( she herself is embarassingly hopeless in the kitchen ), nice to spend time with those annoyingly cute little kids. By seven thirty, they’re piled over her. Tommy’s showing her the firetruck he’d unwrapped that morning ( she thinks to herself that she’s fairly glad she hadn’t gotten him that one ) and Billy is curled up in her lap with his new little friend. Fast asleep, of course. It’s been a very long day for them both.

 

It’s no miracle cure, of course, but it’s still nice.

 

It’s there, sitting with the twins, that she remembers the the snippet of song that had been nagging her the day before. All of a sudden, she realizes where she remembers it from. It’s a movie, released in 2003? Maybe 2004? Either way, she remembers because Rio had loved it. A musical adaptation of A Christmas Carol, a story her ex-wife adored- even more so when it had been put to song. She’d flopped across Agatha’s lap after Nicky had gone to bed, and they’d watched actors prance across the screen in frilly ballgowns and poorly disguised tap shoes. 

 

The actress who’d sung the song she’s thinking of- the girl from Ghost Whisperer, she remembers, though only because Rio had hated that show- had had her hair arranged in an absolutely awful updo, but that hadn’t been the part that had mattered. What had mattered had been the song she’d sung, had been the words that had passed her lips. 

 

There's a place called home,

I can almost see,

With a red front door and a roaring fire,

And a Christmas tree.

 

A place called home. She’d had one of those. It had broken, broken with the loss of her son. But for tonight, she’s been granted asylum in one that belonged to another family. And for just tonight, she lets herself be enveloped in it.

 

It’s not much, but it’s love of some kind. And for awhile, just a short while, she’s happy.

 

It's a place called home,

Full of love and family. 

 

And I'm there at the door,

Watching you come home to me.

 



Chapter End Notes

There's a place called home,

I can almost see,

With a red front door and a roaring fire,

And a Christmas tree.

It's a place called home,

Full of love and family.

And I'm there at the door,

Watching you come home to me.

Through the years,

I'll recall this day,

In your arms,

When I finally found my way.

To a place called home,

And a life with you.

Where the days are long and the love is strong,

And the dreams are true,

Just a place called home,

You and I will always see.

In the dark of the night,

Let your heart come home to me..

Chapter 17

Chapter Summary

In which Alice uncovers some uncomfortable truths, and our favorite lesbians spend some more time in therapy.

Chapter Notes

Reminder again that I am neither a trained psychiatrist or a law enforcement professional!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

She’d always had a decent sense for when someone’s watching her. Some of it came from her years as a cop- probably most of it, if she’s honest. But at least some came from a childhood spent at her mother’s leather-clad knee, dodging strangers who were sure that they were special, that they loved her more than anything else. 

 

A few particularly pushy ones had tried to give her the same attention. One woman- probably high on something, if Alice stops to think about it- had even given her a flower. That was the one and only time she’d ever seen her mother- a drunk, sure, but not an angry one, never an angry one. That was something the tabloids and the podcasters and the conspiracy theorists got wrong every time- lash out at a fan. 

 

Not my daughter! Never my daughter! 

 

Always her protector. 

 

Regardless of how, she feels Jen’s eyes on her before she even clears the coatroom. Her glare practically burns a hole in the back of her head as she strips her rain-soaked coat and shakes out her boots. She still makes a halfhearted attempt at a peaceful conversation, however.

 

“Hey. Sorry I’m late.”

 

Jennifer doesn’t answer. When she turns to look, Alice finds her giving her an accusatory stare- the effect of which is slightly lessened by the shimmery, gooey face mask she’s painted along her cheeks.

 

“You were with her, weren’t you?”

 

Alice snorts in spite of herself. “You make me sound like a cheating husband or something. I was at work, Jen, of course I was with her.”

 

Jen doesn’t laugh. Alice sighs, leaning against the hallway wall. “Okay, what’re you thinking right now? Because I feel like you’re getting more upset with me by the minute, and I want to know why before I try and defend myself.” 

 

Jen crosses her arms. “You want to go straight to the point? Okay.” She glares at Alice. “Are you sleeping with her?”

 

Alice does a double take. “Excuse me?”

 

“You heard me,” the beauty guru snaps. “Are. you. Sleeping with her? ”

 

“No!” Alice splutters. “Wha- no! Even if I wanted to- and I don’t- we’re coworkers! Do you have any idea how much trouble that would get me in?!”

 

Jen rolls her eyes. “Oh, please. People sleep with their coworkers all the time.”

 

“Not their coworkers who are visiting from the FBI!” Alice protests. “And even if I could, do you seriously think I’m the kind of person to do that?”

 

Jen purses her lips. “I think I’ve seen a lot of people do a lot of stupid things I never thought they would for Rio Vidal.”

 

Alice shakes her head in disbelief, turning and heading into the hall bathroom to brush out her hair. “Well, I’m not sleeping with her. So you can find some other reason to be angry at her tonight.”

 

Jen stands from the counter and follows her, clearly still searching for a fight. Why , Alice can’t understand. Maybe lingering resentment, sure ( or maybe jealousy, judging from her tone- but she’s really not in the headspace to think about that possibility right now) , but sometimes she honestly just wonders if Jen and Agatha both enjoy having something to be angry about. She finds herself thinking it’s a shame Agatha’s out having drinks with coworkers tonight, because it would have been nice to redirect their energy at each other. Like a cannon. Or a bomb. Something explosive. 

 

Then she feels guilty for thinking that. 

 

Jennifer cocks her head at her, giving her an accusing look. “Okay, you aren’t sleeping with her. So are you spending so much time with her lately?”

 

“For the third time in this conversation, ” Alice replies (finding herself dangerously close to losing her own cool). “We are working together. It’s my job to be spending time with her right now.” She throws her hands up a little. “The government is literally paying us to spend time together!"

 

“The state isn’t paying you to have bubble tea with her,” Jen snipes. Alice groans, flicking the hot water tap open. 

 

“We’ve spent pretty much every work day together for months. It’s not that crazy that we’re friends.” 

 

Jen rolls her eyes, hopping up onto the counter and perching daintily next to her. “Speak for yourself. Me personally, I can’t believe you’re friends with that woman.”

 

“And I can’t believe how much time you’re spending thinking about that!” Alice snaps. “For god’s sake, Jen, you’re almost as bad as Agatha. Why can’t you just let it go?” 

 

Jen glares at her. “No one here is going to be able to let it go until Rio is gone . Unless you plan on keeping in touch with your new friend , I suppose.”

 

She laughs, and Alice hates it. It’s her camera laugh- one that sounds pretty and clear, but not too loud. Perfect for strangers on the other side of the screen, the ones whose opinions make up half of Jen’s job description. But to Alice, who knows her- Alice, who’s rubbed her back when she was sick, who’s held her when she cried, who sleeps on that awful plastic pillow when nightmares take over and holds the hand that she dangles off the side of the bed- it sounds like something twisted and venemous. There’s no mirth in that laugh, and it’s nothing like her real one. 

 

Not her Jen.

 

She rubs her forehead, pausing a moment to calm down. Turning from Jen, she runs her wrist under the tap quickly to check the temperature, then splashes some water onto her face and hair- taking her time with it, and pointedly ignoring the heat of Jen’s eyes in her reflection. 

 

“I just wish you’d leave it alone,” she finally murmurs. “You don’t have to like that we’re friendly, but we also don’t have to fight about it. We don’t even have to talk about it.”

 

“But we do! ” Jen snaps. “Because even if we don’t talk about it, we all still know that you’re walking around all chummy with Agatha’s ex-wife! After everything that bitch-”

 

Alice drops her hands onto the counter, patience officially exhausted. “ Stop. Calling her that.”

 

Jen crosses her arms. “Are you seriously defending her right now?”

 

“No!” Alice snaps. “Maybe. I- I don’t know.”

 

She pinches the bridge of her nose. “Look, is there something I’m missing here? Was she- I don’t know, abusive? Did she hit Agatha or something?”

 

Jen frowns. “No.”

 

“Was she emotionally abusive?”

 

“No, but…”

 

“But what, Jen?” Alice folds her own arms, slumping against the wall. The light switch digs uncomfortably into the back of her neck and she straightens up almost as quickly as she leans over. “Tell me why you hate her so much! Do you have a reason, or is it just because she’s your best friend’s ex and- don’t even start about Agatha not being your best friend, that is so not my point- because she’s your best friend’s ex and it’s your job to find reasons hate her? Because that’s sort of the idea I’m getting right now!”

 

Jennifer leaps down from the counter, beginning to pace. “Because I was there when they broke up, Alice! I was there when they buried their son, I was there when they started fighting all the time, and I was there when Agatha tried to jump off of a fucking bridge!”

 

She points an accusatory finger at Alice. “And you were there for that last one, too. You know damn well that Rio wasn’t. But if she had just been there for Agatha, none of us would have been. And none of us would have still been dealing with their shit now, nearly twenty years later!”

 

“Agatha, who fights like a feral cat whenever anyone tries to care about her?” Alice retorts. “Agatha, who’s been bitching nonstop about going to therapy and threw a knife at my head when I asked if it might actually be helping her feel better? Are we talking about the same Agatha here? I’m not saying it’s her fault, so don’t start on me there, but I can sort of see how being married to her in the wake of a massive tragedy might not be easy!”

 

“It was a plastic knife,” Jen snarls. “And are you seriously taking Rio’s side?”

 

“No!” Alice exclaims. “I’m saying that you’re forgetting that there are two sides here. And of course we’re on Agatha’s side, because she’s our person and we love her.  But just- just think about one thing for me, okay? Before you start yelling at me again.”

 

“ What? ” Jen hisses. Alice rubs her forehead again. The headache that had finally begun to ebb earlier- not quite a migraine, but could certainly develop into one if she wasn’t careful- is back in full force.

 

“Jen, do you think…look, you’ve been Agatha’s friend a long time, I get it. But if you weren’t her friend first, if you met her through Rio and not the other way around, would you still be on her side?”

 

Jen opens her mouth to snap back at her, and Alice has a brief moment where she wonders if they really are going to escalate to a full-blown shouting match. By now, she’s certainly considering it. 

 

Then Jen pauses and purses her lips. She doesn’t offer any verbal response, but Alice knows she’s gotten her attention. When the silence drags on, she sighs and unfolds her arms, turning to head out of the bathroom. 

 

“Look, I…I don’t want to fight about this, J. I hate fighting with you in the first place, and this is a stupid thing to fight about. I’m just- gonna go watch TV and cool down a bit, okay? You can come sit with me if you want, but you don’t have to.”

 

She doesn’t look over her shoulder as she leaves, not sure she wants to see Jen’s expression. The kitchen is quiet when she enters- Agatha is still out, while Teen and Lilia are almost certainly both in bed. She hopes so, anyways- she’s realizing rapidly that she and Jen have both completely failed at keeping their voices down. 

 

Alice ponders whether or not to simply grab a bag of chocolate pretzels from the cabinet and call it good, but decides against it. Tonight is a popcorn and slippers night if there ever was one. She hovers for a moment over the little pre-buttered, microwaveable sachets, but sighs and grabs the bottle of kernels instead. 

 

The things she does for her people.

 

She gets about halfway through her episode of Bones (she’d started rewatching it with Teen lately and had found herself on somewhat of a kick) before footsteps quietly approach behind her. Alice pauses the show, but keeps her eyes on the screen until a weight sinks into the couch next to her. Then, she turns to look at Jen. 

 

“Hey.”

 

“Hey,” Jen replies quietly. She shuffles awkwardly in her seat, for once seemingly unsure of what to do with herself. Though Alice- still simmering in spite of her best efforts- briefly considers keeping her distance, she eventually shifts the popcorn to the other side of her lap and opens an arm to her. 

 

She’s never been one to hold a grudge.

 

Jen sighs in relief and curls into her, leaning her head back against Alice’s shoulder. She props her ankles up on the coffee table and Alice mimics her, stretching out more comfortably. Once they’re settled, she wraps her arm around Jen and unpauses the television. 

 

Onscreen, two male scientists are dangling a large frozen turkey from an apparently careful measured height. Jen cocks her head, looking half curious and half exasperated. 

 

“Um. Why?”

 

Alice starts to answer, then pauses. “I…don’t actually remember. I wasn’t paying a ton of attention.”

 

Jen wrinkles her nose. “That’s going to go so wrong. I bet someone gets impaled by a shard of raw turkey.” She shudders. “Gross.”

 

Alice chuckles softly. “Nah, I bet it’ll bounce.”

 

Jen snorts and starts to retort. Before she can, however, the onscreen lab rats drop the turkey. Sure enough, the bird carcass hits the tile and bounces like an overinflated basketball, striking the team’s forensic artist right in her pretty face. 

 

Jen blinks and glances over at Alice. 

 

“Wait, how did you know that?” 

 

Alice grins at her. “This show’s been out since the early 2000s, Jen, I’ve totally seen this episode before. I may not remember why they dropped the turkey, but I definitely remembered the aftermath.”

 

She giggles when Jen swats her thigh, hastily picking up the popcorn before it can spill. The beauty guru starts to reach for the bowl, then pauses. “Is this-”

 

“Sea salt and olive oil,” Alice finishes, hold the snack out to her with a knowing little smirk. “And I popped it in coconut oil. I know you hate movie theater butter.” 

 

“ Fake butter,” Jen corrects, but she’s smiling a little too warmly for Alice to take her seriously. She takes a handful and nibbles on it, purring contentedly. “Mmm. Perfect.”

 

Alice laughs. “Wow. Real high praise, coming from you. You must be in a better mood.” 

 

“Only for you,” Jen replies. She slides down and tucks her head under Alice’s chin, chewing on her lip. 

 

“...I’m sorry about earlier.”

 

“Me too,” Alice murmurs. “I meant it when I said that this is a stupid thing to fight about. If we argued about every conflict Agatha had in her life, we’d never have made it this far.”

 

Jen chuckles wryly, but her eyes are far away. Alice squeezes her gently. “Jen?”

 

“I’m not answering that question you asked me,” Jen mutters. “Back in the bathroom. I’ll leave it alone, but I’m not answering that.” 

 

Alice nods silently. She doesn’t push for an answer. She doesn’t need one. The hesitation is answer enough.

 

~

 

Rio rubs her forehead, doing her best not to glare. She’s trying at this whole counselling thing. Honestly, she is. Her therapist, however, is not making it easy in the slightest.

 

“Will you stop looking at me like that?” she mutters after a moment. The psychiatrist raises their hands placatingly.

 

“Sorry. Not doing it intentionally.”

 

Their brow remains furrowed, however, and there’s that same nervous look in their eyes. Rio rolls her eyes. 

 

“You’re looking at me like I just confessed to being a psycho killer. It was a little bit of light stalking at most. ” 

 

“Well, I do think it’s a bit concerning how lightly you take stalking,” her therapist replies cautiously. “You are in the FBI, Agent. I don’t think I need to spell out for you why I’m a bit troubled.”

 

She’s going to fire this therapist. Right as soon as their session ends, she decides. For now, she simply rolls her eyes. 

 

“If my wife wanted to press charges, she could have easily had me arrested. She lives with a detective, you know.” She shrugs innocently. “Alice was literally a stone’s throw away, but Agatha didn’t yell for her. That tell you something, doctor?”

 

The doc nods, but they’re frowning again. Rio crosses her arms. 

 

“ Now what?”

 

Her therapist takes a breath. They look young , Rio thinks. If she’d passed them on the street, she’d probably assume they were about the same age as Agatha’s godson. She’s sure they are older than that, but it can’t be by much. She makes them far too nervous for an experienced psychiatrist. 

 

“I’m noticing that you still refer to Ms-”

 

“Professor ,” Rio corrects. “She’s a professor.”

 

The doc looks almost annoyed. Yes, definitely too young to be seeing someone like her. Too green. “Right, sorry. Professor. Well, I’m noticing that you still refer to Professor Harkness as your wife, even though you’ve been seperated for…”

 

They raise an expectant eyebrow at her. Rio rolls her eyes. “Longer than you’ve been out of grad school,” she snipes when they continue to stare at her. The sad thing is that it’s probably true.

 

“Right,” her psychiatrist replies. There’s definitely a touch of irritation in their voice now, and Rio feels a sense of bright, ugly satisfaction. “A decent amount of time, then?”

 

“Sure,” Rio sighs. “Too long to still be calling her my wife and not be creepy, which I’m sure is what you’re getting at.”

 

“Actually, no.” They scribble down a quick note on their blue-striped notepad, then set their pen back down. “Let’s shift our train of thought slightly, just for a minute. You told me the two of you often get together on your son’s birthday, correct?”

 

Rio frowns. “When we can. Why?”

 

“Mmhm.” Another quick note. “And you have an agreement in place to not fight on those days, correct?” 

 

“Yes,” Rio snaps. “ Why? ”

 

Her therapist folds one leg over their lap, now looking almost a bit smug. Rio’s starting to really dislike them. “You tell me.”

 

“¿Perdoname?”

 

They nod. “Why do you think I’m asking?” 

 

“Because you’re a sadist who’s fixated on my personal life instead of what we’re supposed to be working on?”

 

The doctor sighs, looking exasperated.”Let me try another question. Why are those dates so important to you?”

 

Rio glares. Her therapist waits. 

 

“Because it feels like a step back in time,” she replies eventually. “Back to when we were happier. Just for a little while, things get to be…like they used to be.”

 

The psychiatrist taps their fingers along the edge of their clipboard. The sound grates on her nerves- a bit hypocritical, she supposes, but she’ll allow herself that. Rio unfolds her arms, lounging back in her chair. 

 

“What’re you thinking there, doctor?”

 

They set the clipboard down and lace their fingers together. “Can we agree to hold comments until I’m done explaining?”

 

“Fine,” Rio grumbles. “Go ahead.”

 

Her therapist nods, looking pleased. “Okay. Well, as we’ve been talking, I’ve started to notice a common thread.” They tap their nails along their own knuckles. “The case that you were recommended to speak to me about is from a few weeks ago. But every time we talk, the topic seems to end up shifting to a few years ago. More than a few, actually.”

 

Rio refolds her arms. Her psychatrist ignores that.

 

 “It seems to me like that the past is a fairly common point of focus in your mind. Now, I try not to speculate too much, but-”

 

Rio snorts. The doctor gives her an annoyed look and continues. “ But I think that may be because you’re trying- subconsciously or not- to recreate it. Just-” They give her a warning look when she opens her mouth to protest. “Take a second and think about it, alright?”

 

They look her in the eyes, and suddenly those young, nervous-looking eyes are hard as steel. They pin hers steady, forcing her to listen. “You told me that those birthday dates feel like going back to the way things used to be. I think that’s what you want, in your heart. Does that sound right to you?”

 

She purses her lips stubbornly. Her therapist sighs after a moment. “You can comment now.”

 

“Of course I want to go back to the way things used to be!” Rio snaps. “What mother in my shoes wouldn’t? What divorceé wouldn’t?”

 

“But you can’t ,” the doctor replies gently. “We all wish we could, at some point in our lives. But we can’t.”

 

She can’t bring herself to look away. She wants to, wants to so desperately , but she can’t. Her therapist puts a gentle hand on the table between them- not touching, but still supporting. 

 

“We can visit our history, Agent, but we can’t live there. No matter how much we miss it.”

 

This time, Rio can manage to break the stare. She rolls her neck in a circle, landing on a spot on the ceiling. The psychiatrist doesn’t drag her back, but she can still feel that intense stare on her. She’s honestly almost impressed. She didn’t think they had it in them.

 

“You can’t change your past,” the too-young shrink continues calmly. “Not with your ex-wife, and not with your son. I know that’s harsh, but you don’t seem like the kind of person who likes things sugarcoated.”

 

Rio chuckles humorlessly. “You’re right about that.”

 

Her doctor smiles. They have a nice smile, now that she stops to look at it. “I’m going to ask you this question again, okay? Why do you think I’m asking you about all this?”

 

Rio takes a deep breath and lets it out. “Because that’s what it all goes back to in the end, isn’t it? My job, my ex, my life…it all leads back to what I lost.”

 

“It does. But it’s your choice whether to to follow that lead, Rio. You can continue to walk backwards down that path…” Her therapists cocks their head at her. “Or you can see what you can make of the future. What that future looks like…” 

 

A wry smile. “Or who it looks like…well, you won’t know until it starts, will you?”

 

~

 

“Have you tried dating again, Agatha?”

 

Most of the time, her laughter in her therapist’s office is forced. This time, however, Agatha lets out a genuine cackle. 

 

“Don’t tell me you’re propositioning me, Doc? Don’t get me wrong, you’re not bad looking, but I doubt you’re good enough in bed to make looking for another therapist worth it . ”

 

Her psychologist smiles calmly. She’s always so infuriatingly calm, no matter what Agatha says. She supposes that that’s the reason she’s managed to keep seeing her. 

 

“You and I both know that’s not what I meant. Have you tried seeing anyone else since you and your ex-wife split up?”

 

Agatha shrugs. “A hookup or two. Nothing crazy.”

 

The curly-haired doctor nods. She’s wearing something slightly easier to stomach today- a simple black-and-white checkered dress- but it clashes awkwardly with those awful throw pillows. 

 

“No serious emotional connections, though?”

 

She snorts. “ God no. No, no. Definitely not.”

 

Her therapist cocks her head. “Can you tell me why you feel so…strongly about that?”

 

Agatha purses her lips. The doctor waits patiently. So goddamn patient. 

 

“I’m not looking to see anyone else,” she finally replies. “Never have, probably never will.”

 

The shrink smooths down her skirt. It’s a compulsive habit of hers, Agatha’s noticed. Maybe her psychiatrist has some psychiatric issues of her own. 

 

“And there’s nothing wrong with staying single, of course. You clearly have people who love you, romantic or not. But I can’t help but wonder if there’s a deeper reason behind it.” She crosses her legs, completely undoing the work she’d done just a moment earlier. “Have you thought about what it would be like being in a serious relationship again?”

 

Agatha considers. She’s gotten better at taking her therapist’s ideas into consideration, though she still finds them stupid about fifty percent of the time. 

 

“No. Can’t even picture it.”

 

“Do you think that’s just because you don’t want it? Or is there something stopping you?”

 

Agatha crosses her arms. “Are you implying that I’m letting feelings for my ex-wife creating some kind of mental block that stops me from screwing other women? In case I need to remind you, Doc, the only reason I’m here is because I ended things with her. What, you think I’m just lying to myself about that?”

 

The doctor smiles indulgently. “Yes and no, I think.” She holds up a hand, stopping any indignant spluttering before it can start. “Let me finish. I don’t think there’s any mental block, as you put it. But I do think there are some feelings weighing you down, and I don’t think that breakup resolved any of them. Does that make sense?”

 

She grumbles petulantly, but nods. “So what do you think I have to do to resolve those, huh?”

 

Her therapist folds her hands calmly. “I can’t answer that for you, Agatha. You have to decide that for yourself.” She chews on her lip, contemplating. “Some people find writing a letter cathartic. You can send it or destroy it, it’s up to you. I’ve also had a few clients try emails, or sit down to have a conversation with a mediator. Even had one once try roleplay. In the end, only you can decide what will give you the closure you need.”

 

~

 

“You aren’t actually going to do it, are you?” Jen demands. Agatha rolls her eyes, swirling her glass of wine in her hand. 

 

“Jesus, Jen, have you no faith in me?”

 

“No,” Jennifer snarks. Agatha rolls her eyes again. 

 

At the kitchen table, Alice’s phone trills. She leaves it facedown on the table, keeping her focus on her roommates. Her eyes- normally patient and kind, if a little exasperated- are steely and guarded- and, Agatha notices, fixed on Jen. What exactly have you two been talking about when you’re alone, huh?  

 

“What is your plan, Agatha?”

 

She shrugs and takes a swallow of her wine. “Don’t have one, don’t need one. At this point, I think just letting it go is just easier.”

 

Lilia frowns, plucking the glass from her hand. “That’s not healthy and you know it.”

 

Alice’s phone rings again. This time, she picks it up and glances at the screen. Agatha snatches her drink back from Lilia, narrowly avoiding sloshing it down her front. 

 

“Neither is fast food, Lils. Or social media, or sleep deprivation, or drinking… ” She raises her glass to the room at large and takes another swig. “But no one really stages an intervention for that, do they?”

 

Alice opens her mouth and Agatha waves her away. “Okay, okay, bad example. But still.”

 

“That wasn’t what I-”

 

“ My point, ” Agatha drawls, “Is that everyone has those little unhealthy habits. As long as they don’t get out of control, it’s not a big deal! And I have a shrink now to prevent them from getting out of control, so-”

 

Jen starts to argue almost immediately, while Lilia swipes the drink from Agatha once more and takes a drink herself. None of the three notice Alice’s phone ringing once more, nor do they notice when she exits the room with a distressed frown on her face- and certainly none of them hear the voicemail that sets her to pacing the hall.

 

But someone else in the house does. 

 

~

 

8:37 PM. Wednesday, December 11th.

 

You Have: One New Voicemail From Rio Vidal (Work)

 

“It’s me. Look, I just got a heads-up call from a friend in the DA’s office, and…they don’t like our arrest. Not enough forensic evidence, too much focus on the circumstantial. The ADA won’t prosecute, Alice. The case won’t even go to grand jury.”

 

“...”

 

“I called my commander when I got the news. The case will stay officially open, but reading between the lines…I think they’re going to pull me out of it. Knowing my boss, I imagine I’ll be back in Washington in maybe a week, week and a half at most. Just…thought you might want to know. Get some sleep, chica. We’ll cross our fingers that this will look better in the morning.”



Chapter End Notes

Uh oh 👀

A couple things! We're in the home stretch now. Our final "chapter" will be a couple deleted scenes that I didn't want to leave out entirely, plus a note from me, so these next couple chapters will bring this story to an end! I can't believe how far we've come and I'm so grateful for all of your kindness along the way. This is the longest piece of writing I've EVER done, original or fanfic, and I'm honestly probably going to cry when I complete it.

This also wasn't my favorite chapter to write, hence why it took so long. In the end, I think it comes down to the fact that I, I- like Rio- don't particularly like her psychiatrist. I struggled a lot with differentiating them from Agatha's therapist and not letting them go completely 2D, and in the end I think it ended with someone slightly less professional than I wanted. I do have to remind myself that this is fiction and what would be appropriate for a real-life person or therapist is very different, but it still bugged me, and is frankly still bugging me. So please be gentle with me this chapter lol. Hope you're still enjoying and we'll see y'all soon!

Chapter 18

Chapter Summary

In the wake of Rio's last voicemail, one member of the household makes a decision for someone they love.

Chapter Notes

A few reminders before I post this chapter:

- I am true to my tags and trigger warnings.

- A lot of therapy has happened "offscreen", so to speak. Agatha and Rio have both undergone some healing that couldn't make it into the final draft, and Agatha has earned back some of Billy's trust through continuing to go to therapy.

- Teen is absolutely a dork who believes in meet-cutes and rom-coms.

- Rom-coms aren't fun unless they're a little fucked up.

This took me SO long and I'll admit, I'm a touch anxious about posting it. This chapter has actually been in the works since the story started, and the buildup has been so fun that I'm nervous the climax will underwhelm. Nonetheless, I hope you guys continue to enjoy, and thank you SO MUCH to everyone who has stuck with me on this crazy ride! Now throw your hands up, because this chapter is going to be a ROLLER COASTER!!!!

...oh, and if you hate me after this, I don't blame you 😈

See the end of the chapter for more notes
