7:42 AM. Friday, December 13th.

 

You Have One New Message From: Alice Wu (Cell)

 

Alice Wu (Cell): hey sorry to bother but are you busy?

 

Rio Vidal: No. Are you alright?

 

Alice Wu (Cell): m fine, but im at therapy and have a flat tire. could you come give me a ride?

 

Rio Vidal: Sure. Send me the address.

 

Alice Wu (Cell) shared a location with you. 

 

Alice Wu (Cell): thanks. ur a lifesaver. 

 

Rio Vidal: …are you sure you’re alright? 

 

Alice Wu (Cell): yea why?

 

Rio Vidal: It’s nothing. See u soon.

 

~

 

“What kind of moron schedules a therapy session at this time of day, huh?”

 

Agatha snatches Jen’s bagel from her hand as she sweeps through the kitchen and takes a bite. A smattering of crumbs hit the front of her university t-shirt and she brushes them away with an annoyed grumble. Jen gives her a dirty look. 

 

“Good morning to you too, Agatha. What’s got you feeling so pleasant today?” 

 

Agatha rolls her eyes. “Alice. She just texted me, saying she has a flat tire and can I come pick her up from therapy? Never mind that I could have had classes today, which I luckily don’t .”

 

She sticks the bagel between her teeth and hops into her boots, not bothering to change out of her sequined Naughty sweatpants. If Alice is going to be embarrassed, she should have texted someone else.

 

In the hall mirror, she sees Jen and Lilia exchange an odd look. She furrows her brow, glancing over her shoulder. “What?”


“I thought…” Lilia begins. She shakes her head, looking confused. “Never mind.”

 

Agatha shrugs, grabbing a jacket and throwing it on. “Whatever. I’ll be back. If I decide to force Alice to buy me coffee as a thank you for saving her ass, I’ll bring you something.”

 

She swings out the door with a wave and a chirped ta-ta! . Jen shakes her head, rolling her eyes. “I swear she’s more cheerful when she has something to complain about.” She stands and pops another bagel into the toaster, then frowns at the look on Lilia’s face. “What’s up, Lils?”

 

Lilia points. Jen glances around down the hall, towards Alice’s room. 

 

Towards Alice. 

 

Jen blinks, poking her head out into the hallway to make sure she’s seeing correctly. “Wait, what? Alice, you’re- home?”

 

Alice- standing over her bed, which looks like a tornado hit it- glances over at her. She’s flushed, flustered, and very obviously not at therapy, considering the flannel shorts and silky pink t-shirt she’d clearly been wearing as pajamas. 

 

( Is that Jen’s?)

 

 “Yeah, I am. Where else would I be? I’m coming off a night shift, I won’t go in until this afternoon.”

 

“Wait, I thought…” Jen trails off, glancing over at Lilia. Alice tilts her head, a touch of anxiety creeping into her face.

 

“What, what’s wrong?”

 

Lilia raises her eyebrows. Your guess is as good as mine. Jen sighs and pinches the bridge of her nose. “Nothing, I guess. Just…weird morning, that’s all. Not a big deal.”

 

At least, she hopes not.

 

Alice gives her a disbelieving look. “Okay, we’re gonna talk about that in a minute. But-” She rifles through her bedding, looking increasingly frustrated. “Can you call my phone first? I can’t find it anywhere.”

 

~

 

Alice isn’t in the parking lot when she gets there. Later, Agatha will notice that her car isn’t there either. But for now, she’s too preoccupied with figuring out where the hell her friend actually is. Alice hasn’t picked up her phone since sending her the address, a fact that’s annoying the shit out of her on this miserable, misty December morning. All she wants right now is to be at home with her bunny and a cup of coffee. Maybe even hot chocolate, if she’s feeling a little naughty. She still doctors it the way she did in college- cinnamon, sugar, and way too much cocoa. 

 

Like Rio used to make, she thinks involuntarily, and shudders. God , she needs to get her out of her head. 

 

“But you don’t want her out, do you?”, that pesky little voice in her head whispers. Agatha growls to herself and grinds the heel of her palm into her forehead.

 

“Nope. Okay, that’s enough.” 

 

She hops out of the car and walks towards the building, keeping her eyes peeled as she goes. Still no sign of Alice. When she gets into the lobby, she glances over the signboard and finds the business labeled ‘ counselling’ . 

 

Eleventh floor. Perfect.

 

The elevator is playing something vaguely annoying as she takes the ride up. Agatha taps her foot impatiently as it rises, far too slowly for her tastes. It finally spits her out in a plain-looking hallway lined with office doors, each with the same dark wood and frosted glass windows. Like something out of a horror movie, she thinks, and wrinkles her nose.

 

Ugh.  

 

She finds the correct door and considers knocking briefly. Then, she shrugs to herself and pushes it open. A young man with glasses looks up from behind the reception desk as the bell on the door chimes. 

 

“Hi there. Are you here for an appointment?”

Agatha leans one elbow on his desk, resting her chin on her fist. “I’ve already got a shrink, sweetheart. I’m just here picking up a friend from hers.” 

 

The young man glances at his computer with a confused look. “Er- I’m sorry, Miss, but no one else is here. Are you sure your friend’s appointment is with us?” 

 

Agatha frowns. “There’s not another therapist in the building, is there?”

 

“No, but-”

She spreads her hands. “Okay, then her appointment must have been here. Alice Wu-Gulliver. If you can’t tell me she’s here, then just tell her I’m here.”

 

The receptionist sighs. “Can I get a name?”

Agatha rolls her eyes. God, why couldn’t Alice just have been waiting for her in the parking lot?  

 

“Agatha Harkness.”

 

The kid taps away at his computer, brow wrinkling. “Uh, well- I don’t have your friend on my schedule, but…” 

 

He tilts his head, looking awkwardly confounded. Agatha rolls her eyes again. 

 

“What now ?”

 

“Well, I see your name on my schedule.”

 

She blinks. “Come again?”

 

Now he’s really looking awkward, pimply face flushing slightly. “I- see your name on the schedule, along with-”

 

The door chimes. The kid’s head turns. 

 

Agatha almost doesn’t need to look. Normally, she laughs at the idea that one can sense a person’s presence. She doesn’t believe in soulmates, after all ( except for on lonely and cold nights, when no drink burns quite hot enough to replace the warmth of a body beside her. Then, she sets aside her disbelief and cries for a time.). But she supposes some long-buried instinct remembers a pattern of some kind. Maybe the tap of her boot on the wood floors, or the rustling of fabric as she scopes the place out, or the sharp intake of breath when she registers who she’s looking at. 

 

Agatha closes her eyes, rubs her temples.

 

“Are you here for the reason I think you are?”

 

“Why do you think I’m here?” Rio replies softly. Her tone is tenatative, cautious- almost as though she’s afraid Agatha’s going to lash out at her. Like her very presence is an offense. 

 

Agatha drops her chin to her chest with a disbelieving laugh, shaking her head as she turns. “Probably to arrest me.”

 

Rio’s eyebrows lift and Agatha laughs again. “Because I’m going to kill my roommate.”

 

The kid at the front desk sits up, looking alarmed. But Rio chuckles, and some of the tension eases off her shoulders just the slightest bit. The atmosphere is still unbelievably thick, choked with a briar of things unsaid that feel as though they’ll slice like thorns if they try to push through them. But it’s nice to laugh with her again, just for a moment. 

 

“So you really think she set us up?”

 

Agatha snorts. “Normally I wouldn’t say she’s that stupid , but I can’t think of another alternative. Unless you booked us an appointment with…” She glances over the cards, then rolls her eyes. “Oh my god, a couple’s counselor. ” 

 

Rio’s eyes widen. “No, I absolutely did not do that.”

 

At the desk, the kid clears his throat. “So…did you guys want the session or…?”

 

Collectively, they ignore him. Rio fishes her cell phone out of her pocket (black cargo pants, Agatha notices- she must be working later) and swipes it open. She calls, waits, then grumbles something unflattering in Spanish as the call presumably goes to voicemail.

 

 Agatha rolls her eyes and takes out her own cell. A new text message blares across her home screen and she glances over it. 

 

~

 

8:30 AM

 

Mommy Issues ™: i know youre probably mad and thats ok

 

Mommy Issues ™: but i also know that if u don’t talk to rio before she leaves you’re just going to stay mad 

 

Mommy Issues ™: and sad

 

Mommy Issues ™: and no one wants u to live ur life like that, because even when we’re mad at u we still love you. 

 

Mommy Issues ™: so please listen to ur therapist, go resolve some issues, and dont do anything stupid

 

Mommy Issues ™: and dont waste time yelling at me because i need to go give Alice back her phone

 

~

 

Her head is starting to hurt. Pinching the bridge of her nose, Agatha reads over the messages once more. There’s a lot to process, and absolutely none of it makes sense.

 

What in the hell is going on here?

 

She shakes her head as if to clear it- like a damn Etch-a-Sketch- then jumps as she realizes Rio has moved significantly closer. Her ex-wife steps back hastily, raising her hands. 

 

“Lo siento, lo siento. I didn’t mean to-”

 

“To read over my shoulder?” Agatha replies dryly. Rio winces. 

 

“I said I was sorry.”

 

Agatha rubs her forehead, scanning the texts again . This time, one line catches her eye. She glances up at Rio, brow wrinkling. 

 

“What does she mean by leave? Are you…?”

 

She trails off, raising an eyebrow (trying to ignore the sinking feeling the idea puts in the pit of her stomach). Rio twists her fingers in her sleeve, gaze dropping to the ground. 

 

“The case that brought me here is almost certainly over. We have a few more things to do, but I expect to be out of your hair within a week or so.”

 

Agatha exhales softly, letting out a heavy woosh! of air. She should be glad. She should be relieved. She should be waving her ex-wife goodbye with a giddy air, frolicking in the dust her stupid motorcycle leaves behind.

 

Of course, she’s never been good at doing what she’s supposed to. 

 

Rio glances up at her, and there’s a sad sort of resignation in her eyes. She’s going to walk away, Agatha realizes. For once in her life, she’s going to do what she had asked without a fight. She almost opens her mouth to- to what? Comfort her? Shout at her? Beg her to stay? She’s not sure, and thankfully, Rio saves her from having to find out.

 

“So what did she mean about you? You’re…in therapy?”

 

Agatha laughs. She can’t help it. Standing here in a counselor’s office, talking to her ex-wife about therapy , is a situation so fantastical that she knows the Agatha of fifteen years ago would never have believed it. Hell, Agatha of three years ago probably wouldn’t have believed it either. 

 

“I am. Not my idea, believe me- and my therapist has awful taste in decor- but I am.”

 

Rio chuckles softly. “I am too. Wasn’t my idea, either.” She sticks her hands in her pockets, sliding her thumbs through her belt loops. Some old voice in Agatha’s head wants to scold her, remind her that they will rip if she keeps doing that, and who will fix them because she knows damn well she can’t sew. 

 

“So your therapist thought we should go to couples counseling? Seems like fraught with peril, no?”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes. “That wasn’t exactly what she said. She thinks I need to find a way to talk through my feelings on our…situation. I guess Alice- or whoever that is, since she says she needs to give the phone back- thought this was the solution.”

 

Rio gives her a small smile. “Well, she cares, anyways.” She turns and gestures gallantly to the door- still theatrical, in spite of everything. “After you?”

 

“There’s a cancellation fee,” the kid behind the desk squeaks quietly. Agatha fixes him with a death glare- the kind that leaves her students cowering in their seats, which certainly always feeds her ego. Following Rio’s lead, she tosses her hair over one shoulder and makes to leave. 

 

Her feet don’t move. 

 

She frowns, tries again. Her feet- traitorous feet, traitorous legs, traitorous heart - get maybe a step, then hesitate. Rio turns and glances over her shoulder. 

 

“Agatha?”

 

Agatha closes her eyes. Deep breath in, deep breath out . Leave, and go home, and put the past in the past.

 

Or stay. 

 

Stay, and get what she’s been longing for. Which is…what, exactly? Closure? Or something else?

 

Rio turns fully. She starts to edge back over- cautiously, so cautiously, like a feral animal scoping out an offered morsel. “Agatha? Are you…”

 

She doesn’t finish, letting the question hang in the air. Agatha chews on her lip, takes another breath. 

 

Leave. 

 

Stay. 

 

She makes a decision. 

 

“What the hell?” Agatha shrugs, hoping her forced grin is enough to conceal her nerves. “May as well avoid the cancellation fee. That is, if you don’t have plans.”

 

Rio blinks, then nods slowly. “I…don’t. I intend to meet with Alice later today, but…nothing else.”

 

Agatha spreads her hands. “Well then!” She spins to the kid, who looks just about as lost as a little boy on his first day of kindergarten. “Which way to the office?”

 

~

 

The couple’s therapist- great, more therapists. And she just got to sort of liking hers, too- is a tall, willowy man with closely cropped hair and rimless glasses that, Agatha thinks, make him look like an absolute tool. He has an annoyingly neutral face- not just his expression, either, it’s his entire face. Everything about him is plain, neat, and entirely dismissable. She thinks she might make it down the end of the hallway before she forgets what he looks like. 

 

Next to her, Rio bounces her foot absently. Up, down. Up, down. Up-down, up-down, up-down, up-down. Agatha almost asks her to stop, but restrains herself. 

 

Up, down. Up, down. 

 

The couch is small. Their thighs brush together each time her foot bounces. 

 

Up, down. Up, down.

 

Agatha, frankly, doesn’t know what to do with herself. She can admit to herself now that she’d settled into their little routine- not quite comfortable, but complacent. Enough so that sitting here now with Rio, in a space where they’re neither fighting or fucking, feels incredibly foreign to her. 

 

Up, down. Up, down. 

 

On the couch across from them, the counselor folds his hands calmly. “Let’s begin by hearing a bit of background. How long have you two been together?”

 

Agatha snorts. “ That’s complicated.”

 

Rio says nothing.

 

The doctor pushes his glasses up the bridge of his nose. “How so?”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes, flopping back in her seat. Her knees splay, and one ankle brushes Rio’s. “It’s been thirty years. But we’ve been divorced for a little over half of that.”

 

One eyebrow lifts. Just barely, just enough to be perceptible. “Oh?”

 

Agatha shrugs. She glances over at Rio, cocking her head in the doc’s direction. Your turn. 

 

Her ex-wife twists her fingers in her sleeves. Once again, Agatha wants to scold her. Her fingertips twinge with the memories of old needle pricks, sewing on picked off buttons by hand after their machine had torn up the sleeves of shirts too expensive to continue ruining. 

 

“We’ve been divorced, sí, but we haven’t exactly been…seperated.”

 

“Mmm.” The counselor nods, tapping his pencil on his clipboard. He’s moving in the same rhythm as Rio’s boot, Agatha notices. Up-down, tap-tap. Up-down, tap-tap. “And so everyone’s a little unsure of where we stand, hm?”

 

Rio sucks in her lower lip, and Agatha suspects she’s chewing on the inside. “Something like that.”

Agatha snorts again, but there’s a hint of bitterness seeping into her tone ( she debates trying to stop it. She doesn’t) . “ I’m pretty sure of where we stand. I’m pretty sure I decided that over a month ago.”

 

“Then why are we here, Agatha?” Rio mutters. “This was your idea.”

 

“Technically, this was Alice’s idea.”

 

( Or whichever member of the household swiped her phone, anyways, and she has a decent idea of who that was. But she’s not in the mood to share right about now.)

 

Rio purses her lips- and yes, she’s definitely been gnawing, there’s a touch of blood on the edge. “Yes, but it was your idea to come in. I was prepared to leave.” 

 

Agatha rolls her eyes. “Yeah, well, my therapist has been pushing me to get closure. Figured she hasn’t been all that awful to me yet, so…”

 

Rio closes her eyes, leaning forward on her knees. Her foot has stopped bouncing. “If that’s what you’re after, we may as well go now.”

 

Their counselor cocks his head. “What makes you say that?”

 

“There’s no such thing as closure,” Rio replies softly. “Not when you broke up the way we did. For the reasons we did.”

 

Agatha barks out a laugh. “And what makes you so qualified to say that, huh?” She turns to face her ex-wife, folding her arms over her chest when Rio glances away. “No, please, go on. Tell me your expertise. ”

 

“My expertise?” Rio repeats. “My expertise is my work, Agatha!”

 

Agatha laughs again, this time disbelievingly. The counselor glances between them. “What line of work are you, Rio?”

 

“FBI.”

 

“ Homi-cide, ” Agatha sing-songs. “Spends all her time around dead bodies. Tells you a lot about her, doesn’t it?”

 

Rio’s head whips towards her. “I’ve told you, my work-”

 

“Always work with this one, isn’t it?” Agatha smirks wryly, trying to belie the rising discomfort in her voice. There’s an old beast stirring deep in her chest, something so old she’d honestly believed it had died- but she knows better now, now that she’s started to pick her walls apart, peeling them away like layers of skin, of tissue, of bone, dissecting herself until there’s nothing left but…

 

What? What does she have, once you took away that immediate burst of attraction, once you lifted the lid of the anger hiding just beneneath it?

 

She doesn’t know, and it frightens her. So she drags the anger to the forefront of her mind instead. 

 

“ Always work. Guess that’s one thing that hasn’t changed in all these years.”  

 

Rio shakes her head, still chewing on her lip. Agatha cocks an eyebrow at her. “Feel free to chime in anytime, sweetheart.” 

 

Rio spreads her hands. “I’m not sure what you want me to say.”

 

Anger. Anger, anger, anger.

 

“ Anything, ” she snaps. “Tell the therapist, if you don’t want to tell me. Tell him all about it.”

 

“Agatha-”

 

“Tell him why we got divorced, baby. Tell him!” She’s on a roll now, moving too quickly to stop, moving too quickly to slow down. “Tell the good doctor all about it.”

 

“We didn’t break up because of my work.”

 

“Didn’t we?” Agatha cocks an eyebrow. “I seem to recall at least a few arguments about that. But please, go on. Maybe I’m losing track. There were a lot, after all.” 

 

Rio rubs her head. “It’s normal for a couple to fight after losing a child, especially in cases like Nicky’s.”

 

“Cases like- !” Agatha tosses her hands up, shaking her head in disbelief. “Even now, you’re still talking like you’re at work. You can’t break away from it, can you? Not then- not when I begged, not when I cried, not when cops had to tackle me down from a fucking bridge, which has cost me way more than I'd like to admit in therapy bills-  and definitely not now.” 

 

She jumps up like someone’s rammed a poker through her spine, beginning to pace. She’s hitting her stride now, walking an old path, but one that shapes to her footsteps like she’d never strayed from it. “Our son died and you ran to the fucking morgue! You turned into some cold, case-obsessed cop and left me alone while I was losing my mind, lashing out at my friends, getting myself fired- oh, you remember that, right?” She cackles- sounding like a damn madwoman and not caring in the slightest- and tosses her head in the direction of her ex-wife. “That was enough to finally pull you out of the office, wasn’t it? Couldn’t have the star agent’s wife getting dragged to jail for assault, after all- which would have been bullshit, by the way, and I still stand by that.”

 

 “I never disagreed with you on that.”

 

“ Really. Because the way you talked to your little colleagues, I would have thought that-”

 

On the other sofa, the doctor raps lightly on the end table beside him to get their attention. Agatha gives him a withering stare that does absolutely nothing, much to her annoyance. “We’re wandering off here. I’m going to give you a moment to wrap up that thought, then I’d like you to go back to where you started. Correct me if you think I’m wrong, but I think the looming issue at hand seems to be more along the lines of where you started.”

 

Agatha growls, but flops over into the wingback chair beside the window. She doesn’t want to sit down on the couch again. No more touching.

 

“Fine. Fine, fine. So in your professional opinion, doc, what seems to be my big problem, huh?”

 

The counselor raps his pen again- tap-tap, tap-tap, tap-tap, like the beat of a heart. Across from him, Rio is still.

 

“Well, I don’t know your full history, so it would be remiss of me to make assumptions this early into the session. But it sounds to me like you two went through a great tragedy, yes?” He holds his hand up, clearly not looking for an answer.  “Now your wife is right, it’s very normal for couples’ relationships to suffer in the wake of such a loss. The key, in my opinion, is to make sure that you’re communicating your issues- and based on the…conversation we’ve had so far, I’m going to guess that the pair of you struggled with that. Does that sound correct?”

 

On the sofa, Rio chuckles mirthlessly. “Sounds like an understatement to me.” 

 

Agatha’s head whips towards her. “And what the hell is that supposed to mean?”

 

Their therapist opens his mouth to intervene, but Rio spins to face her before he can speak. “Struggled with communication? We didn’t communicate, Agatha! You didn’t talk to me! You wouldn’t talk to me!”

 

She runs her hands across her head in her agitation, smoothing down hair that’s already perfectly in place. “You closed me out at every turn , fought me on practically everything! Every conversation I started, you turned into an argument. You say I left you alone, that I wasn’t there for you, but how could I be when you constantly pushed me away?!”

 

Agatha laughs again, a sharp, ugly sound that she hardly recognizes as her own voice. “Oh, so it’s my fault now?”

 

“I didn’t say that!” Rio snaps. “But the doctor is right , querida, he’s right. We didn’t speak to each other about anything, we didn’t ever stop to work on our issues. We never have! Even after all these years, we’re either in each other’s arms or at each other’s throats. We don’t seem to be capable of anything else!”

 

“If we’re such a mess, why do you keep coming back to me?” Agatha drawls. “Because I don’t believe for a second you ended up on assignment here on accident.”

 

“ Because I love you!”  

 

A pause. Blue eyes meet brown, earnest and intense in that old, spine-tingling way that had gotten her attention when she’d been a young woman in her mother’s church choir. 

 

I love you. 

 

When was the last time she’d said those words? There had certainly been plenty of ‘ I want you ’s and ‘ I need you’ s more than a few ‘I miss you’s. But I love you?

 

“I love you,” Rio repeats, as if reading her mind. “I never stopped loving you. You know that.” 

 

She stops and waits, giving Agatha an expectant look. Agatha purses her lips, dropping her gaze to the floor. “Wish you would have said that years ago. Things would have been a lot less complicated then.”

 

“Love isn’t complicated,” her ex-wife retorts. “ Relationships are complicated. Love is just…love.” 

 

Her voice is starting to quaver now- slightly, so slightly that the doctor probably doesn’t hear it. But Agatha does. Agatha knows her better than anyone else in the world. She knows that too. “I love you. After all these years, after everything we’ve been through…I love you. I can’t not love you. To me, loving you is as involuntary as breathing.” She pauses. “And I think you love me too. At least, I did. But if you don’t…”

 

She takes a shaky breath, and it’s obvious that she’s near tears. “If you meant it when you said you hated me, then tell me now, and I’ll walk away.”

 

A beat passes.

 

Let her go. Let her walk away. You hate her. 

 

Rio hiccups out a soft laugh. Those professional black cargo pants rustle as she stands. 

 

You can’t let her walk away. 

 

Rio’s shadow crosses the floor in front of her. Something small and shiny- an earring, proably made herself- slips free and hits the ground behind her. 

 

Agatha picks it up on instinct and turns it in her fingers. Like so many things she owns, hidden away from the light of day, it’s a little piece of her ex-wife. The ring still hidden in a lockbox under her bed. The painting she still keeps in her closet. The package that Alice had tried and failed to lie about- because oh, that had been from Rio too, hadn’t it? Of course it had. S o many artifacts of the life she’d had, of the woman she’d loved. Who loved her enough to follow her all these years, and who now loved her enough to leave.

 

You can’t let her go.

 

Boots tap across the hardwood floor. Agatha has a sudden memory of those boots running up to her in the midst of a ringing chaos, picking her up from a puddle of plywood and her own blood. 

 

Rio, saving her life. Rio, stroking her hair as paramedics wiped the blood and snot and tears away from her face. Rio, curled up in her arms in a field lit by stars, looking up at her like she could see the secrets of the universe in her eyes.

 

You don’t hate her. You can’t hate her.

 

The doorknob turns. 

 

You love her. 

 

Rio starts to pull the door open. Before she can leave, however, Agatha stretches out and grabs ahold of her hand. 

 

“ Wait. ”

 

She pulls, and Rio follows obediently. Her ex-wife ends up settled on the arm of her chair- not quite sitting on her lap, but close, so close that Agatha can breathe the fresh smell of her perfume. The position must be uncomfortable, but Rio looks at her like there’s nowhere else in the world she’d rather be.

Agatha reaches up to cup her face, running her thumb along Rio’s jawline. Rio leans into her touch like she’s starved for it. 

 

“Believe me, I wish I could say I hated you. I’ve tried for years to convince myself that I do.”

“But?”

 

Agatha laughs softly. “But I could never hate you.”

 

Rio nods. Though her expression is solemn, her eyes are shining hopefully. Agatha grins in spite of herself and hooks an arm around her back, dragging her down into her lap. Rio goes willingly, plaint as putty in her hands, and Agatha smirks. 

 

Their therapist coughs. She ignores him. Rio is more important. 

 

“I don’t hate you,” she murmurs, still stroking her ex-wife’s cheek. “But you’re right when you said we didn’t talk. So we need to talk now, if we want to have any chance of getting our shit together before we do hate each other.”

 

Rio nods, nuzzling into her palm. Agatha rolls her eyes and snatches her hand back. “Focus.” 

 

“Lo siento,” Rio murmurs, though she doesn’t look particularly sorry. “Where do you want to start?”

 

Agatha sighs. “Well, there’s definitely too much to go through in one damn therapy session.”

 

“And our time is almost up, I’m afraid,” the doctor chimes in helpfully. Agatha rolls her eyes again. 

 

“Yeah, yeah. We’ve held on years for this, you can wait five extra minutes.” She focuses her attention back on Rio. “I just…want to talk about one thing before we go.”

 

Rio cocks her head. Agatha rests a hand on her arm, holding her steady almost preemptively. “You know what.”

 

As she’d predicted, Rio bites her lip and makes an attempt to start fidgeting with her sleeve. Agatha squeezes her wrist and she leaves it obediently. “Work?” 

 

Agatha nods, smiling grimly. “If you put a gun to my head, I suppose I could admit that I maybe wasn’t always the easiest to be with. But you have to admit, your work took up a lot of your life. I just…” She sighs. “I want to know why . Why it mattered more than me.”

 

“It didn’t ,” Rio replies emphatically. “But work was how I…I…” 

 

She trails off, now playing with Agatha’s sleeve instead. Agatha makes a face, but allows it.

 

“Has Alice told you what department I’m in now?”

 

Agatha blinks. “No. You left homicide?”

 

Rio twirls her fingers in the soft purple fabric of Agatha’s shirt, first twisting it, then letting go. “I did. A long time ago, actually.”

 

“Then what-” 

 

“Special Victims,” Rio says softly. “I’m a child abductions specialist.”

 

Oh. 

 

“I couldn’t help Nicky,” Rio whispers. There’s tears sparkling at the edges of her eyes now, and Agatha cups her face once more. She leans into her hand gratefully as she continues. “I couldn’t save him. I couldn’t even find his killer. But I could do my best to make sure no other family had to suffer like ours. And doing that…”

 

“Made you feel better,” Agatha finishes. She wipes a stray tear away from her ex’s cheek. “Made you feel less guilty?”

 

Rio nods, letting the tears fall freely now. Agatha cradles her face in both hands, pressing their foreheads together.

 

“I didn’t know.”

 

“I think there’s a lot of things we don’t know about each other,” Rio murmurs. Agatha hums in agreement, running her thumbs along her jawline. Though Rio sighs in relief and lets her eyes flutter shut, she opens them after a moment and gives Agatha an adorably concerned look. 

 

“But you do know that…you know I don’t blame you, right?”

 

No, I don’t know that is the immediate answer that pops into her mind. I blame myself is the second. Somewhere in her head, however, her therapist’s voice nags a gentle correction. 

 

“Yes. And you know I don’t blame you either?”

 

“Yes,” Rio replies softly. “But it’s good to hear you say it.”

 

~

 

Afterwards, they make the poor decision of getting ice cream for breakfast- a pasttime of Rio’s from her academy days that she had never quite managed to shed, and one Agatha had unfortunately picked up on. Though it’s cold, too cold for both ice cream and the outdoors, they walk through the park side by side with their guilty pleasures in hand. Rio soaks it up greedily, sucking in breath after breath of crisp, clean air, so cold that it nearly stings her lungs to inhale. When the frost gets too much even for her insulated gloves to handle, she brushes her fingers hopefully against Agatha’s- and is delighted when Agatha grabs her hand and squeezes it. 

 

“You could have just asked to hold my hand, you know.”

 

Rio drops her head onto her shoulder, taking a tiny lick of her matcha (something Agatha always turns her nose up at). “Where’s the fun in that?”

 

Though they walk, eat, and flirt just a touch, they spend most of the morning talking . Actually talking. Not fighting, not pillow talk, just…conversation. Reintegrating themselves with each other’s lives. Rio tells Agatha a few stories of her most entertaining coworkers, and of the little black cat she’d brought in from the alley behind her apartment one day. In return, Agatha shares tales of her students and the various antics she gets up to in her classroom (“ After all, I’m one of only a few professors who can play a prank on an entire lecture hall full of students and call it study material.”). Rio laughs at the modified version of the Prisoner’s Dilemma she’d done in place of a test- “ God, the looks on their faces when I finally told them that it was only a participation grade. By then, I think a few friendships and at least one relationship had broken up.”- and actually cackles when Agatha tells her the story of the day she’d simply just…not shown up to class, just to see what the kids would do.

 

“Best day of my teaching career,” Agatha drawls, and Rio doubles over with laughter.

 

When they finish their ice cream, they find a quiet bench to sit on. There, Agatha’s tone goes solemn and soft. She lays her cheek against the top of Rio’s head and tells her about Westview, about wallowing there in her heartbreak and about the kind, bubbly mother-to-be who’d welcomed her on her first night with open arms and an open heart. Rio takes her hand when she shares her falling-out with Jen (however temporary that had turned out to be. In the end, Jen had found her way back to Agatha, just as Rio always does. Perhaps they have that in common after all.) and holds it until she’s caught up to the present day.

 

If she has her way, she’ll never let it go again.

 

When she’s done, Agatha looks expectantly at her. She sighs and snuggles closer, wrapping herself in the comfort of Agatha’s embrace before her own stories start to slip out in explosive, painful little bursts. 

 

She starts with the happy endings- the little girl who’d been found in the woods alive and well, the little boy who’d been safely retrieved from his mother’s drug den and returned to the care of his grandmother, the teenage twins who’d been the sole survivors of a man who had later been unmasked as a serial killer- but quickly lapses into the darker days. The ugly custody battle that had ended like something out of the Old Testament ( and this one, she tells Agatha to use her imagination ), the transgender teen who’d faked his own abduction to break away from his parents- which had, in the end, cost him his life- and the boy whose father gave him a bottle of monster spray. She starts to cry when she gets to the drowned girl- the girl with the beaded braids, who’d love fairies and the rain- and Agatha draws her head against her chest. She strokes her hair as she weeps, and her soft wool gloves scrape away the tears that freeze to her cheeks. 

 

Agatha holds her close, even after she’s calmed down. Rio would have been content to sit with her like that for the rest of the day- or at least, until she had to go meet up with Alice- but the ring of a cell phone rather rudely interrupts them. She pouts as Agatha fishes her phone out of her pocket and checks the caller ID. 

 

“I thought you didn’t have class today?”

 

“I don’t,” Agatha replies with a frown. “That’s not my school, that’s…Teen’s. The hell?”

 

She tugs her glove off with her teeth and swipes to answer it. “If he’s sick, send him home, if he got in a fight, ask him who won, and yes, I know he’s flunking Spanish. Does that answer whatever you’re bothering me about?”

 

The person on the other end says something unintelligible, and Agatha’s expression morphs slowly from annoyance, to concern, to muted alarm. “You’ve gotta be shitting me . How the hell did that happen?!” 

 

Rio cocks her head. Agatha jumps up and starts to power-walk back to her car- though not before grabbing Rio’s hand and dragging her along, which makes the latter chirp happily. “And where the hell was his teacher, huh? I- clearly they weren’t supervising, were they?! ”

 

She growls and glares at the screen. “You know what, I am not in the mood to hear it right now. Just tell me which ER they took my kid to and get the hell off my phone, capicsche?”

 

After a moment, Agatha ends the call and slumps back against the side of her car, massaging her temples. Rio rubs her arms, raising an eyebrow. “Something wrong?”

 

“Accident in theater class,” Agatha grumbles. “Apparently Teen got thrown during a scene and managed to go crashing right through a set wall. Supposedly okay, but I’m going to strangle that teacher.”

 

Rio hums gently. “Querida…”

 

“ Yes, I know teenagers do stupid shit,” Agatha sighs. “And yes, I know it probably wasn’t the teacher’s fault. I’ll worry about that when the kid’s stitched up.”

 

“I was going to say you shouldn’t have taken it out on that poor receptionist.”

 

“Oh, that?” Agatha snorts. “She’s a homophobe. I’ve been looking for an excuse to tear into her.”

 

Rio laughs. Agatha smiles, but there’s a hint of nervousness in her eyes. Even without asking, Rio knows she’s remembering the last time they spoke. 

 

“Sweetheart, I need to-”

 

“Go,” Rio murmurs. She wraps her arms reassuringly around Agatha’s waist, smiling when she sighs softly. She wants her here, she does, but you’d have to be a fool to miss the worry in her face. She certainly hadn’t missed the words my kid. Though they still sting in a way, it’s getting less and less each time. 

 

He isn’t taking Nicky’s place, no more than Agatha’s taken his Wanda’s. They’re simply a childless mother and a motherless child, finding their way in the world together. 

 

“Go see your boy. Just call me when you get the chance, okay?”

 

Agatha hums and answers her with a kiss.

 

~ 

 

Jennifer Kale- a passenger princess if there ever was one, and an impressively bad driver when she can’t be- nearly lands them all in a car crash as she and Agatha both come screeching into the parking lot of their nearest emergency room. Agatha flips her off unabashedly as she parks, pointedly ignoring Lilia’s eye roll.

 

“School called you too?” She hollers, leaning against her car as they pile out and head towards her. 

 

“You almost killed us, Agatha!” Jen shrieks. Agatha rolls her eyes. 

 

“Yes or no, Jen? Simple answer!”

 

“We’re all on Billy’s emergency contact form,” Lilia replies, neatly putting herself between the younger two before a catfight can start. She’s holding Jen’s arm in one hand (mindful of the touches of ice scattered across the asphalt, though she’s certainly more graceful than most women her age- likely even women half her age, considering Alice has had more stumbles in the past few years than anyone else in the house combined. ) and her phone in the other, and Agatha notices Alice’s smiling photo on the screen. She snatches it from Lilia as they begin to walk to the front entrance. 

 

“Good timing. I have a bone to pick with you.”

 

“That wasn’t me!”, her friend protests. “Talk to Teen!”

 

Agatha pauses, but only for a moment . Of-fucking-COURSE it was Teen. No one but her kid could come up with that plan. 

 

She smiles in spite of herself, but hastily pinches it back. Have to keep up an air of disapproval, after all.

 

“I knew it. That whole scheme reeked of his dramatics.” She wrinkles her nose at the phone as they approach the front desk, though she knows Alice can’t see her. “How did he know your password, though?”

On the other end, Alice sighs exasperatedly.  “Well, I thought I could trust him with it. Guess I’m changing that.” 

 

Agatha snorts. “Sort of locking the stable door after the horses bolt there, Al.” 

 

“Who says that?” Jen snipes. She sweeps away before Agatha can argue, walking up to the front desk and rapping insistently on the plexiglass barrier. The receptionist gives her a dirty look, which she ignores. 

 

“We’re here to pick up Billy Maximoff. And before you ask, his date of birth is May 25th, 2008, he’s here for stitches and a potential concussion, and she-” Quick as a flash, she turns and snatches Agatha’s wallet from her pocket, sliding it through the partition. “-is his legal guardian. Her ID and insurance cards are both in the front pocket.” 

 

The receptionist glares at her, but takes the cards out. From the other end of the line, someone calls Alice’s name and she swears softly. 

 

“ Shit. Agatha, I’ve gotta go. Keep me updated, okay?”

 

She’s gone before Agatha has the chance to respond. Lilia- appearing from behind her- swipes her phone back from Agatha. 

 

“Where is she off to?”

 

Agatha shrugs. “Important police work, no doubt. Probably off to gossip with Rio about…”

 

She pauses, glancing over Lilia’s face. The older woman raises an eyebrow. “About?”

 

Agatha hesitates. After a moment, Lilia breaks into a small smirk. “Joking. Teen told us everything.” She nudges Agatha’s arm, still giving her that wry, slightly smug look. “And I’m guessing from your hesitation that the results were favorable.”

 

Agatha makes a face at her, trying in vain to repress the slight flush that rises to her cheeks. For fuck’s sake, she’s nearly fifty, not some awkward high schooler being teased by her mother.

 

“You could say that, I guess.”

 

She glances over Lilia’s face, searching for disapproval or anger. Seeing her expression, Lilia takes her arm and squeezes it gently. In the blink of an eye, her smile has gone from amused to kind and reassuring, and it absolutely doesn’t make Agatha melt slightly. Just a touch.

 

“I’m inclined to have hope, dear. Jennifer may be a harder sell, but she’ll come around.”

 

Without turning around, Jen flips them both off. Lilia swats her hand admonishingly, rolling her eyes. “Behave.” 

 

She squeezes Agatha’s arm once more as Jen takes the wallet back from the frustrated-looking receptionist. “You too. Take care with your heart, Agatha, and those of the people around you. So long as you do that…I think we’ll be alright.”

 

Agatha pats her hand, managing a half-hearted eye roll of her eyes. She’s horrified to find that those eyes are tearing up, and hastily swipes them away before Jen notices. She’s certainly not in the mood to deal with that mockery.

 

Jennifer herself- who’s migrated to the double doors with a nurse- waves them over impatiently. “C’mon. Teen’s waiting for us.” 

 

~

 

Sure enough, Teen is up and waiting for them when they make it back to his room. Though he looks incredibly sheepish, he also appears relatively unharmed, aside from a bruise on his head and a few small scrapes along his arms. The real kicker, however, is a nasty-looking gash along his ribs. There’s a young-looking doctor sewing it up, but he pauses once the women enter. 

 

“Uh, hi. Can I help-” 

 

Ignoring him, Jen shoves her way in to examine Billy herself. She scoffs when she examines the stitches on his torso, raising a disbelieving eyebrow at the doctor. “These are awful . Did you sleep through the wound care unit in med school?”

 

The young man bristles. “Excuse me? Ma’am, stitches never look pretty at first, but I assure you they’re perfectly adequate.”

 

“No, they aren’t!” Jen snaps, and throws a hand up to stop him when he tries to protest. “Put your eyes back in your head and look for a minute. The edges aren’t even at all, and the sutures are way too close to the surface. You keep going like that and it’s going to leave a massive scar. I’m sure you’re freshly graduated and riding the high of passing your boards, but if your ego is still too puffed up to fix it, go get another damn doctor.”

 

The man leaves in a huff and Agatha smirks after him. “ ‘Atta girl, Jen. Tell him how you see it.”

 

Lilia pats her shoulder proudly, and Jen preens. She moves without protest when Agatha shoulders past, perching on the side of the cot with a grinning Billy. His smile fades into something a bit more…apprehensive, however, as she takes his face in her hands and turns it back and forth.

 

“You alright? Nothing broken? No brain injury?”

 

“Nothing broken,” Teen replies slowly, following her guiding hands. “And they said no concussion.” 

 

She spots the small bruise on his chin and frowns. Spotting her furrowed brow and no doubt ascertaining what the look means, he hastily carries on. “It was just a stupid accident, Agatha, I swear! A couple friends and I were messing around with a couple toy swords while we were working sets, and I tripped backwards into one that wasn’t fully secured.” He folds his arms stubbornly, looking her in the eyes. “Don’t go chew out my teacher. I’m fine. ”

 

Agatha examines him for a moment longer ( satisfying an old itch of anxiety, one that reminds her of the days when Nicky was fresh from the hospital and she had been terrified of something, anything being wrong), then lets go and claps him on the shoulders. “Good! Then you’re grounded for your little stunt this morning. And don’t play dumb, Alice ratted you out.”

 

Teen groans, but wisely doesn’t argue. “Okay. For how long?”

 

She taps her chin, tilting her head theatrically. “I’d say…however long Alice thinks, since it was her you framed. Sound fair?”

 

“I didn’t frame her!” Teen protests, at the same time as Jen scoffs. 

 

“Fair, my ass. Alice is a soft touch and you both know it.”

 

“True,” Agatha hums. She gives Teen a wicked smirk, letting the boy stew for a moment. She’s not mad, not really- she doesn’t think she could manage mad after the morning she’s had, even if she’d wanted to- but it wouldn’t do to let the kid know that. 

 

“Maybe I should ask Rio for her opinion. Thoughts on that?” She lets Billy squirm for a moment longer before sighing dramatically, sticking her hands in her pockets. “Then again, she’ll probably let you off easy too. We did have a nice little chat this morning. She’s probably in a good mood.”

 

Her kid’s face- her kid, and for the first time, she notices she doesn’t feel a pang of guilt at the thought. Probably hasn’t in a long time, if she stops to think about it, but the realization still feels damn good - breaks into a grin. She rolls her eyes, but lets a smile tug at the corners of her own lips. When he throws his arms around her, pressing his not-bleeding side against hers, it breaks free entirely. 

 

Teen rests his chin on her shoulder, and Agatha tilts her head to let him whisper in her ear. “You’re happy?”

 

“Mmhm.”

 

He nods, squeezing her tighter. “Good. Don’t let it make you stupid.”

 

Agatha snorts, pushing him lightly off as she sees another doctor approaching. This one is female, and she notices with satisfaction that she has the younger doctor trailing at her heels like a cowed puppy. 

 

“Thought that’s supposed to be my line. I’m the adult here, you know.”

 

“Oh, please. ” Billy rolls his eyes. Then he laughs and ducks as Agatha promptly swats him on the arm.

 

“Watch your damn attitude, Teenager.”

 

“Okay, okay! Sorry.”

 

Agatha rolls her own eyes, but lets him lean on her shoulder as the new doctor swabs the cut with antiseptic again (for caution’s sake). She gives him her hand when the needles come back out, and he clutches it tightly as the thread slowly pulls skin back together. 

 

“Doing great, kiddo.” Agatha eyes Jen’s face, glad to find a note of approval in her expression. “Almost over.”

 

Teen nods, sighing in relief when they’re done. He tucks his head against Agatha’s neck as the doctor cleans and inspects her work. Agatha pats his shoulder, resting her chin on the top of his head.

 

“Oh, and Teen?”

 

“Yeah?”

 

She thinks back to the messages he’d used Alice’s phone to send, and smiles.

 

“I love you too.”




~

 

On her way into the station, in what has to be up there in the list of most inconvenient coincidences she’s experienced, Rio brushes elbows with the man she and Alice had tried and failed to put away. He’s scrawny, gaunt, and greasy all over, with an unpleasant smirk that says I think I’m smarter than you, and you can’t convince me otherwise. He is also, in her professional opinion, a murderer- to be specific, a murderer of pregnant mistresses. But according to their wiseass ADA, he’s a bit too anal of a killer to go to court and win. So she smiles coolly and lets him go, staring into those wild-animal eyes until he’s gone. Then she turns on her heel and heads off to find her partner, leaving him behind.

 

He troubles her slightly, yes, but not enough to really worry. He’s not a real threat. Not to her, anyways, though they may have to keep an eye out on his domestic situation until they finally manage to get their case settled. 

 

But those are thoughts for later.

 

Alice is waiting for her at her desk, staring down their case files like she hopes a hidden picture will reveal itself to her if she just stares long enough. She glances up, however, when she hears Rio approaching. 

 

“Hey. Listen, I, uh…I know this morning wasn’t really…” She winces, rubbing the back of her neck. “God, I’m completely butchering this. Rio, I-”

 

It’s only been a minute, but she can’t help it any longer.

 

Alice yelps when she surges forward, and Rio realizes belatedly that she’d probably expected her federal partner to hit her. Instead, she throws her arms around her in a massive bear hug, feeling her face split into an overjoyed grin as she pulls the detective in close. 

 

Alice lets out a little squeak of surprise, muffled against her shoulder.

 

“Whoa! Uh, hi?” She lifts her own arms, then hesitates, hovering them awkwardly over Rio’s back. “I, uh…I’m gonna guess it went well, then?”

 

Rio snorts. “Yes, it went well. Now stop waving your hands around and hug me back.”

 

Alice bursts out laughing and does just that. Rio beams, rocking back and forth on the tips of her toes. 

 

She’s so happy. She can’t remember the last time she’s been this happy. 

 

Alice pulls away slightly, though she doesn’t let go. Rio doesn’t either. She’s nice to hug, Rio has decided. Not as nice as Agatha- no one compares to Agatha- but her smile is just as warm. “So…”

 

Her lips are going to split from grinning, Rio is sure of it. “Baby steps, baby steps. But we’re talking. Really talking, and we haven’t done that in…”

 

She makes an odd, noncommittal noise, and Alice laughs again. They’re giggling like a couple of teenage girls, the pair of them. The thought makes Rio- who hadn’t had many friends to giggle with in her teenage years- even giddier.

 

Alice pulls her back in, resting her chin on her shoulder. “You’re happy?” she whispers softly. Rio squeezes her shoulders. 

 

“So happy.”

 

“And Agatha seems happy?”

 

Rio bumps her cheek against the top of Alice’s head. If she squeezes her any tighter, she’s going to leave bruises. “Sí.”

 

“Then I’m happy for you,” Alice murmurs. “Both of you.”

 

“Happy enough to break the news to Jen?”

 

Alice snorts. “Dream on, Agent. But considering it was Teen’s suggestion, I think she’ll be a little more forgiving.”

 

Rio blinks, pushing away to look her in the face. “Wait, what?”

 

Alice laughs. “You didn’t know? I thought for sure you would have figured it out by now. Yeah, Billy was behind the whole stunt. I guess he overheard the voicemail you left me the other night and decided to take matters into his own hands.” She shakes her head, looking half exasperated and half amused. “From what I heard, he booked the appointment online, then swiped my phone this morning while I was asleep. I honestly don’t know whether I should be mad or impressed.”

 

Rio shrugs slightly, still processing- though now that she thinks about it, it makes a certain amount of sense. Alice is no stickler for grammar, but those texts certainly hadn’t read like her. There had been a very teenage aspect to them, no doubt about it. Really, the whole plot was just a very juvenile thing, wasn’t it? All the more reason to be somewhat amazed by its success.

 

“Mad to his face, impressed in a few years when you can laugh about it?” She’s certainly impressed. Alice rolls her eyes, but she’s still grinning.

 

 “That sounds about right. He’s a good kid, but man, he does some stupid stuff sometimes.” She rolls her eyes again, but fondly this time. “You know, I think when you finally get to meet him, you’ll really li-“

 

She cuts herself off mid-sentence, eyes going wide. “What the- shit! Get down !” 

 

Pop!

 

Something whistles past her head.

 

She doesn’t have her gun. Of course she doesn’t. Are guns even allowed in couples therapy? God knows they shouldn’t be. And Alice must know that too, because she’s tackling her before she can even glance around. With Rio still in her arms, she spins them both around, taking her down to the ground with her.

 

 Pop! Pop!

 

They hit the ground with a harsh smack! as people around them start to shout. Alice lets out a strangled gasp as she thumps down on top of Rio, and Rio herself can barely hear her own yelp as her body bounces off the tile floor- which hurts far more than it has any right to. You’re getting too old for this, a voice in her head whispers, and she almost giggles . Minds under stress work differently, of course, but that’s certainly a new one for her. 

 

Boots thunder around her as a couple of their colleagues come running past. Alice braces her elbows on either side of Rio’s head with a wince ( Had she landed on something sensitive?) and tucks her chin down, shielding both their heads. Someone she can barely see from this angle- a woman with tan skin and dark, curly hair- raises her own service weapon and fires back. This shot isn’t a pop, but a bang!, leaving her ears ringing and her head throbbing. She fires once more, twice more- bang! bang! - and then-

 

The gunshots stop. All the gunshots stop. 

 

Rio exhales sharply, briefly digging her fingers into Alice’s back. They’re both going to be sore later, there’s no doubt about that, but sore muscles and a bruise or two are hardly her biggest priority right now.

 

  It certainly could have been worse. Thank god Alice had been holding her when the shooter- had it been their perp? It must have, there’s no way that was a coincidence-had opened fire. Thank god she’d even been looking. If she hadn’t moved so quickly…

 

The female cop crouches over them. She’s asking if they’re alright, though her own ears are also no doubt still ringing too much to really hear the answer. Rio opens her mouth to tell her that they’re fine, that neither of them had been hurt, thank god- thank Alice- but that they should probably make a call or two before Jen or Billy hears it on the news. Before she can, however, the detective’s eyes go wide. 

 

“ Shit! Someone get a bus!”

 

And then slowly, belatedly, she realizes there’s something warm and wet coating her hands. She lifts one, almost amazed, and finds it streaked scarlet. As the officer raises her radio and shouts into it, shouts loud enough for the entire precinct to hear, she notices hot tears running down her cheeks. She’s not sure whether they’re hers or Alice’s. She’s not sure it matters. All that matters is that she’s lying on the ground, and Alice is lying on the ground with her, looking down at her with panicked eyes, and the adrenaline is wearing off just enough to feel her body starting to ache, her head to throb, her heart to race, and por dios, her hands are covered in blood.

 

“ We have an officer down! I repeat, officer down!”




~

Though Teen gets a clean bill of health after the doctor gives him his wound care instructions, it still takes them over an hour to finally be discharged. During that time, his various guardians start up a lecture on D-I-Y safety and theater etiquette and not being an absolute idiot- one that continues all the way down the hall, and which is absolutely unintelligible due to their unfortunate habit of talking over one another (which had led to more than a few board games being banned from their household) . When they finally all stop for breath at once, Billy shrugs sheepishly and gives them a tentative grin. “Well…it’s kind of like a sitcom. All’s well that ends well, right?”

 

The adults promptly explode into a symphony of scolding all over again, and he ducks his head in apology. Lilia actually wags her finger at him like she’s lecturing a naughty puppy. 

 

“ …ever again. You understand us, young man?”

 

The young man in question rubs the back of his neck awkwardly. “Not really, but I think I got the gist of it. I’m sorry, guys.”

 

Jen crosses her arms as they enter the main lobby of the hospital (stopping at the front desk so that Agatha can finish the discharge paperwork). “I ought to call Alice so she can chime in too. Where is she, anyways? Did anybody update her?”

 

Agatha flips the page with a dramatic roll of her eyes. “I dunno, but Rio said they had a meeting later today. Reading between the lines, I think their case is falling apart. They’re probably off to either petition the courts or arrest their charge for something else to stall for time.” She groans softly. “Maybe they’ve got the right idea. I’d rather deal with an angry judge or some creepy crook than this mountain of red tape. Seriously, am I getting my kid medical care or selling my soul to the devil?” 

 

Jen snorts, leaning on the counter next to her. “The real things they teach you in med school.”

 

“So do they teach you how to avoid it?”

 

“Yeah, by teaching your kids to have a little common sense.”

 

“Oh, you want to blame me? This was very clearly a group effort. ”

 

“You- huh?”

 

Jen cuts herself off with a surprised murmur. She grabs her phone- ringing merrily- out of her purse. “Wha-”

 

Lilia’s starts to chime, as if responding to Jen’s. She glances around at the beauty guru, alarm starting to bloom in her face. Jen, for her part, looks up at Agatha. After a moment, her phone goes off as well, completing the dreaded chorus. 

 

Jen fumbles to answer it, but the wail of sirens drowns her out as an ambulance whips into the bay outside. The next second, Lilia’s shriek of horror makes whatever she’s going to ask redundant. 


Agatha sees it at the same time Jen does. Dimly, in some corner of her mind, she recognizes that she’s pulling Billy to her, attempting to turn him away so he can’t see.  

 

Too late. He sees what she does. 

 

The gurney seems to slow down as it exits the ambulance and whips through the lobby. The paramedic on it- a black woman in her forties, maybe- is straddling a dark-haired, unconscious woman, her hands working furiously even as her coworkers haul them both past the desk and into the hospital. She presses down on the woman’s chest as she passes them and bright red blood spurts out from under her hands, splattering both Agatha’s face and Jen’s pretty white pantsuit. The latter woman gives a cry of horror- not at the ruining of her clothes, but at the moment she catches sight of the patient’s face. 

 

“ Ohmygod-!”

 

She all but collapses into Lilia’s arms as doctors come rushing past, whisking the woman away to some hell unknown. A trembling Teen clutches at Agatha, who cradles him against her with one arm. 

 

“ Agatha? Agatha, what’s going on?! ”

 

In disbelief, she fishes her own phone out of her pocket. In addition to the now missed call- New Jersey State Police, and her heart sinks- she has a couple missed texts. She flicks them open with one hand, the messages all but confirming what she already knew.

 

1:17 PM  

 

Maybe: Rio Vidal sent you three new messages. 

 

Rio Vidal: I’m so sorry 

 

Rio Vidal: Lo siento, amor. Lo siento. 

 

Rio Vidal: Te amo 



Chapter End Notes

You know it's bad when even my best friend and beta reader of over a decade is shocked-

Chapter 19: Interlude- The Moment After

Chapter Notes

The full chapter isn't done yet, nor have I gone through and replied to comments- so if you want to wait for things to REALLY resolve a touch, feel free to wait a bit longer and read both at once when they come out 😉 however, I know the whole not knowing thing from the last chapter was stressing some people out. Thus...an extra few pages of angst.

Because, you know, there wasn’t already enough of that.

(All jokes aside, this chapter was difficult to write (both grammatically and emotionally) and even more difficult to read back. There are some episodes of TV shows that sometimes you just have to skip, and if this chapter is like that, I promise I won’t be offended)

TWs for: blood, injury, gun violence, mentions of death, and just...some generalized sadness. As per usual, I am NOT a doctor or law enforcement officer, and not everything will be accurate.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The shock only lasts a moment. It has to, because she’s yanked out of it as abruptly as it had set in. Hands are on the pair of them, prying them apart, rolling their wounded warrior away and onto her side to get a look at the injury - and suddenly she comes back to life, thrashing like a trapped animal to get back to her. 

 

She’s free in an instant and crouching over her fallen partner, shoving away more insistent hands that try to get in her way. Someone’s torn her shirt open to get a look at the damage, but there’s blood, so much blood, too much to see. God, why do gunshots bleed so much? Why do wounds look so much worse when they’re worn on the skin of a friend?

 

She snatches a wad of bandages from a first aid kit that’s materialized nearby, pressing it to the apex of the red and wincing when she whimpers. 

 

“I know, baby, I know. Deep breaths. You can get through this.”

 

She shakes her head weakly, looking over her shoulder with big, frightened eyes. “Can’t…”

 

“You can.”

 

She shakes her head again. “No, I can’t- I- can’t breathe .” 

 

Shit .

 

Bad. Bad, bad, bad. She shuffles over, takes her head in her lap and sees more blood dribbling from her lips. Worse. Worse, worse, worse. Where the hell is that ambulance?

 

On the ground, her friend coughs, tries again to speak. She hushes her when she fails, gently turning her and pulling her into her arms- not strictly protocol, but there’s too much blood and too little breath, and she’s very, very afraid for her. One hand comes in to help, but only to offer a fresh press of gauze just below her heaving shoulders. They back away once it’s steady and in place, giving the pair plenty of space.

 

 No one interferes. No one tries to take her. Good. She’d kill anyone who did. 

 

In her arms, her partner chokes and whimpers. She props her head up hastily on one arm, helping her cough out a fresh glob of blood. It’s dark, dark coppery red. That’s bad, isn’t it? She should know. She doesn’t know. 

 

Tears are soaking into her shirt sleeve now, though she tries and fails to wipe them away. She looks up to her eyes, and the pain in her face goes beyond the shot.

 

“S’bad?”

 

“No.”

 

A weak, halfhearted laugh. “ Liar. And you…call me…”

 

“ No .” She presses their foreheads together. “You’re alright. You’re going to be okay.”

 

“No.”

 

“Yes.”

 

“M’dying.”

 

“You’re not. ” She strokes her hair with trembling hands. “You’re staying right here.”

 

“Trying. Believe me, I-” She interrupts herself to suck in air, hungrily, desperately. She can’t seem to get enough to talk. Bad, bad, bad.

 

 “…don’t wanna die. I…don’t want to die.” 

 

“You’re not going to. Not for a long, long, long time.  And not here, not like this.” She tries for a reassuring smile. “Can’t die protecting me. Agatha would kill me.”

 

Another choked laugh. “She would. She- she…” 

 

She’s gasping now, skin slowly turning a waxy pale. Every breath is rattly and liquid, and she knows damn well that their window is closing, closing quickly. She gathers her up and holds her closer, as if by clinging to her, she can shield her from the slow, creeping death crawling about through her lungs.

 

“Shh-shh-shh. Save your breath, cariño. Just stay with us. You have a family to get home to, remember?”

 

“Family…” Her eyes are starting to go frighteningly dull, but a small spark pops into them at the mention of her loved ones. “ Teen. Is he…?”

 

“I’m sure he’s alright. Theater kids have had worse accidents.” 

 

“Check? Please?”

 

She relents. She can’t tell her no, not now, not like this.

 

 “Okay, okay. Where’s your phone?”

 

“Front pocket.”

 

Her heart sinks when she fishes it out and finds the screen shattered- probably from the impact of tackling her to the ground. Hope springs anew, however, when she recognizes the plain black plastic of her work cell, rather than the studded leather case she keeps on her own private phone.

 

“Your- oye, stay awake!” Brown eyes flutter open with a slightly sheepish smile and she exhales sharply in relief. “Good, good. Where’s your personal?”

 

Her head jerks towards her jacket, still draped over her desk chair. A nearby officer scrambles to retrieve the phone, hastily waving it in front of her face to unlock it. Her partner snatches it away to open her recent messages.

 

“Look, look. Just some stitches. He’s okay, see?” 

 

There are sirens whirling closer, louder, louder, closer, then finally brighter through the mist-clogged windows- but those teary brown eyes are dulling once more, and she has the sinking fear that they might be too late. She squeezes her arm, strokes her hair, shows her random messages in her phone- anything to keep her partner and friend awake, alert, alive. A photo of Agatha and the kid, cuddled together on the table and laughing- no doubt while they waited for the doctor- at least pulls a small smile from her, and that’s good, isn’t it?

 

“They’re…good. All of them…”

 

“Lo sé.” 

 

“Will…you…”

 

She coughs. A glance at the door confirms that paramedics are pulling up and thank god, thank all the gods, because the blood still isn’t slowing, and she’s getting far too pale.

 

“Will you…take my…my…if I don’t make it, then-”

 

“Hush. You’re going to survive this.”

 

“If I…don’t.” She’s crying again- probably hasn’t fully stopped, and who can blame her?- but her expression is determined. Determined to get these words out, even if they’re her last. Especially if they’re her last. “Will you…take me home to them? Please?”

 

She squeezes her hand, brushes it against her lips as someone with a stretcher rushes towards them with a shout. “I will.”

 

“Pro…mise?”

 

“I swear it.”

 

Then the paramedics are there, and she’s gone from her arms. They’re whisking her away before she can even demand to go with her, leaving her alone once more. After a moment, her captain- a tall, gruff woman as stoic as a cliffside- puts a hand on her shoulder. She whips around, glares like a coiled, cornered viper- no one in this station has dared touch her outside of the ( dying- no, no, no, she can’t think like that) detective she’d been assigned to ride with- but she must be losing her touch, because the captain doesn’t pull away. 

 

“I’m…going to call her emergency contacts, unless you’d like to.” Upon the glare deepening, that stoic face softens slightly. “I know you two have become close. And I’m glad for it, honestly. Poor girl’s had a string of bad luck when it comes to partners.” Large, calloused fingers squeeze her shoulder in an unusually gentle move. “You’ve been one of her better pairings, maybe even one of the best. So if you want to call her loved ones, I won’t begrudge you that.” 

 

And fuck, if that doesn’t hit hard. She sits back on her haunches and wipes at her eyes, trying not to fall into hysteria. 

 

“They won’t want to hear it from me.”

 

The captain, though she certainly doesn’t know why- she’d better not know why- nods understandingly and turns away. Though part of her longs to chase after her, shrieks at her to make the calls herself, her body stays stubbornly behind. 

 

They won’t want to hear it from me. 

 

After a moment, however, the captain hollers in that none of her calls have gone through- and that’s when she remembers the accident in the theater, the angry call to the school secretary, the trip to the hospital. The damned hospital, which isn’t far, she knows- and Jen’s latest message had said they were still filling out discharge paperwork.

 

 Are they still there? Will they have to see?

 

The captain will leave messages, no doubt. And still, she grabs her phone and sends a handful of her own.
