Chapter 20

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

~

Beep!

 

The shrill, repeated chirp of the monitors is the only thing breaking the silence. 

 

Beep!

 

Jen sniffles again. Lilia puts an arm around her, her own eyes welling up.

 

“She’s strong. She-”

 

“Don’t!” Agatha snaps at her. “ Don’t . Don’t do that.”

 

Lilia goes quiet.

 

Beep!

 

“How the hell did this happen?” Jen whispers to the room at large. “How?” 

 

Lilia draws her down to her level, enough to press their heads gently together.. “We always knew it was a risk, Jennifer. There’s always-”

 

“No, we didn’t!” Jen bursts out. “I mean, of course we knew it could happen, but we were never prepared for this! How is anyone prepared?! How could anyone be- be- ”

 

She sobs. Lilia kisses her forehead.

 

Beep!

 

Teen glances around at them all, his eyes red and puffy. “She’s gonna be okay, right? She’s gotta be.” 

 

No one answers him. His lip trembles and he shuffles a little closer to the bed. “Alice, come on. Please wake up. Please.”

 

Agatha closes her eyes, then opens them reluctantly. At her side, Billy grasps Alice’s hand, his fingers trembling when they brush one of the wires attached to her wrist.

 

“You can’t go. Alice, you can’t go .”

 

He looks helplessly at his godmother. At a loss, she simply wraps her arm around him. 

 

“It’s gonna be alright, kid. It’ll be okay.”

 

Teen clutches Alice’s hand a little tighter, looking up at Agatha with a kind of frightened, child-like pleading that stings her heart. “Agatha, she’s not going to die, right?”

 

Jen makes a strangled little mewling noise, pressing her fingers to her lips. Agatha exhales sharply, glancing over the monitors. “I don’t know, Teen. I wish I did.”

 

He sniffles. “She can’t die. It’s not fair.”

 

“It’s not.”

 

“It’s not fair, Agatha.” 

 

“I know. I know it’s not.” She rubs his arm. “Death isn’t fair, Billy. We look for reasons and explanations in the universe, but the truth is…sometimes people just die. And there’s nothing we can do about it.”

 

Teen buries his face in her shoulder, tears beginning to stain her sweater. “I can’t lose her too. I can’t handle it, Agatha, I can’t.”

 

“You could. It’d be hard as all get out, but you’d survive. Just like you did before.” She presses her cheek against his forehead. “The first time you feel it, grief can feel like the biggest, scariest monster you’ve ever seen. You’ll want nothing but to run like hell from it, because it’ll seem like it’ll kill you if you stop. And yeah, it will knock you on your ass a time or two, but you’ll get back up and try again. And it’ll get easier to do each time.”

 

Billy doesn’t answer her, but he does nod minutely against her arm. She hums softly. “Take it from me, bud, you won’t ever stop missing her. I know you know that as well as I do. But every day, it’ll start to hurt less and less to think of her. Before you know it, you’ll be living your life again.”

 

She leans her forehead against his. “You’ll feel bad about it at first, for sure. Sometimes you’ll feel good, then feel guilty for being happy while someone you loved so much is in the ground. But there’ll be a day when you look around and realize you don’t feel like that anymore.”

 

Teen lifts his head from her shoulder slightly. “And you’re ready to move on?”

 

“You don’t move on, Teen. So don’t ever try and force yourself to. It’ll only hurt more.” Agatha closes her eyes. “Moving on…would imply leaving the people we lose in the past, and we don’t .” 

 

In her mind’s eye, she can see the losses of their lives- all of their lives. Wanda. Lorna. Tommy. Nicky. All the people their ragtag little family had loved and lost over their years, standing together, just out of reach. 

 

“We don’t move on, ” she repeats. “We move forward.”

 

Lilia reaches out and puts her free hand on the boy’s shoulder. “ And they don’t move on without us, either. If Alice dies- and I’m not saying she will, but if she does- her spirit will stay a step behind you along the road as you travel through life. Maybe out of sight, but not gone from your side. She won’t leave you, Billy. Not really.”

 

Teen nods. Though he doesn’t take his eyes off of Alice, it’s clear from the way he leans towards Lilia that he’s hanging onto her every word, desperate for any comfort as she continues quietly.

 

“The people we love don’t ever really leave us, dear. And neither does our love for them. Love doesn’t die, even if people do- it’s why it hurts so much to lose them.” Her eyes sparkle slightly, though she doesn’t let the tears fall just yet. “What is grief, if not love persevering?”

 

Teen turns to look at her, sniffing again softly. “My- my dad loved that show.”

 

Lilia gives his shoulder a squeeze. “So did the woman who raised me. I always thought it was so silly until the day she died. Now I think of it whenever I miss her.”

 

Billy nods, letting go of Agatha with his free hand and taking Lilia’s instead. “Me too.” 

 

The group lapses into a pained quiet, listening to the beep of the monitors and the quiet rasp of Alice’s breathing. The blue-green green plastic of the oxygen mask makes her face look even more pale, standing out awkwardly against the crimson streaks in her hair. Agatha has the sickening thought that the color is incredibly similar to the blood that had been spilling from the bullet hole in her back. 

 

Jen reaches out and tweaks the mask just slightly, stroking her fingers along Alice’s cheek as she pulls away. She doesn’t quite look Agatha in the eye, but she clearly catches the questioning glance she gets nonetheless.

 

“It- it was crooked.” 

 

Her hands are shaking, Agatha notices, even as she takes Alice’s free one in both of hers. For the first time, she realizes that Jen is still wearing the bloodstained pantsuit that had gotten ruined when first responders had rushed Alice into the hospital. 

 

She’s a wreck. And who can blame her?

 

Agatha sighs, reaching across the bed and nudging Jen. “Hey. It’s been a long day, it’s going to be a longer night, and sitting here isn’t going to make it go any faster. Get out of here for a bit, okay?” 

 

Jennifer opens her mouth and Agatha promptly covers it with her palm, earning herself a startled squawk of indignation. “ Seriously, Jen. Go home, take a shower, and get some rest. Take Teen with you.”

 

“I can hear you”, the young man protests. “I want to stay with Alice!”

 

“No,” Agatha replies firmly. “ I’ll stay with Alice. You two need to get out of this place for a bit. You too, Lilia.”

 

Jen- twisting away from Agatha’s hand with a disgusted look on her face- looks ready to argue as well, but Lilia puts a hand on her arm. Jen looks around, the anger in her expression fading into something lost, confused, and incredibly, deeply hurt. Like a frightened little girl, searching for guidance from anyone who might know better.

 

  Lilia squeezes her gently, but fixes her eyes firmly on Agatha’s. “You’ll look after her? And call us if anything changes?”

 

“The very second. Cross my heart.” 

 

Lilia nods solemnly. “Alright, then.” She takes Jen’s hand and gently pulls her to her feet. “Come on. Destroying ourselves won’t do Alice any good. And you really should change clothes.”

 

Jennifer- in an uncharacteristic move for her- lets herself be led without a fight. Lilia pats her back, then turns and gestures for Teen to follow her. 

 

“Come on, kiddo. You too.” 

 

The boy gulps back a protest and stands obediently, taking her free arm. He starts to leave the room with her, but suddenly pulls away and doubles back. As the adults in the room look on, he bends carefully and kisses Alice’s forehead. 

 

“Please hang on, okay? Please. For us.”

 

Her eyelids flutter briefly and the room sucks in a collective inhale. But she doesn’t stir, and this time Jen moves over and puts an arm around Teen. “C’mon. I…I bet the bunny’s getting lonely. And probably wondering where his dinner is.” 

 

It’s exactly the right thing to coax him on his way- something Alice would have said, had she been conscious. She’s not entirely sure if Jen had meant to emulate her or not, but regardless, it does the trick. Billy touches Alice’s shoulder one last time, then follows the other two out of the room and down the hall, leaving Agatha alone with their comatose friend. 

 

Nothing left to talk to but the damned monitors.

 

She takes Alice’s hand, cupping it in both of hers. It’s an odd feeling, touching someone so limp. So lifeless. Agatha shudders, then squeezes lightly in hopes that Alice will somehow miraculously squeeze back. 

 

She doesn’t. Agatha has the sinking feeling that she may never do it again. 

 

“You better prove me wrong,” she mutters. She squeezes Alice’s hand again. “I don’t care that you can’t hear what I’m thinking, and I don’t care that you don’t exactly have much say in it. You better not die, Alice, because I just lied to Billy- and I don’t know if you remember, but I sort of promised to stop doing that. I told him he’d survive, but…I honestly don’t know if he would. I…don’t know if we would.”

 

She laughs to herself mirthlessly, like she’s trying to make herself feel better. It doesn’t work. She hadn’t really expected it to.



“Look, you’ll never catch me saying something this sappy out loud again, so you’d better listen close. I don’t know about you, but this household is the closest I’ve gotten to having a real family in a long, long time. But we need you. I need you to keep me from landing my ass in jail, Lilia needs you to keep Jen and I from driving her insane, and I can’t think of another woman who’s managed to put up with Jenny Kale for that long and actually still like her, much less look at her the way you do. Don’t think I don’t see that, by the way, you’re not subtle. And as for Teen…” 

 

She swallows thickly, harshly, painfully.  “That kid loves you so, so fucking much, Al. Losing you would break his heart, and that kid can’t take much more heartbreak. So don’t you dare die on us, Alice Wu-Gulliver. Don’t you dare. You understand me?”

 

Alice, of course, doesn’t answer her. Agatha sighs, running her thumbs along the back of her friend’s hands. She almost wishes Lilia or Jennifer had stayed after all. Though she’s technically not on her own, the silence that comes after she speaks is suffocating. She’s suddenly wanting badly for someone to talk to, someone who can talk back . The hospital is full of people and noise, but here in this terrible limbo- waiting to see where the wind blows, how the dice fall, where the road ends- she feels terribly, horribly alone.

 

Except she isn’t alone, isn’t she?

 

Agatha sits up in her seat, closes her eyes. “I know you’re there,” she murmurs. “You can come in.”

 

There’s that familiar tap of heels, and the smell of rain washes over her.

 

“Lo siento. I wasn’t sure I would be welcome.”

 

Agatha reaches back unconsciously, fingers brushing her shoulder, searching for a hand that isn’t there yet. Sensing the invitation, Rio slips her own under them, resting them on her arm. 

 

“Alice wouldn’t mind. She likes you, for some reason.”

 

Rio’s nails dig into her shoulder. Agatha digs her own into her knuckles in response, and her ex…- ex-ex-lover? Not a great time to be thinking about where they stand- squeezes her in apology.

 

“How is she? The captain said she was out of surgery when I called, but she didn’t know…”

 

Agatha purses her lips, fingers tightening on Alice’s. “It’s…pretty bad, babe. Thank fuck the bullet missed her spine, but the surgeon said it fragmented when it passed through her ribcage, and the shards embedded themselves in her lungs. Did some serious damage there. Shit, she was nearly blue in the face by the time paramedics got her here.”  She shakes her head gently to and fro. “Doctors say she’s stable, but critical. Means that they went in and patched up what was immediately killing her for now, but there’s an endless amount of shit that could go wrong from here. She could have a stroke, start choking on her own blood, or…she could just stop breathing for no reason at all.”

 

She swallows, hating the bitter taste of those damn words in her mouth. Rio reaches out and touches Alice’s cheek, keeping her other hand on Agatha’s shoulder. “Her chances?”

 

Agatha opens her mouth. Closes it. Tries again, and fails again. Rio rubs her shoulder to stop her.

 

“¿Tan mal?”

 

Agatha nods. “Yeah.” Her voice cracks slightly. She turns her head into Rio’s arm, muffling her words. “Yeah.” 

 

Rio squeezes her gently. “I’ve seen officers get hurt like this before, sweetheart. Her odds get better every hour.”

 

Agatha laughs humorlessly. “Doesn’t mean much when they were shitty to begin with, does it?”

 

“No.”

 

“She could die.”

 

“I know.”

 

“She could die, Rio.”

 

“I know.”

 

The monitor beeps. Rio starts to stroke her hair. 

 

“She saved my life once. Did you know that?”

 

Agatha keeps her face buried in Rio’s arm. Still, without looking, she can tell Rio is considering. 

 

“I think so. If we’re talking about the night I’m thinking of, anyways.” Her grip on Agatha’s shoulder tightens slightly. “That night is a blur to me, though.” 

 

Agatha nods. “Same here. But I remember Alice.” She smiles bitterly, peeking out from Rio’s arm. “She would have been a rookie then, I suppose. Sometimes I forget how much younger she is. But she had the guts of a veteran, didn’t she?”

 

Most of the night is a blur in her mind, it’s true. But try as she might to forget it, she remembers every second on that bridge. Parking her car at the edge- she’d turned her hazards on first, and remembers thinking it was so silly- finding a point she knew had been rusting and kicking out the rail, hovering on the edge as Jen cried her name over the roar of the rain- and of course, she remembers Alice. Alice’s voice- nervous, but kind, so kind- as she edged closer to her, Alice’s gentle hand on her shoulder- and Alice’s arms around her waist, pulling her away just as she toppled over the edge. She’d thrown them both backwards, pulling Agatha down on top of her. And she’d held her then, held her tight and whispered comforts that fell on a stranger’s deaf ears even as she fought- even as she clawed at her arms, even as she’d finally started to break down. 

 

“She saved my life,” she murmurs again, and fights back the sting in her eyes.

 

Rio squeezes her tightly, and Agatha glances up to see her own barely restrained tears reflected in those brown eyes. “Mine too. Today.”

 

Oh god. Agatha closes her eyes, exhaling sharply as the pieces slide into place. 

 

“God, of course she did. She would throw herself in front of a bullet to protect someone, wouldn’t she? That’s just like her.”

 

Stupid Alice. Stupid, brave Alice. Stupid brave, kind, selfless Alice. 

 

She purses her lips and digs her nails into Rio’s hand once more. She’s going to leave marks. She doesn’t care. She knows Rio doesn’t either. “Tell me they at least got the person who did it.”

 

“Agatha…”

 

“ Tell me.” She pulls away and turns to look Rio in the face, sudden fire burning in her chest. “And feel free to lie if you have to, because if the cops don’t have him, I’m going to hunt him down myself.”

 

“Don’t need to,” Rio says softly. “He’s dead.”

 

Agatha raises an eyebrow cautiously. “You’re sure?” 

 

Rio meets her eyes, nodding firmly. “Pronounced at the station, the absolute fool .” She scoffs, shaking her head. “Would’ve have walked out of there scot-free if his temper hadn’t gotten the better of him. Pendejo. ”

 

Perhaps an average person would miss the subtext in her statement. The average person however, hadn’t been married to a cop for over a decade. The average person hadn’t been Rio for over a decade.

 

 “You arrested him? That’s why he did it?”

 

“We did,” Rio mutters. “But he had a decent case and an even better lawyer, who would have had him out of our reach for good in only a matter of hours. God only knows why he made the choice he did.” 

 

There’s a touch of underlying anger in her tone, distress that Agatha knows well- the anguish of knowing someone had slipped through her fingers, one whose life she would only touch again when another’s had been broken. 

 

She squeezes her hand. Rio shakes her head minutely, a few strands of hair slipping free of their braid. They’re damp, Agatha notices- no, not just damp, soaked. She must have gone back to her room to shower and change, but clearly hadn’t bothered drying it before rushing to the hospital. And why would she, anyways? Appearances surely hadn’t been the first thing on her mind, not as she stood under the water and scrubbed her friend’s pain from her hands. 

 

She pictures flakes of dried blood whirling down the drain and nearly retches. 

 

After another long few moments, Agatha lets go of Alice’s hand and stretches over to grab a chair. She tugs Rio around and pats the seat, not bothering with a verbal request. She doesn’t need to. Rio sits obediently and drops her head onto her shoulder, shifting slightly to get comfortable- or at least, as comfortable as she can. Hospital furniture is awful .

 

With, seemingly, nothing else to say for the time being, silence takes over once more. Rio reaches out and takes Alice’s hand, holding it as gently as if she’s cradling a wounded bird. Agatha puts an arm around her shoulders and pulls her close, creating an odd link between the three of them, like an invisible shield against the suffocating air of the hospital- just barely preventing it from crushing them all.

 

Minutes tick, tick, tick away into hours. Agatha watches them pass by the clock on the wall, positioned perfectly for her to stare at from her place at the bedside. She wonders if whoever had designed the room had done that on purpose. If so, she thinks to herself, that person was one sadistic motherfucker.  

 

The hands turn stubbornly along, slowly blurring into one another as her eyes and mind tire. Clock-ticks melt from the wall, sliding down and pooling into the polka dots covering Alice’s hospital gown as her chest rises and falls, rises and falls. They tick away in harmony with each beat of her heart, counting away every second that she’s still breathing. 

 

She’s exhausted. Though she’s been watching the hours pass with hyper-focused eyes, it hasn’t fully processed in her mind how long she’s been here until- 

 

Rio’s nails dig into her shoulder, holding her upright. Agatha jolts, realizing with a start that she’s fallen about halfway out of her seat. Her sequined ass- she’s very much regretting that choice of clothing by now, thank you very much- is perched awkwardly on the edge of her uncomfortable hospital chair, and Rio is looking at her with a mixture of concern and confusion. 

 

“Ay mierda- ¿estás bien?”

 

“M’fine. Must’ve dozed off,” Agatha grunts. She shifts herself upright, putting her arm back around Rio. “Sorry, love. Good catch, by the way.”

 

Rio frowns at her. “Agatha, it is late. If you’re tired-”

“I’m not going home.”

 

“I wasn’t suggesting that,” Rio scolds lightly. “I was only going to tell you that you should sleep if you need to. I think there’s a cot in that closet there, most hospitals I’ve visited keep one.”

 

Agatha shakes her head, trying to get her bearings. “I- what?” 

 

“Pull out the cot and lie down ,” Rio says slowly. “You’ve been here for over twelve hours now, no? Some sleep will do you good.” 

 

Agatha rubs her eyes and glances at the clock- and shit, Rio is right. She’d arrived at the hospital for Teen at around noon, and it’s nearly one in the morning now. No wonder she’s so damn tired. 

 

Rio stands with a stretch, pads over to the closet and hauls out a lumpy-looking fold-a-bed. Despite the…odd shape and the suspiciously ratty sheet, even the sight makes her eyelids droop. Still, she glances warily over at Rio. 

 

“You gonna stay up with her?”

 

“Of course,” Rio murmurs. She pats the edge of cot. “Now come on.”

 

Agatha hesitates for a moment longer, bodily instincts warring with the nagging fear of sleeping through something serious ( a fear that’s followed her for years now ). Rio reaches over and takes her hand, giving it a gentle squeeze. 

 

“Trust me.”

 

She takes a deep breath, then lets it out through her nose. “Fine. Fine, fine, fine.”

 

Ignoring her grumbles, Rio offers her a hand up. Agatha wriggles around for a moment, trying to get comfortable. Once she’s settled, Rio rests a hand on her hair and starts to fuss with it. Agatha rolls her eyes- particularly once her elastic snaps and her hair tumbles loose around her shoulders. Then carefully trimmed nails are scratching away at her scalp, and she stops caring. 

 

She’s not exactly aware of falling asleep, but everything goes a bit…vague for a time. She doesn’t dream. At least, she thinks she doesn’t. In the back of her mind, she’s aware of Rio playing with her hair, the soft trill of the monitors, and the occasional murmur of nurses. At one point, she could swear she catches a glimpse of Wanda’s red hair at the very edges of her vision. At another, she opens her eyes and sees a woman with a face both familiar and unfamilar hovering at Alice’s bedside, one hand resting gently on her cheek. Her features, though relatively unmarred by age ( in fact, she can’t have been much older than Alice is now), are lined with a unique sort of grief, a pain that Agatha recognizes well. 

 

She should, after all. She sees it every time she looks in the mirror. 

 

Somewhere nearby, someone- Rio? - begins to sing quietly in Spanish. The woman cocks her head, glances around, and looks Agatha in the eyes. She nods once, solemnly, understandingly. Then she turns, bends, and kisses Alice on the forehead. Agatha blinks- seemingly in slow motion- and when her eyes shutter open once more, the woman is gone. 

 

Agatha closes her eyes again. Rio’s voice- she’s sure of that now- croons a soft, mournful lullaby, washing over her like the babbling of a river. She drifts in that song like she’s floating on gentle waves, cradled in the tender embrace of the melody as she slips to and fro through the land of the waking. A memory flits past the edge of her consciousness and she catches it in her fingers, cradling it in her palms and examining it carefully. 

 

“Should you really be singing that to him? Isn’t that story, like…super depressing?”

 

“Maybe. But there’s elements of love to the song all the same, don’t you think?” Rio gently bounces Nicky- bundled in a warm, silky-soft blanket gifted to him by Jennifer- in the crook of her arm, her eyes never leaving his face. “Love has a way of creeping into even the darkest of places. Like a spider, or a moss. Even the ugliest, angriest of pains have some roots in love.”

 

She has no argument. Or rather, she could , if she truly wanted to. But tonight, she’d rather listen. Rio shifts their baby boy in her arms and picks up her song once more, and she settles back to take it in. 

 

“Si porque te quiero, quieres, Llorona, que yo la muerte reciba. Si porque te quiero, quieres, Llorona, que yo la muerte reciba.”

 

Her rocking chair sways. Her eyes are drifting shut. 

 

 “Que se haga tu voluntad, Llorona, que muera por que otro viva. Que se haga tu voluntad, Llorona, que muera por que otro viva.”

 

Agatha opens her eyes. 

 

It must have at least been a few hours, because daylight is starting to creep in under the crack in the door. There’s no windows in the room, but she remembers the large, unshaded ones at he end of the hallway, and those must be leeching in some sun.

 

 At her side, Rio still sits in her place of vigilance. Her eyes are slightly red, but she seems otherwise largely unaffected by the lack of sleep. Impressively so, really.

 

Agatha sits up and kisses her cheek without thinking. How quickly she falls back into old habits. 

 

“Hey. How’re we looking this morning?”

 

Rio hums, squeezing Alice’s hand lightly. “The doctors came in the night and took a test or two, with favorable results. She’s breathing well, and the blood loss doesn’t appear to have caused brain damage.” She knocks her head lightly against Agatha’s shoulder like an affectionate cat. “Our girl seems to have taken a turn for the better, my love. I don’t want to jinx anything, but her chances of a full recovery are higher than ever.”

 

Agatha frowns. “Then why’s she still unconscious?”

 

“Her body has been through a major trauma. She’s just recuperating, that’s all.”

 

Agatha stretches with a groan. “Guess she does need the sleep.” She reaches down and pats Alice’s knee. “Still, I’d rather she wake up. Just for a little while, Al, c’mon.” 

 

No luck. Agatha sighs, wrapping an arm around Rio. “Worth a shot.” Then she considers and bites the inside of her cheek. “I’ve gotta stop saying that around the house for awhile, huh?”

 

Rio snorts and nestles against her side, still cradling Alice’s hand in hers. She tucks her cheek against Agatha’s neck and Agatha rests her chin on top of her head. After a long moment, she turns and murmurs into her ear. 

 

“Rio.”

 

“Mmh?” She’s sleepy. Agatha can’t blame her, she supposes, but she needs her awake for at least a few more minutes. There’s something that’s been nagging at the back of her mind since yesterday morning, and now that the constant adrenaline of the past day has finally started to cool, she can’t think about anything else. 

 

“You have to go back. To Washington.”

 

Rio sits up, blinking. “You want to talk about this now?” She glances down at Alice, lying prone in the bed. “ Here ?”

 

Agatha purses her lips. “I really don’t . But we shouldn’t leave her, and I can’t keep sitting on this.” 

 

She reaches out and brushes Alice’s hand with her own. “Besides, not to sound like a total cornball, but she sort of did give us each a new lease on life. We’ve fucked that up too many times now. At some point, it just gets rude.”

 

Rio nods slowly, rubbing her eyes. “Okay. I…okay, I guess that’s fair.” She sighs, tucking her ankles underneath her. “I will have to go back to Washington, yes. At some point.”

 

Agatha raises an eyebrow, trying to ignore the sinking in the pit of her stomach. “At some point?”

 

Rio absently sucks her lower lip into her mouth. “I can’t stay away forever. But…”

 

 “Finish your damn sentences, please.”

 

Most people would prickle at that. Rio simply rolls her eyes fondly. “ But Alice’s attacker was technically killed in an officer involved shooting. There will be an internal affairs hearing, of course, and I’ll need to hang around for that. And I imagine that the shooter will be posthumously indicted for her attempted murder, if not the murder we arrested him for.” She twists her fingers contemplatively in her sleeve. “ That will be the part that takes time. Alice will need to be well enough to testify, after all. And even after that, the wheels of justice turn slowly.”

 

Agatha wrinkles her nose. “Okay, so we have a few months. What then? Because in case you haven’t noticed, I’m not particularly geared towards the whole long distance thing.” 

 

Rio takes a quick, shallow breath and glances up to her. Her eyes are bright but anxious, shimmering with a curious mix of affection and trepidation. 

 

“I have an old friend in the New York field office who’s been trying to lure me there for years. He may or may not have made an offer when he heard I was in town, and I may or may not have expressed interest.” She shrugs. “Good pay, plenty of field work, and not the worst commute in the world. And I’d be back on homicide, which I’m told may actually be better for my mental health. Who’dve thought?”

 

Is forty-nine too young for a heart attack? She doesn’t think so. 

 

Agatha purses her lips, tosses her hair slightly in a halfhearted attempt to rifle out the tangles from the night. “So you’re saying what, exactly? That you’ve been fishing for a job around here?”

 

Rio rather abruptly stands, taking both her hands in hers. Agatha- struck by a sudden memory of when she’d proposed - follows, rather dumbstruck. 

 

“I’m saying that I will upend my entire life- social and work all at once- to quite literally move across the country if you ask me to, Agatha. So…” She squeezes Agatha’s hands. “Ask me.” 

 

She’s absolutely not too young for a heart attack. If that’s not it, then why does her heart feel as though it’s about to pound out of her chest?

 

“I have conditions.” 

 

“Name them.” 

 

Agatha turns her ex-wife’s hands in hers, running her fingers across her palms. There’s a new scar on her left one. She wonders where she’d gotten it. She resists the urge to kiss it. 

 

“We keep going to counseling. As much as I hate to admit it, that was the first time we’d actually talked about our issues in years. I don’t ever want us to go back to where we’ve been.”

 

Rio raises one hand to her cheek and nuzzles it. “Done. In a heartbeat.”

 

Agatha sighs. “And I need to talk to the rest of my household, open that line of communication with them too. As much shit as I give her, I’d really rather not have Jen hating me again. And I think it’s safe to say you feel the same way about Alice.”

 

A flash of pain crosses Rio’s face and she glances over at the bed. “Sí.”

 

“Okay.” Agatha considers for a moment, then bites down on her lip. “And one more thing.”

 

Rio turns back to face her, all hopeful, doe-eyed, and also completely exhausted. Agatha wonders if she shouldn’t have let her sleep before they’d had this conversation. 

 

Too late now, she supposes.

 

“Hm?”

Agatha tightens her grip on her hands, digging little crescents into her pale skin. “Teen has to come first. And I mean that, Rio. Whatever unresolved feelings having a kid around triggers in you- and I get it, I do. Believe me, I’ve had them all, and they suck . But you work through that shit in therapy, we clear?” 

 

“Claro,” Rio murmurs. Agatha runs her thumbs gently along the marks she’d made, examining them.

 

“I’m choosing to believe that that thinly veiled gift of yours was an apology, and a step forward from where we last left off. But you don’t get three strikes when it comes to him. And if I tell him about us and he expresses any discomfort-”

 

“I’m gone,” Rio finishes. She’s not frowning when Agatha looks at her face, quite the opposite. A soft smile has crept across her features, one that widens slightly when she reaches up to cup Agatha’s face in her hands. “I’d expect nothing less of you, querida. Any half-decent mother would do the same, and you are so, so much more than that.” 

 

A tear slips free from her eyes before she can stop it. Rio wipes it away with a tender touch and a knowing eye. “Agatha. You are a good mother, no matter what you let yourself believe. No matter how short our journey through motherhood was, I was lucky to do it with you.” 

 

She kisses two more tears from the corners of Agatha’s eyes. “You’re a good person. A good mother. And I love you. Te amo, mi vida. ”

 

Agatha grasps her wrists, closing her eyes. “Say it again.” 

 

“Te amo,” Rio whispers. She sways like a tree in the wind, and Agatha sways with her. “I love you.”

 

Agatha leans her forehead against hers, soaking her up like a sponge. Her touch, her voice, her smell. Rio, Rio, Rio.

 

“Again.”

 

“I love you.”

 

“Again.” 

 

“I love you. I love you.”

 

“Again.” 

 

“I lo-”

 

Agatha cuts her off with a kiss. Rio squeaks, then melts into her lips with a happy sigh. Agatha winds a hand through her hair and draws her closer, closer, presses their bodies together until it feels like their very beings are melding. A thousand lonely nights, a million unshared embraces, countless unspoken words all pass between them in an instant. 

 

And suddenly, for the first time in a long time, she feels entirely whole again.

 

Agatha breaks the kiss with a soft sigh, stroking Rio’s dark curls. She cocks her head when Rio attempts to chase her lips, holding her gently at bay. 

 

“I love you too.”

 

Rio’s face blossoms like a sunflower in July. She stares at Agatha like she’s the sun, soaking reverently in her warmth. Agatha chuckles, cradling her face in her hands and preparing to repeat herself when-

 

Someone nearby laughs. Softly, gingerly, but it’s there. 

 

Agatha jumps, nearly tripping over the chair she’d abandoned. Rio hisses like a spooked cat and grabs her arm, instinctively pushing Agatha behind her. Then, she gasps.

 

“¡ Ay, por dios! Alice !”

 

Alice? 

 

Alice. 

 

Agatha whips around, gaping in shock.  Sure enough, Alice Wu-Gulliver- friend, partner, family- is finally, finally awake. Awake and grinning back at her like the cat that caught the canary, Agatha notes- and damn it, she’s really never going to hear the end of this one. Right now, however, she can’t bring herself to care. 

 

Alice’s face is still pale, but her eyes- bright and full of life- are sparkling with mirth. She wriggles her fingers mischeviously at them, then fumbles for the oxygen mask over her mouth and nose. Still in a slight stupor, Agatha reaches out and helps her lift it a bit. 

 

“Here. I got you.”

 

Alice squeezes her wrist gently in thanks before she clears her throat to speak .Her voice is soft and raspy, but she sounds damn good for someone who’d been bleeding out on the station floor less than twenty-four hours ago. 

 

“Somebody…shoot me.”

 

Rio bursts out laughing and Agatha drops her head onto the bed with a squawk of frustration. 

 

“Oh my god, you suck! We spend all day and night worrying about you, crying over you, hovering at your bedside, and that’s the first thing you say? Your first words out of a coma, and you use them to give us shit?!”

 

Alice giggles, shaking the hand she still holds about playfully.  “Don’t get me wrong, I’m happy for you two. But I’m getting really, really sick of waking up like this.” She winks in Rio’s direction. “No offense, but I was already sick of it by the first night we met.”

 

Agatha snorts, shaking her head in mock disbelief. Alice laughs, then leans over and pulls her into a hug. Agatha rolls her eyes, then hugs her in return, holds her both as tightly as she dares and as gingerly as she can manage and relishes the feeling of her squeezing back. 

 

Sometimes, people just die. But not today. Today, they live.

Chapter 21

Chapter Notes

Still wrapping up replying to comments, but I wanted to get this posted before I leave for work, or I'm going to be crying all day. For reasons that will soon become clear, I'm posting the last two chapters at once! Next chapter will be epilogue and announcement.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Lilia must have been the one driving the others back to the hospital, because they make it there disturbingly quickly. Alice- who’s stubbornly managed to prop herself upright on her pillows, despite Agatha’s objections and with Rio’s help- starts to cry as soon as she sees them, and Agatha has to snag Teen by the hoodie to stop him from throwing himself into her arms. He and Lilia each get in a quick, weepy hug before a nurse comes in to scold the whole bunch of them.

 

“Easy, easy! The last thing we need is for too tight of a squeeze to split open our girl’s stitches.” 

 

Jen- who’d been next up to hug Alice- smiles sweetly to the man’s face. Then, once he’s gone, she climbs onto her lap, takes her face in her hands, and promptly covers it in kisses. 

 

“Didn’t tell me I can’t do that, did he?

 

Agatha makes a gagging sound (mostly to hide her grin, though she’s fairly sure Lilia sees). Alice laughs, though it looks like it hurts, and takes Jen’s hands in hers.

 

“It’s good to see you too, J.” Her eyes start to well up again, though she manages to keep them from spilling over this time. “It’s good to see all you guys. Really, really good.”

 

That gets poor Teen sniffling all over again, and Jennifer looks dangerously close to weeping herself. Lilia- ever devoted to her role as pseudo-matriarch- drags Agatha over to the end of the bed then, and, in an impressive display, manages to fold all four of her chicks into her arms at once. Then they’re all crying, even (though she loathes to admit it) Agatha, and damn if it doesn’t feel good. Good, because they’re all there, good, because they’re all okay, good because they’re gathered around a hospital bed and not an open grave. 

 

No one notices Rio slipping away. Technically, she supposes, no one probably knew she was there in the first place. She’d embraced Alice herself long before they arrived, had kissed her once on each cheek (not dissimilar to the one Alice had given her a few days before her birthday, just after reminding her that she could be there if she needed a friend- and oh, how she had) and whispered thanks into her ear like a woman reciting a prayer, then held her hand while Agatha stepped out to call their family. But she’s careful to be out of sight by the time they arrive, and she’s walking down the hallway with a small, bittersweet smile as Jennifer Kale swings up into the hospital bed. Agatha blows her away with a wave and a kiss, and that’s really all she needs. 

 

Someone catches her arm just as she rounds the corner. Expecting a doctor or perhaps a fellow cop, Rio allows herself to be turned- but instead, to her surprise, finds herself looking into the piercing eyes of Lilia Calderu. 

 

Lilia looks her over for a long, long moment. Rio stands perfectly still, letting herself be examined. She knows, without speaking a word, that she’s being assessed- judged, perhaps, is a better term-, and she’s suddenly afraid to do anything to sour it. 

 

After a beat, Lilia reaches up and cups Rio’s face in one hand. Her thumb strokes along the  hollows under her eyes and across the tacky remnants of tears that still cling to her cheeks, slowly exploring the evidence of the long, nervewracking night she’s spent here. Rio inhales- both surprised and unexpectedly overwhelmed- and Lilia’s face softens. 

 

“Oh, darling. Come here.”

 

She opens her arms, as warm and welcoming as a blazing hearth on a snowy day. And Rio Vidal, who hasn’t been anyone’s darling in a very, very long time, falls into those outstretched      arms and lets herself begin to melt. 

 

~

 

The doctors release Alice only a short few days later- faster than anyone had expected, but not at all unwelcome. Still, as she makes her way back into the house with Jen’s arm firmly hooked through hers, Agatha can’t stop herself from wondering at least a few times if it wasn’t a mistake. It’s an unmistakeably nostalgic sort of anxiety- an odd way to describe it, she knows, but she’s sure that’s what it is.

 

 When the reason why finally comes to her, she can’t help a laugh. Jen glances at her over the pile of takeout wrappers she’s gathering, raising an eyebrow.

 

“Something funny?

 

“ Taking Nicky home from the hospital! ” Agatha chortles, laughing harder when her friend cocks her head. She’s well aware she’s probably bordering on hysterical and doesn’t care. “ That’s what this whole day has been reminding me of. All the anxiety, all the uncertainty, the whole feeling of are sure you want to trust us with this, doc, because you’re sort of handing us an actual human life here and that seems like kind of a bad idea, yeah? ”

 

Jen bites her lips, then raises her hand to her mouth to hide a burgeoning grin. “You aren’t seriously comparing a fully grown, fully capable police officer to a newborn baby, are you?” 

 

Agatha cackles, slapping the counter in front of her.  “Why the hell not, huh? S’what we all have in common. Guess some people would say the other end, but I’m not worried about splitting hairs.” 

 

Jennifer crosses her arms, shaking her head wryly. “You are making literally no sense.” 

 

Agatha slings an arm around her waist, still chuckling. “Death and taxes and all that shit. We’re all born, we all die, and all we can do in the middle is hang on tight and enjoy the ride.”

 

“Did you hit your head while I wasn’t looking? Because I have no clue what you’re talking about.”

 

Agatha ignores her. “C’est la vie, Jenny Kale! Life’ll will screw you over in an instant. May as well enjoy the good times while the times are good.”

 

“Wow, that clarified absolutely nothing ,” Jen snarks. Still, she drapes her own arms around Agatha and leans into her, dropping her head against the top of her friend’s with a tired sigh.

 

“Agatha, is this your weird-ass way of telling me you’re back together with your ex-wife?”

 

“We may be…together-ish.” She peeks at Jen out of the corner of her eyes. “I’m sure that makes you absolutely furious.”

 

Jen rolls her eyes. “I mean, always. But collectively, we seem to have moved on, so…” 

 

She sighs again. “Just don’t be a moron, okay?”

 

It’s not exactly approval, per se- but Agatha had, of course, never expected approval. Jennifer Kale- both a phenomenal pain in her ass and probably closest thing in the world she’ll ever have to a best friend- would never be caught dead giving the pair her blessing. But she’s hugging Agatha instead of hitting her, and that’s good enough for now. 

 

(And when Alice returns to the hospital a few weeks later- only for a minor procedure, thank god, she’s in and out before the day’s end- Jen allows Rio to join her at her bedside, and they wait together for her to wake up.)

 

~

 

It’s honestly sort of funny how completely unnatural their daily routine feels as it starts to slowly creep back into their lives. Though Lilia and Jen- blessed with the luxury of being their own bosses- can (and do, much to Alice’s chagrin) take time off to spend at home and fuss, Agatha has no choice but to return to her classroom after the first week. Teen- though he puts up an impressive protest- is back in school after only two days or so. Collectively they wake up, eat breakfast, and leave to their lives as though nothing had happened, and Alice will certainly follow them to work instead of curling up on the couch and trying not to aggravate her wound. A very everything is fine philosophy, as Teen puts it. Whatever gets ya through the day, right?

 

Which, of course, is all very well and good- except for the fact that it’s normal, and normal feels very, very wrong. Surgeons had barely finished picking shrapnel out of their friend’s lungs and they’re right back to worrying about exams? Bullshit. 

 

 Her therapist tells her that’s a totally reasonable response to what they’ve been through, that their collective minds are still tensed for an emergency and will probably be for some time. “But it will fade , Agatha, it will,” she reminds her gently.  “And every day will get a little easier. For all of you.”

 

(Agatha leaves that day with a card for a youth psychiatrist and a pamphlet titled “How To Talk To Your Child About Trauma, something that makes her shrink nod with pride.). 

 

Eventually, however, they really do start to go back to normal- or at least, whatever they count as normal. Though at first, it’s nerve-wracking to leave (the nagging fear of a disaster nipping at their heels like some psychic stray dog), each time they come back to a whole household makes it easier. 

 

Collectively they wake up, eat breakfast, leave to their lives. And collectively, they return home to the same table, where they sate their souls with the knowledge that they all made it through the day. And what more, in the end, can they ask for?

 

It certainly helps that Alice- a fighter if there ever was one- recovers remarkably quickly. Though Jennifer spends at least the first week or so doting on her with little resistance, tender hours spent in each other’s company- Alice curled against Jen’s side to ground herself against the grogginess her painkillers cause, Jen stroking her hair on the pretense of helping her comb it and pretending she’s not similarly greedy for her touch- quickly give way to exasperated bickering and constant hounding to quit pushing yourself so hard, for fuck’s sake, and sit down before you burst your stitches! 

 

Alice melts to her badgering, of course (and with good reason, after all- Jen’s sharp tongue belied an even sharper mind, and she didn’t have a medical degree for nothing, no matter what old men with wandering hands and cruel tempers say). Alice always folds for Jen. 

 

Sometimes Agatha is surprised by how well Rio- an exercise in chaos personified and packed into one little human being- gets along with the kindest, most patient member of their household. Then she remembers seeing Alice and Jen on their back porch together one night- shoulder to shoulder, hand in hand- and understands perfectly. 

 

Because the stars are brilliant that night (shimmering like diamonds woven through the deep blue velvet of the moon’s midnight gown, outlining her curves and complimenting her clever, all-seeing eyes) and the auroras cut ribbons of color to weave across the sky, and Jen watches them dance with a smile that her camera will never see. But Alice looks at her, always looks at her, only looks at her- just as Rio looks at Agatha, always looks at Agatha, only looks at Agatha . They look at the world through the same eyes, those two, and those eyes have adapted to see the greatest darkness that humanity has to offer- but eyes that are trained to see in the dark are also capable of taking in the most light. 

 

Alice looks at Jennifer in the same reverent, awestruck way she gazes upon the moon. Rio basks in Agatha like a plant seeks the sun. 

 

~

 

Rio, much to Agatha’s disappointment, doesn’t get the few months she’d estimated. Between the constant backlog in the courts and the department’s insistence on Alice taking plenty of time to rest and recover, her boss had made the annoying call that she can go back when the time is right- but for now, the wait time would be better spent at her home base. Alice rather wisely comments that there’s probably at least a little pettiness involved- after all, she had submitted a transfer request as soon as she’d returned from the hospital. According to some snooping Alice’s squad had done, Rio is somewhat of a highly prized asset- (“Her close rate is, like…twelve percent over the national average,” Alice had told her one day. “She’s probably boosting their department’s stats just by being there.”), and one that her commander certainly isn’t eager to lose.  

 

Agatha supposes she can understand that, but still. You’ve had her for a decade, pal. Time to move over.

 

Still, there seems to be at least some benefit to the time spent apart. Absence makes the heart grow fonder and all that jazz. And at the very least, the distance forces them to continue their work in counseling- without jumping each other’s bones, which their therapist- who thankfully offers virtual sessions- reminds them often adds to the complications in their relationship (Agatha disagrees, though she knows perfectly well he’s right). Though the wait to see her in person is painful, the love that still connects them- the one that had dragged her across the Ferris Wheel to kiss Rio for the first time, that had bolstered them to run away in a stolen van and start a life of their own, the one that had created their baby boy and the one that hadn’t died with him after all- only seems to grow stronger. Perhaps spending the time building their emotional connection (and, of course, in both dual and individual therapy- which, much to Agatha’s chagrin, really does seem to be helping) is better for them than she’d anticipated.

 

Though her libido disagrees.

 

In any case, Rio leaves, and the months pass slowly. In one letter (which she insists on sending, despite daily texts and weekly video calls), she writes that she doesn’t expect to be back before May- and that’s at the earliest. The wheels of beauracracy turn slowly, far too slowly for Agatha’s liking. She’s denied herself what she wants for far too long to have any patience for something so trivial as the federal government doing the same. 

 

On Valentine’s Day, at least a few of her colleagues cancel class- particularly the afternoon and evening classes. Despite cajoling from her students, however, Agatha still stubbornly intends to hold court at the head of her lecture hall. “You have three-hundred and sixty-five days of the year to be horny, kids!”, she chirps, smacking her palm down on the desk of a pair who she probably should have split up. “You’ll survive…one…”

 

She stops. 

 

Taped the whiteboard behind the desk, dressed in a green velvet ribbon and an attached gold tag, is a single long-stemmed black rose. 

 

The rest of the bouquet- a mix of the same black roses, baby’s breath, and a smattering of sunset-colored chrysanthemums- is grasped in Rio’s right hand when Agatha bursts out through the door to her classroom. Her left holds a jewelry box close to her hip, which she holds out to her lover with a glowing smile. 

 

“Surprise, my lady.”

 

“I didn’t- I- you- what ?!”, Agatha splutters. Rio laughs, taking a step closer and pushing the flowers gently into her hands. 

 

“I had some vacation time squirrelled away.” She gives Agatha an unabashedly long once-over, trailing the fingers of her now free hand over the faux crystals sewn into the black fabric of her dress. “You look incredible. ”

“I- well yes, thank you, I know that,” Agatha mumbles, trying to hide the flush rising to her cheeks. “It’s new.” 

 

Rio hums, fingering the jeweled flower buttons at the cuffs of her sleeves. “It’s beautiful. Makes you look like a storm.”

 

Agatha raises an eyebrow, slowly shaking off some of her shock. “A storm?”

 

“A force of nature,” Rio murmurs. “The rain-” She touches a crystal teardrop. “The lightening-” A nail runs along one silvery thread. “And you, of course.”

 

Agatha snorts, shaking her head. “Take a picture, it’ll last longer.” She runs her own hands along the fabric of her new dress- a Christmas gift from Lilia that had arrived rather late, and therefore hadn’t yet made it out of her closet. “And to think I was just going to wear jeans and a sweater today. Guess you’d better thank Alice and Jen for convincing-” 

 

She stops midsentence, the realization hitting her like truck. “Oh, those bitches. Is that what Jen was all smug about this morning?”

 

Rio smirks. “Well, I had to make sure it matched with what I got you.”

 

She holds out the box again. Agatha inhales softly, passing the flowers back to take it.

 

 “I- you know I didn’t get you anything, right? I mean, I did, but it’s not here. ”

 

Rio laughs. “I know.”

 

“And it’s definitely not for opening in public.”

 

“Just open your present, mi vida.”

Agatha rolls her eyes and lifts the lid. 

 

Nestled inside, on a cushion of plush velvet, is an absolutely beautiful necklace. It’s made of multiple strings of creamy white pearls, layered on top of each other to create a choker-like effect. A few, however, are stained a deep red, with nearly invisible strings dangling crimson gemstones down like droplets of blood. 

 

“It’s called a vampire’s kiss,” Rio adds, cupping her hands around her lover’s. “You like it?”

 

And oh , if that isn’t so, so very Rio.

 

Petals flutter into the air as Agatha crashes into her for a kiss.



February 14th, 2025.

 

3:14 PM. 

 

Coven Of Chaos

 

Big Mama Witch: Might be home late tonight. Just FYI.

 

The Worst Type Of Vegetable: “Late”, huh? 🤨

 

Big Mama Witch: I apparently have a date. Which I would have LOVED to know ahead of time, you assholes.

 

Freeloader: omgggggg

 

Spooky Kooky B: Whoever could have predicted that?

 

Big Mama Witch: Bitch.

 

Mommy Issues ™: Have fun!

 

The Worst Type Of Vegetable: Not too much fun.

 

Big Mama Witch: Right back at you, Jenny (and Alice).

 

The Worst Type Of Vegetable: 🖕

 

Big Mama Witch: 🖕🖕



~

 

The next few months continue to crawl by. Alice eventually goes back to work (though she spends plenty of time at her desk and far away from armed criminals), Jen takes back over her store, and Agatha- though she gripes consistently about it and likely will until the day she dies- continues to go to therapy. 

 

In that, she certainly isn’t alone. Between the bunch, they rack up impressive therapy bills- both individualized and, eventually, group. As it turns out, Agatha and Rio’s couples therapist offers a sort of family-style counselling- which Agatha complains sounds like a buffet, but actually turns out to be fairly effective, maybe even necessary . They certainly have enough to talk about.

 

Agatha is, in all honesty, surprised by how well things continue to go. Where tragedy and trauma had torn apart her life before, their little family now seems to be closer than ever. While they aren’t out of the woods by any stretch, that newfound bond gets them through the worst of the aftershocks- ones they might not have survived on their own. In March, when Jen’s assaulter (who’d thankfully, for both Jen and Lilia’s sakes, pled guilty) is brought to a sentencing hearing, Agatha sits up with her until the early hours of the morning while she writes and rewrites her statement. The next day, when it’s all over, Lilia takes her in her arms and holds her until she cries herself out. Teen sometimes accompanies Alice to physical therapy (as much to reassure himself as to cheer her on), and she works him through the anxiety that comes with his SATs. Not as serious as getting shot, really, but try telling that to a high school counselor.

 

In the middle of May, just after school sessions end, Billy makes a suggestion that Agatha deems completely insane. So naturally, of course, they go through with it. Alice borrows a van from a friend at the impound lot (which no one pries into the origins of), Jen delegates a few of her employees to watch over both hers and Lilia’s stores, and Teen packs a travel cage for the bunny. Then, for the first time since they’ve all known each other, they set off on a road trip. 

 

Agatha, ever the cynic, calls the whole thing incredibly morbid. Still, when they make their final stop, she’s privately very, very grateful to not be alone. And though none of them say it, she knows that each of them (first Lilia, whose destination is farthest away, then Alice, and then Billy) feel the same. 

 

She stands a step back, watching Jen and Lilia bicker over their impromptu flower arrangement with a wry, amused smile. Alice catches her gaze and rolls her eyes at the pair- earning herself a snort and an indignant splutter- before resuming her gentle, methodical polishing of the headstone. Out of all of them, she’s the only one who’d even thought to bring cleaning supplies. Agatha makes a mental note to thank her for that later.

 

Teen- jeans stained slightly with mud from gathering said flowers- shuffles closer, and Agatha puts an arm around him. It’s a bit of a reach- he’s taller than her now and still growing at an alarming rate- but she manages. Her godson butts his forehead against hers and she gives him a little squeeze.

 

“I’m glad I got to come meet him.”

 

“Me too, kid.” 

 

Lilia and Jen- having apparently reached an agreement- straighten up and step back to join them. Alice stands as well, and Jen takes her hand. With them out of the way, the newly polished inscription shimmers in the glinting sunlight. 

 

With every bend beyond the end, your mothers love you so. 

 

Nicholas Scratch, October 16th, 2001- October 12th, 2007. 

 

Tears are flowing down her cheeks before she realizes it. For the first time in a long time, however, her heart doesn’t sting with bitterness. She’ll call Rio later and they’ll reminisce together, and they’ll cry together, and she’ll tell her she loves her before she hangs up. In the meantime, however, she has her friends on one side and her godson on the other. And she could certainly do worse, couldn’t she?

 

She hugs Teen a little tighter and he leans his head against hers. 

 

“Happy Mother’s Day, Agatha.”

 

Chapter End Notes

So because I'm going to make an announcement next chapter, I'm going to add my "last" author note here. And holy shit, guys, this is...not gonna lie, this is WAY more emotional for me than it should be. On so many levels. But here goes.

Thank you all. From the very bottom of my cold, black heart. I have never ever even come CLOSE to finishing a project like this in my life, much less finished it with such a wonderful, kind, loyal audience. This is every dream I've ever had as an author come true. I've grown so much throughout, both as a writer and as an AO3 author, and I owe you so, so many more thanks than I'll ever be able to give.

I also have another reason to thank you, which I've mentioned in a few comments, but have mostly kept to myself. Right around when chapters three and four came out (ish? I think?), I suffered two of the worst losses in my life. For privacy's sake, I won't go into details, but...needless to say, it sucked. Writing this story was probably the best catharsis I could have asked for, but I didn't expect anything to come of it. Have hopes, but not expectations, right?

But you guys loved it, and you loved me, and I needed that so, SO bad. I used words to keep myself afloat, but y'all took that and built a lifeboat. You kept me going, gave me an outlet, and got me through the worst time of my life. Fanfiction may seem like a silly hobby to some, but I have NEVER felt more confident, proud, or loved than when I was reading your comments.

So thank you, my friends, for loving me when I needed it most. Thank you for loving my characters, thank you for loving my story, and thank you for loving me. And above all else, one last time...thank you so, so much for reading. <3

(Oh man, I'm totally gonna go to work in tears, aren't I?)

Chapter 22: Epilogue

Chapter Notes

Yes, I had to go retcon Billy's birthday. What about it?

See the end of the chapter for more notes

May 25th, 2025. 

 

“Agatha, the pizza rolls are burning!”

 

Agatha rolls her eyes, hefting the cooler higher in her arms. “Then grab an oven mitt, Jen.”

 

“Agatha, does this look okay?” Teen calls to her from where he’s adjusting the banner he’s hanging above the fireplace. Agatha rolls her eyes again, not bothering to look.

 

“It’s your party, Teen. Do what you want.”

 

“That’s not helpful!” The boy complains. His godmother ignores him, shoving the back door open with the side of her foot. 

 

“Kid, if you want a straight answer, ask a straight lady.”

 

Billy’s boyfriend pops up from the backyard as she exits, nearly smacking his head on the porch. 

 

“Oh good- Professor Harkness?”

 

Agatha drops the drinks with a grunt and a curse, nearly catching her own toes.“Yeah?”

 

“Oh sorry, Professor, I know you’ve got your hands full, but-”

 

“Just Agatha. Only people who pay me get to call me Professor.”

 

“Agatha, the smoke alarm’s going to go off!”

 

“Then get them out of the damn oven, Jen!”

 

“Right. Agatha. Agatha, is the rabbit allowed in the backyard?”

 

“Is he eating bugs?”

 

“Uh-”

 

“Agatha!”

 

“You’re a grown woman, Jen! Get the damn pizza bagels out of the oven yourself!”

 

“Uh, yeah, I think he’s chasing bugs.”

 

“But did he catch any?”

 

“ Agatha!”

 

“No, but-”

 

“Then he’s fine.”

 

“ AGATHA!”

 

“ What?! ” Agatha barks, slamming the lid on the cooler closed. “For Christ’s sake, Jen, have you never made party snacks before? Do you need me to come hold your hand?”

Jennifer Kale appears in the doorway with a sour look on her face and a baking sheet in hand. “Someone’s ringing the doorbell and my hands are obviously full.” She jerks her head at the offending bagels. “Since I handled this , I’d appreciate if you could handle that. ”

 

“No bickering at the birthday party!” Lilia scolds from somewhere deeper in the house. After some begging from the birthday boy, she’d agreed to set up a classic fortune teller’s stand for his friends to try- the drawback being that she’d been conveniently absent all day while the rest of them got the house ready, claiming she was “preparing herself”.

 

More like she just wanted to get out of hanging streamers. 

 

“The party hasn’t started yet!” Jen snarks back. “Which is why I don’t understand why someone’s at the damn door!”

Alice- who’d been doing something out in the yard that involved mixing bowls of colorful paste- sets down what she’s holding and jogs up to the house. She brushes Jen’s shoulder on her way in, and Jen softens almost immediately. 

 

“Here, I’ll get it. And I’ll come help you in the kitchen in a minute, okay?”

 

Jen does a bit of noncommittal grumbling, but smiles warmly when as soon as Alice is out of sight.  Agatha makes a little oohing noise, to which she makes a face. “Oh, shut up.”

 

“I didn’t actually say anything!” Agatha chirps. Jen sighs, turning gracefully on her heel. 

 

“Ugh, I can’t handle you today.”

 

Agatha’s mounting a retort when Alice’s surprised laughter cuts her short. She blinks in surprise, glancing in the direction of her friend’s voice. 

 

“Oh my god, what are you doing here?! I thought you had another month before your transfer!”

 

Someone laughs and responds in Spanish, and Agatha’s heart skips a beat. 

 

Rio- wearing a black sweater knit with a skull pattern and carrying a neatly wrapped box-  just pulling away from Alice when she makes it into the hallway. She glances over Alice’s shoulder and her face lights up. 

 

“Hello, my love.” 

 

Agatha nudges Alice aside (none too gently either, though more so than she would have been with Lilia or Jen)  to take Rio into her own arms. Rio chirps happily and gives her a little squeeze. 

 

“Mmm, I missed you.” She pulls away slightly, showing Agatha the box she holds. “I brought your boy a butterfly in a case. Teenage boys like that kind of thing, right?”

 

Agatha snorts. Behind her, Jen scoffs and slams an oven door. “Who invited her?”

 

“Good to see you too, Jenny!” Rio calls, apparently unperturbed. Agatha pulls away, brow wrinkling in confusion. 

 

“Wait, wait, wait. Not that I’m not happy to see you, but she’s right. How did you know about…this?” 

 

She gestured vaguely around the partially decorated room. Before Rio can answer, however, footsteps approach from behind them both. Billy Maximoff coughs softly to get his godmother’s attention, looking like a puppy who’d just chewed up a particularly expensive pair of shoes. 

 

“I…well, I invited her.”

 

Agatha looks between them, startled. Rio nods in affirmation, tightening her grip slightly on her lover. “He did. Though how he got my phone number, I’m actually not sure.” 

 

“Stole it from Alice,” Billy mutters sheepishly. “Sorry.”

 

Alice groans. “Dude, you’ve gotta quit breaking into my phone. That’s, like…it might be illegal, actually.”

 

“I said I was sorry,” Billy whines. “Besides, it’s my birthday, you can’t arrest me.”

 

“That’s not what I- oh, whatever.” Alice rolls her eyes good-naturedly, ruffling his hair when he gets within reach. “I’m gonna head into the kitchen before Jen boils over. Good to see you, Rio. Remind me to grab you later, I wanna pick your brain about a case that’s been bugging me.” 

 

She shoulders past Agatha, mouthing something that looks suspiciously like good luck . Agatha sticks her tongue out at her, trying to mask her own sudden nerves. Why the hell is she nervous? Surely not because two of the most important people in her life are meeting for the first time, absolutely not because this is DEFINITELY a make or break kind of moment. Must be something else. 

 

She turns back to Billy, folding her arms and trying for a stern look. “So why exactly did you decide to start your little schemes again, huh?”

 

“I…I guess I kinda wanted to meet you,” Teen answers, though he’s looking over her shoulder at Rio. He suddenly looks almost shy, as does- much to Agatha’s surprise- Rio. “You’ve, like, sort of been a part of my life, but…”

“But we’ve never seen spoken in person,” Rio finishes, nodding casually. She glances over at Agatha, however, and she can see the nervousness reflecting from her own face. “Seems perfectly reasonable to me.”

 

Billy nods in relief, then sticks his hand out. It’s a stiff gesture, like a little boy who’s just learned how to shake hands. Awkward as all get out, but endearing as hell. That’s her kid. 

 

 “Okay, then, uh…hi. I’m Billy. Maximoff. Billy Maximoff.”

 

She’s forgetting something. What is she forgetting?

 

Rio cocks her head, offering him her own hand. “Rio Vidal. Nice to finally meet you.”

 

Oh right. Breathing. That’s important, isn’t it?

 

Teen takes the proffered hand, and Rio glances down at the boy’s wrist as they shake. A small smile tugs at her lips and she jerks her chin towards it. “That’s your father’s watch, isn’t it?”

 

Billy blinks. “Yeah, it- wait, how did you…?” 

 

Agatha inhales sharply. Her godson glances over at her, slightly confused. Then the pieces suddenly click , and he whirls back to Rio.

 

“ Wait. You…?”

 

Rio shrugs. “I may have.”

 

A grin starts to spread across the boy’s face. He lets go of her hand, then- before Rio or Agatha can say anything else- suddenly throws his arms around her. Rio lets out a genuine squeak of surprise- though her arms instinctively come up to hug him back- and Agatha presses a hand to her mouth to prevent from laughing. 

 

“ Thank you, ” Teen whispers. Rio hums something nonsensical, but her grip tightens on his shoulders, and her eyes are shimmering. 

 

Teen hugs her even tighter and whispers something in her ear. Her eyes flit to Agatha almost immediately, and she nods. 

 

“Of course I will. Of course I will.”

 

“Good.” The boy lets go and steps away, tilting his head at Agatha. She raises an eyebrow, but shuffles into his place and takes Rio’s hand- and oh, oh gods. Is she wearing her wedding band again?

 

Teen’s boyfriend calls out from somewhere in the yard. The kid’s head whips towards him, then back to the women in the doorway. Rio smiles, and Agatha makes a little shooing gesture with her free hand. 

 

“Go, go!”

Billy laughs, then runs off to the back door. Before he can make it out, however, he turns and glances back. 

 

“It’s good to meet you too, Rio. Welcome home.”

 

Chapter End Notes

So...I know I promised this would be a deleted scene. I lied. That would be because I had a few deleted scenes, and I couldn't choose, and I kept writing more, so....

I'm going to be creating a spinoff! Probably coming out later this week. It'll be side stories, flashbacks, and other tales that didn't make it into this story.

As a bonus, I'll also be opening up my asks on Tumblr (where you can find me under the same name) and if you have stories you want to see, shout them at me there! I can't guarantee I will take or respond to every one, but I will credit the ones I do take (provided that's what you want). I'll also be starting another new work sometime later this week (which is a TOTALLY different vibe, but I like it!) ! If you need the link to my Tumblr, let me know, and I hope to continue seeing you guys in the next work :)

(PS: Sorry to anyone who thinks this is an unsatisfying ending. I know a few people have some…strong feelings towards Rio and their breakup argument (honestly, way stronger than I expected). I did my best to redeem her properly (reminder that she sent an apology gift that was incredibly meaningful and that months of therapy went down offscreen), but at this point? I wrote a whole novel in my spare time for you to read for free, sorry if it wasn’t up to your standards 😘. Unkind comments will therefore not get responses and will be deleted. Thank you to all my lovely people again, and sorry to put such a sour note here!)
